    fn diff_keeps_zero_quantity_so_levels_can_be_removed() {
        // A zero qty removes a level; dropping it would leave a phantom level.
        let text = r#"{"e":"depthUpdate","E":1,"U":1,"u":2,"b":[["100.0","0"]],"a":[]}"#;
        match parse_diff(text).expect("parsed") {
            MarketEvent::Diff(f) => {
                assert_eq!(f.updates.len(), 1);
                assert_eq!(f.updates[0].quantity, 0.0);
            }
            other => panic!("expected a diff, got {other:?}"),
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn malformed_levels_are_skipped_not_fatal() {
        let text = r#"{"e":"depthUpdate","E":1,"U":1,"u":2,"b":[["bad","1"],["2.0"],"3",["4.0","5.0"]],"a":[]}"#;
        match parse_diff(text).expect("parsed") {
            MarketEvent::Diff(f) => assert_eq!(f.updates.len(), 1),
            other => panic!("expected a diff, got {other:?}"),
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_a_frame_missing_its_final_update_id() {
        // Without `u` the sequence cannot advance, so the frame is refused
        // rather than silently resetting continuity.
        let text = r#"{"e":"depthUpdate","E":1,"U":1,"b":[],"a":[]}"#;
        assert!(parse_diff(text).is_none());
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod end_to_end {
    use super::*;
    use crate::core::feature::{
        CanonicalIntegers, CanonicalTop, CanonicalValues, row_from_canonical,
    };
    use event_merger_live::schema;

    /// Drive real wire frames through the whole pipeline, offline.
    ///
    /// This is the test that proves the claim the recorder makes: what the
    /// capture writes is the merged table, column for column. It uses the
    /// actual `parse_*` functions and the actual `Merger`/`CsvRecorder`, so a
    /// schema change anywhere in the chain fails here.
    fn run_offline(path: &std::path::Path) -> Vec<Vec<String>> {
        let mut capture = Capture {
            merger: Merger::new(DEFAULT_LEVELS),
            reorder: ReorderBuffer::new(1_000, 1024),
            csv: CsvRecorder::create(path).expect("create"),
            rows_written: 0,
            reseeds_pending: false,
            fanout: None,
            fanout_failed: Arc::new(AtomicBool::new(false)),
        };

        // A REST seed, applied DIRECTLY to the merger. It must not go
        // through the reorder buffer: a snapshot has no exchange event time
        // (`BookSnapshot::event_time_ms` reports the update id), so it would
        // set the watermark far into the future and make every real event
        // look late, dropping all of them.
        capture.merger.apply_seed(&BookSnapshot {
            last_update_id: 7_911_048_639_390,
            bids: vec![(108_663.2, 0.095), (108_662.9, 0.553), (108_653.8, 0.025)],
            asks: vec![(108_666.0, 0.004), (108_668.8, 0.002), (108_668.9, 0.562)],
        });

        for text in [
            r#"{"e":"depthUpdate","E":1751245200083,"U":1,"u":2,"b":[["108663.2","9.141"]],"a":[["108665.6","12.556"]]}"#,
            r#"{"e":"aggTrade","E":1751245200354,"a":2773899591,"p":"108665.6","q":"0.002","f":6438488841,"l":6438488841,"T":1751245200354,"m":true}"#,
            r#"{"e":"depthUpdate","E":1751245200406,"U":3,"u":4,"b":[["108653.8","0.025"]],"a":[["108666.0","0.004"]]}"#,
            r#"{"e":"aggTrade","E":1751245200441,"a":2773899592,"p":"108665.5","q":"0.303","f":6438488842,"l":6438488842,"T":1751245200441,"m":false}"#,
        ] {
            let event = parse_diff(text)
                .or_else(|| parse_trade(text))
                .expect("parsed frame");
            capture.offer(event).expect("offer");
        }
        capture.flush_pending().expect("flush");
        capture.csv.flush().expect("csv flush");

        let text = std::fs::read_to_string(path).expect("read back");
        text.lines()
            .map(|line| line.split(',').map(str::to_string).collect())
            .collect()
    }

    fn temp(name: &str) -> std::path::PathBuf {
        let mut p = std::env::temp_dir();
        p.push(format!("uets_live_e2e_{name}.csv"));
        let _ = std::fs::remove_file(&p);
        p
    }

    #[test]
    fn written_header_matches_the_merger_schema_exactly() {
        let path = temp("header");
        let rows = run_offline(&path);
        let header = &rows[0];
        let schema = schema();
        assert_eq!(header.len(), 128, "128 columns");
        for (i, name) in schema.iter().enumerate() {
            assert_eq!(&header[i], name, "column {i} must match schema()");
        }
        let _ = std::fs::remove_file(&path);
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_data_row_has_128_fields_including_the_header_row() {
        let path = temp("width");
        let rows = run_offline(&path);
        assert!(rows.len() >= 3, "header plus several rows");
        for row in &rows {
            assert_eq!(row.len(), 128, "no row may shift a column");
        }
        let _ = std::fs::remove_file(&path);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_trade_row_carries_the_book_and_empty_lob_identity() {
        let path = temp("trade");
        let rows = run_offline(&path);
        let schema = schema().to_vec();
        let index = |name: &str| schema.iter().position(|c| *c == name).expect(name);
        let trade_row = rows
            .iter()
            .find(|r| r[index("event_type")] == "TRADE")
            .expect("a TRADE row exists");

        // The book rides along on a trade row, as in the historical table.
        assert_ne!(trade_row[index("book_state__best_bid_price")], "");
        assert_ne!(trade_row[index("book_state__best_ask_price")], "");
        // But a trade is not a book update, so LOB identity stays absent.
        assert_eq!(trade_row[index("lob__event_time")], "", "absent, not 0.0");
        assert_eq!(trade_row[index("lob__final_update_id")], "");
        // And the trade payload is present.
        assert_eq!(trade_row[index("agg__price")], "108665.6");
        assert_eq!(trade_row[index("agg__is_buyer_maker")], "True");
        let _ = std::fs::remove_file(&path);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_lob_row_carries_lob_identity_and_empty_trade_block() {
        let path = temp("lob");
        let rows = run_offline(&path);
        let schema = schema().to_vec();
        let index = |name: &str| schema.iter().position(|c| *c == name).expect(name);
        let lob_row = rows
            .iter()
            .find(|r| {
                r[index("event_type")] == "LOB" && !r[index("lob__final_update_id")].is_empty()
            })
            .expect("a LOB row with an update id exists");
        assert_ne!(lob_row[index("lob__event_time")], "");
        assert_eq!(lob_row[index("agg__price")], "", "no trade on a LOB row");
        let _ = std::fs::remove_file(&path);
    }

    #[test]
    fn depth_block_is_absent_never_zero() {
        let path = temp("depth");
        let rows = run_offline(&path);
        let schema = schema().to_vec();
        let first = schema
            .iter()
            .position(|c| *c == "depth__timestamp")
            .unwrap();
        let last = schema
            .iter()
            .position(|c| *c == "depth_state__notional_5")
            .unwrap();
        for row in &rows[1..] {
            for field in &row[first..=last] {
                assert_eq!(field, "", "depth columns must be absent, never 0.0");
            }
        }
        let _ = std::fs::remove_file(&path);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_canonical_row_feeds_the_feature_layer() {
        // The bridge the feature layer relies on: the same row, as named
        // columns, with absence preserved.
        let mut values = CanonicalValues::new();
        values.insert(
            crate::core::feature::CANON_BEST_BID_PRICE.to_string(),
            Some(108_663.2),
        );
        values.insert(
            crate::core::feature::CANON_BEST_ASK_PRICE.to_string(),
            Some(108_666.0),
        );
        values.insert(crate::core::feature::CANON_AGG_PRICE.to_string(), None);

        let integers = CanonicalIntegers {
            timestamp_ns: 1_751_245_200_083_000_000,
            event_sequence: 1,
            event_time_ms: Some(1_751_245_200_083),
            ..Default::default()
        };
        let row = row_from_canonical(&values, &integers);
        assert_eq!(
            row.get(crate::core::feature::CANON_BEST_BID_PRICE),
            Some(108_663.2)
        );
        // Absent stays absent, so a feature over it fails loudly.
        assert_eq!(row.get(crate::core::feature::CANON_AGG_PRICE), None);
        assert!(
            row.get_value(crate::core::feature::CANON_AGG_PRICE)
                .expect("present as Missing")
                .is_missing()
        );

        let top = CanonicalTop::from_values(&values);
        assert_eq!(top.mid(), Some((108_663.2 + 108_666.0) / 2.0));
        assert_eq!(top.spread(), Some(2.8000000000029104));
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod strategy_fanout_tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn strategy_payload_is_length_framed_and_contains_canonical_market_inputs() {
        let mut merger = Merger::new(20);
        merger.apply_seed(&BookSnapshot {
            last_update_id: 1,
            bids: vec![(99.0, 2.0)],
            asks: vec![(101.0, 3.0)],
        });
        let trade = Trade {
            event_time_ms: 1_700_000_000_000,
            transact_time_ms: 1_700_000_000_000,
            agg_trade_id: 10,
            first_trade_id: 10,
            last_trade_id: 10,
            price: 100.0,
            quantity: 0.25,
            is_buyer_maker: true,
        };
        merger.merge_event(MarketEvent::Trade(trade));

        let frame = strategy_frame(&merger, Some(trade), trade.event_time_ms).unwrap();
        let payload_length = u32::from_le_bytes(frame[..4].try_into().unwrap()) as usize;
        assert_eq!(payload_length, frame.len() - 4);
        let payload = std::str::from_utf8(&frame[4..]).unwrap();
        assert!(payload.contains("bid_price:99"));
        assert!(payload.contains("ask_price:101"));
        assert!(payload.contains("price:100"));
        assert!(payload.contains("amount:0.25"));
        assert!(payload.contains("is_buyer_maker:1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn strategy_payload_refuses_unseeded_or_stale_books() {
        let merger = Merger::new(20);
        assert!(strategy_frame(&merger, None, 1).is_err());
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/connectors/binance/websocket/mod.rs =====
//! Binance websocket reads: market streams and the user data stream.
//!
//! Three separate responsibilities, deliberately not merged:
//!
//! | file | role |
//! |------|------|
//! | [`live`] | the canonical capture pipeline: streams -> reorder -> merge -> CSV rows |
//! | [`diagnostic`] | one row per event, `update_type` = aggTrades/orderbook, the other stream `NaN` |
//! | [`monitor`] | the single-symbol live frame `display.py` renders |
//! | [`reader`] | standalone stream listeners, one per stream type |
//!
//! Nothing here translates into `crate::core` types: a websocket frame is market
//! *data*, and its consumers are the feature engine (`core::feature`) and the
//! recorders, not the order path. Keeping the streams out of the connector
//! traits is what lets `crate::exchange` stay synchronous and testable.

pub mod diagnostic;
pub mod live;
pub mod monitor;
pub mod reader;


===== FILE: uets/executor/connectors/binance/websocket/monitor.rs =====
//! Live market monitor for one symbol: aggTrades + partial book depth.
//!
//! Two stream threads feed a shared state; the main loop writes a JSON frame
//! every 100 ms to `out/live/<symbol>.json`, which `display.py` renders as a
//! self-updating orderbook + tape view:
//!
//! ```text
//! # terminal 1
//! cargo run --bin dev_test -- monitor btcusdt
//! # terminal 2
//! python display.py btcusdt
//! ```
//!
//! Depth note: this monitor uses the PARTIAL BOOK DEPTH stream
//! (`@depth20@100ms`), which pushes COMPLETE snapshots of the top 20 bid and
//! ask levels every 100 ms -- 20 of each, verified on the wire. The
//! diff-depth stream (`@depth@100ms`) used by the `depth`/`multi` commands
//! only carries the CHANGED levels, which is why it never printed 20 rows.
//!
//! All parsing is done here with serde_json instead of the `binance` crate's
//! event enum (same rationale as `all_trades_websocket`): we control every
//! field, including trade direction ("m"), event times, and the raw
//! lastUpdateId.

use serde_json::{Value, json};
use std::fs;
use std::io::Write;
use std::path::PathBuf;
use std::sync::{Arc, Mutex};
use std::time::{Duration, SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};
use tungstenite::{Message, connect};

/// Aggregated trade + book state for the monitored symbol.
#[derive(Default)]
struct State {
    // tape
    last_price: f64,
    prev_price: f64,
    first_price: f64, // session open (first trade observed)
    buy_vol: f64,
    sell_vol: f64,
    trade_count: u64,
    last_trade_ms: u64,
    recent: Vec<Value>, // last few trades, oldest first
    // partial book (top 20 each side, best-first)
    bids: Vec<(f64, f64)>,
    asks: Vec<(f64, f64)>,
    book_ms: u64, // local receive time of the last book frame
    book_update_id: u64,
}

fn now_ms() -> u64 {
    SystemTime::now()
        .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
        .unwrap_or_default()
        .as_millis() as u64
}

/// Handle one aggTrade push: `{"e":"aggTrade","E":ms,"p":"price","q":"qty",
/// "m":bool}` -- `m=true` means the buyer was the maker, i.e. the taker SOLD.
fn on_trade(state: &Arc<Mutex<State>>, text: &str) {
    let v = match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(text) {
        Ok(v) => v,
        Err(_) => return,
    };
    if v["e"].as_str() != Some("aggTrade") {
        return;
    }
    let price: f64 = v["p"].as_str().and_then(|s| s.parse().ok()).unwrap_or(0.0);
    let qty: f64 = v["q"].as_str().and_then(|s| s.parse().ok()).unwrap_or(0.0);
    let side = if v["m"].as_bool().unwrap_or(false) {
        "sell"
    } else {
        "buy"
    };
    let event_ms = v["E"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0);

    let mut st = state.lock().expect("state poisoned");
    st.prev_price = if st.trade_count > 0 {
        st.last_price
    } else {
        price
    };
    st.last_price = price;
    if st.first_price == 0.0 {
        st.first_price = price;
    }
    if side == "buy" {
        st.buy_vol += qty;
    } else {
        st.sell_vol += qty;
    }
    st.trade_count += 1;
    st.last_trade_ms = event_ms;
    st.recent
        .push(json!({"t": event_ms, "p": price, "q": qty, "side": side}));
    if st.recent.len() > 8 {
        st.recent.remove(0);
    }
}

/// Handle one partial-book snapshot: `{"lastUpdateId":N,"bids":[[p,q]..20],
/// "asks":[[p,q]..20]}`. bids are already price-desc, asks price-asc.
fn on_book(state: &Arc<Mutex<State>>, text: &str) {
    let v = match serde_json::from_str::<Value>(text) {
        Ok(v) => v,
        Err(_) => return,
    };
    let parse_side = |arr: Option<&Vec<Value>>| -> Vec<(f64, f64)> {
        arr.map(|levels| {
            levels
                .iter()
                .filter_map(|lv| {
                    let p: f64 = lv.get(0)?.as_str()?.parse().ok()?;
                    let q: f64 = lv.get(1)?.as_str()?.parse().ok()?;
                    Some((p, q))
                })
                .collect()
        })
        .unwrap_or_default()
    };
    let bids = parse_side(v["bids"].as_array());
    let asks = parse_side(v["asks"].as_array());
    if bids.is_empty() || asks.is_empty() {
        return;
    }

    let mut st = state.lock().expect("state poisoned");
    st.bids = bids;
    st.asks = asks;
    st.book_update_id = v["lastUpdateId"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0);
    st.book_ms = now_ms();
}

/// Fire-and-forget reconnecting stream thread.
fn spawn_stream(url: String, on_msg: impl FnMut(&str) + Send + 'static) {
    std::thread::spawn(move || {
        let mut on_msg = on_msg;
        loop {
            match connect(url.as_str()) {
                Ok((mut ws, _)) => loop {
                    match ws.read() {
                        Ok(Message::Text(text)) => on_msg(&text),
                        Ok(Message::Ping(_))
                        | Ok(Message::Pong(_))
                        | Ok(Message::Binary(_))
                        | Ok(Message::Frame(_)) => (),
                        Ok(Message::Close(frame)) => {
                            eprintln!("[{}] closed by server: {:?}", url, frame.map(|f| f.code));
                            break;
                        }
                        Err(e) => {
                            eprintln!("[{}] error: {}", url, e);
                            break;
                        }
                    }
                },
                Err(e) => eprintln!("[{}] connect failed: {}", url, e),
            }
            eprintln!("[{}] reconnecting in 3s...", url);
            std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_secs(3));
        }
    });
}

/// Run the monitor until interrupted. Writes one compact JSON frame every
/// 100 ms (temp-file + rename, so the display never sees a torn file).
pub fn monitor_websocket(symbol: &str) {
    let symbol = symbol.to_uppercase();
    let low = symbol.to_lowercase();
    let out_dir = PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"))
        .join("out")
        .join("live");
    let _ = fs::create_dir_all(&out_dir);
    let frame_path = out_dir.join(format!("{}.json", low));
    let tmp_path = frame_path.with_extension("json.tmp");

    let state = Arc::new(Mutex::new(State::default()));

    {
        let st = Arc::clone(&state);
        spawn_stream(
            format!("wss://stream.binance.com:9443/ws/{}@aggTrade", low),
            move |text| on_trade(&st, text),
        );
    }
    {
        let st = Arc::clone(&state);
        spawn_stream(
            format!("wss://stream.binance.com:9443/ws/{}@depth20@100ms", low),
            move |text| on_book(&st, text),
        );
    }

    println!("monitor: {}  (aggTrade + depth20@100ms)", symbol);
    println!("frames:  {}", frame_path.display());
    println!("render:  python display.py {}", low);
    println!("stop:    Ctrl+C  (one dot per second)");

    let mut last_beat = 0u64;
    loop {
        std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(100));

        let (trades_frame, bids, asks) = {
            let st = state.lock().expect("state poisoned");
            let trades = json!({
                "last_price": st.last_price,
                "prev_price": st.prev_price,
                "first_price": st.first_price,
                "buy_vol": st.buy_vol,
                "sell_vol": st.sell_vol,
                "trade_count": st.trade_count,
                "last_trade_ms": st.last_trade_ms,
                "recent": st.recent.clone(),
            });
            (trades, st.bids.clone(), st.asks.clone())
        };
        let (book_ms, book_update_id) = {
            let st = state.lock().expect("state poisoned");
            (st.book_ms, st.book_update_id)
        };
        let frame = json!({
            "ts": now_ms(),
            "symbol": symbol,
            "trades": trades_frame,
            "bids": bids,
            "asks": asks,
            "book_ms": book_ms,
            "book_update_id": book_update_id,
        });

        if fs::write(&tmp_path, frame.to_string()).is_ok() {
            let _ = fs::remove_file(&frame_path);
            let _ = fs::rename(&tmp_path, &frame_path);
        }

        let now = now_ms();
        if now - last_beat >= 1000 {
            last_beat = now;
            print!(".");
            let _ = std::io::stdout().flush();
        }
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/connectors/binance/websocket/reader.rs =====
//! Standalone Binance websocket readers.
//!
//! Every public function here is a self-contained stream listener. They are
//! NOT meant to be run directly -- use the dispatcher binary instead, which
//! routes to these functions via `executor.rs`:
//!
//! ```text
//! cargo run --bin dev_test -- help
//! ```
//!
use binance::api::*;
use binance::userstream::*;
use binance::websockets::*;

use std::sync::atomic::AtomicBool;
use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};

use tungstenite::Message;

fn now_us() -> u128 {
    SystemTime::now()
        .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
        .unwrap()
        .as_micros()
}

// Manages a "user data stream" over REST (not a websocket by itself).
// A user data stream gives you a `listen_key` which you then feed into
// a websocket connection (see user_stream_websocket) to get live account
// updates. Needs a real API key -- it's tied to your account, not public market data.
pub fn user_stream() {
    let api_key_user = Some("YOUR_API_KEY".into());
    let user_stream: UserStream = Binance::new(api_key_user, None);

    if let Ok(answer) = user_stream.start() {
        println!("Data Stream Started ...");
        let listen_key = answer.listen_key;

        match user_stream.keep_alive(&listen_key) {
            Ok(msg) => println!("Keepalive user data stream: {:?}", msg),
            Err(e) => println!("Error: {}", e),
        }

        match user_stream.close(&listen_key) {
            Ok(msg) => println!("Close user data stream: {:?}", msg),
            Err(e) => println!("Error: {}", e),
        }
    } else {
        println!("Not able to start an User Stream (Check your API_KEY)");
    }
}

// Opens a websocket of your account's live stats using the listen_key from
// user_stream(): balances and your own order fills.
pub fn user_stream_websocket() {
    let keep_running = AtomicBool::new(true);
    let api_key_user = Some("YOUR_KEY".into());
    let user_stream: UserStream = Binance::new(api_key_user, None);

    if let Ok(answer) = user_stream.start() {
        let listen_key = answer.listen_key;

        let mut web_socket: WebSockets<'_> = WebSockets::new(|event: WebsocketEvent| {
            match event {
                WebsocketEvent::AccountUpdate(account_update) => {
                    for balance in &account_update.data.balances {
                        println!(
                            "Asset: {}, wallet_balance: {}, cross_wallet_balance: {}, balance: {}",
                            balance.asset,
                            balance.wallet_balance,
                            balance.cross_wallet_balance,
                            balance.balance_change
                        );
                    }
                }
                WebsocketEvent::OrderTrade(trade) => {
                    println!(
                        "Symbol: {}, Side: {}, Price: {}, Execution Type: {}",
                        trade.symbol, trade.side, trade.price, trade.execution_type
                    );
                }
                _ => (),
            };

            Ok(())
        });

        web_socket.connect(&listen_key).unwrap();
        if let Err(e) = web_socket.event_loop(&keep_running) {
            println!("Error: {}", e);
        }
        user_stream.close(&listen_key).unwrap();
        web_socket.disconnect().unwrap();
        println!("Userstrem closed and disconnected");
    } else {
        println!("Not able to start an User Stream (Check your API_KEY)");
    }
}

// Public market data websocket. No API key required.
// - market_websocket("btcusdt", "aggTrade")     --> aggregated trades
// - market_websocket("btcusdt", "depth@100ms")  --> diff depth stream (incremental)
// - market_websocket("btcusdt", "depth20@100ms")--> fixed top-20 depth snapshot
pub fn market_websocket(ticker: &str, datatype: &str) {
    let keep_running = AtomicBool::new(true);
    let stream = format!("{}@{}", ticker.to_lowercase(), datatype);

    let mut web_socket: WebSockets<'_> = WebSockets::new(|event: WebsocketEvent| {
        match event {
            // NOTE: "@aggTrade" streams emit WebsocketEvent::AggrTrades in
            // binance 0.21 -- WebsocketEvent::Trade is only the raw "@trade"
            // stream. (Both structs share symbol/price/qty fields but are
            // distinct types, hence two arms.)
            WebsocketEvent::AggrTrades(trade) => {
                println!(
                    "Symbol: {}, price: {}, qty: {}",
                    trade.symbol, trade.price, trade.qty
                );
            }
            WebsocketEvent::Trade(trade) => {
                println!(
                    "Symbol: {}, price: {}, qty: {}",
                    trade.symbol, trade.price, trade.qty
                );
            }
            WebsocketEvent::DepthOrderBook(depth) => {
                let recv_time_us = now_us();

                println!(
                    "symbol={} event_time={} recv_time_us={} latency_us={}",
                    depth.symbol,
                    depth.event_time,
                    recv_time_us,
                    recv_time_us.saturating_sub(depth.event_time as u128 * 1000),
                );
            }
            WebsocketEvent::OrderBook(order_book) => {
                println!(
                    "last_update_id: {}, Bids: {:?}, Ask: {:?}",
                    order_book.last_update_id, order_book.bids, order_book.asks
                );
            }
            _ => (),
        };
        Ok(())
    });

    web_socket.connect(&stream).unwrap();
    if let Err(e) = web_socket.event_loop(&keep_running) {
        println!("Error: {}", e);
    }
    web_socket.disconnect().unwrap();
    println!("disconnected");
}

// All-symbols mini-ticker stream ("!miniTicker@arr"). Every push is a JSON
// ARRAY of all symbols' 24h stats (close, high, low, quote volume).
//
// This is deliberately NOT going through the `binance` crate's WebSockets
// helper: its `Events` enum only matches JSON objects keyed on the "e" field,
// so it cannot represent array payloads at all (the old "!ticker@arr" version
// here silently dropped every message because of exactly that). The sibling
// "!ticker@arr" stream itself was also verified dead upstream (connects but
// never pushes, as of 2026-09-19), so this reader uses the working
// "!miniTicker@arr" stream and parses the raw text with serde_json.
pub fn all_trades_websocket() {
    let url = "wss://stream.binance.com:9443/ws/!miniTicker@arr";
    let (mut ws, _response) = tungstenite::connect(url).expect("websocket connect failed");
    println!("connected: {}", url);

    loop {
        match ws.read() {
            Ok(Message::Text(text)) => {
                let parsed: serde_json::Value = match serde_json::from_str(&text) {
                    Ok(v) => v,
                    Err(_) => continue,
                };
                let items = match parsed.as_array() {
                    Some(a) => a,
                    None => continue,
                };
                for item in items {
                    println!(
                        "Symbol: {}, close: {}, high: {}, low: {}, quote_vol: {}",
                        item["s"].as_str().unwrap_or("-"),
                        item["c"].as_str().unwrap_or("-"),
                        item["h"].as_str().unwrap_or("-"),
                        item["l"].as_str().unwrap_or("-"),
                        item["q"].as_str().unwrap_or("-")
                    );
                }
            }
            Ok(Message::Ping(_) | Message::Pong(_) | Message::Binary(_) | Message::Frame(_)) => (),
            Ok(Message::Close(frame)) => {
                println!("closed by server: {:?}", frame);
                break;
            }
            Err(e) => {
                println!("Error: {}", e);
                break;
            }
        }
    }
    println!("disconnected");
}

// 1-minute kline (candlestick) stream for `symbol`.
pub fn kline_websocket(symbol: &str) {
    let keep_running = AtomicBool::new(true);
    let kline = format!("{}@kline_1m", symbol.to_lowercase());
    let mut web_socket = WebSockets::new(|event: WebsocketEvent| {
        if let WebsocketEvent::Kline(kline_event) = event {
            println!(
                "Symbol: {}, high: {}, low: {}",
                kline_event.kline.symbol, kline_event.kline.high, kline_event.kline.low
            );
        }
        Ok(())
    });

    web_socket.connect(&kline).unwrap();
    if let Err(e) = web_socket.event_loop(&keep_running) {
        println!("Error: {}", e);
    }
    web_socket.disconnect().unwrap();
    println!("disconnected");
}

// 24h rolling ticker for `symbol`; prints average and close price on every
// push. Runs until interrupted (Ctrl+C).
pub fn ticker_websocket(symbol: &str) {
    let keep_running = AtomicBool::new(true);
    let stream = format!("{}@ticker", symbol.to_lowercase());

    let mut web_socket = WebSockets::new(|event: WebsocketEvent| {
        if let WebsocketEvent::DayTicker(ticker_event) = event {
            let average_price: f32 = ticker_event.average_price.parse().unwrap();
            let current_close: f32 = ticker_event.current_close.parse().unwrap();
            println!("{} - {}", average_price, current_close);
        }
        Ok(())
    });

    web_socket.connect(&stream).unwrap();
    if let Err(e) = web_socket.event_loop(&keep_running) {
        println!("Error: {}", e);
    }
    web_socket.disconnect().unwrap();
    println!("disconnected");
}

// Subscribes to multiple streams (diff-depth for the given symbols) over one
// connection. The same pattern works for mixing stream types too.
pub fn multiple_streams(symbols: &[&str]) {
    let endpoints: Vec<String> = symbols
        .iter()
        .map(|symbol| format!("{}@depth@100ms", symbol.to_lowercase()))
        .collect();

    let keep_running = AtomicBool::new(true);
    let mut web_socket = WebSockets::new(|event: WebsocketEvent| {
        if let WebsocketEvent::DepthOrderBook(depth_order_book) = event {
            let top_bid = depth_order_book.bids.first();
            let top_ask = depth_order_book.asks.first();
            println!(
                "Symbol: {}, top_bid: {:?}@{:?}, top_ask: {:?}@{:?} (levels: {} bid / {} ask)",
                depth_order_book.symbol,
                top_bid.map(|b| b.price),
                top_bid.map(|b| b.qty),
                top_ask.map(|a| a.price),
                top_ask.map(|a| a.qty),
                depth_order_book.bids.len(),
                depth_order_book.asks.len()
            );
        }
        Ok(())
    });

    web_socket.connect_multiple_streams(&endpoints).unwrap();
    if let Err(e) = web_socket.event_loop(&keep_running) {
        println!("Error: {}", e);
    }
    web_socket.disconnect().unwrap();
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/connectors/mod.rs =====
//! Venue adapters.
//!
//! Each module under here is a *translation layer* and nothing more: it knows
//! the venue's wire format, its REST endpoints and its error codes, and it
//! converts all of that into the vocabulary in `crate::core`. The split is
//! enforced by what each side is allowed to import —
//!
//! ```text
//!   core      never imports connectors   (no venue name appears above this line)
//!   connectors never import pnl/risk     (an adapter does not decide what is safe)
//! ```
//!
//! A connector is therefore *auditable in isolation*: if a wrong number reaches
//! the ledger, the question narrows to "did this adapter decode the frame
//! correctly?", and answering it needs no knowledge of the strategy.
//!
//! | venue | market data | execution | account | streams |
//! |-------|-------------|-----------|---------|---------|
//! | [`binance`] | REST `ticker/price` | REST `order` | REST `account` | `websocket/` |

pub mod binance;


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/feature/compute.rs =====
//! Evaluate a compiled feature spec, one row at a time.
//!
//! This is the "compute" half of `core::feature`. It takes a [`FeatureRow`]
//! (produced by `ingest`) and returns a row of computed features. It has no
//! transport dependency, so it can be driven from a websocket, a replay file,
//! or the parity harness in `researcher-toolkit/stresstest/`.
//!
//! State is per feature: any `window` / `zscore` / `reconstruct` node owns a
//! window that advances by exactly one row per `compute` call, which is what
//! keeps the evaluation causal.

use super::row::FeatureRow;
use super::spec::{Expr, FeatureSpec, SpecError, parse_spec};
use super::window::{Aggregation, RollingWindow, aggregate, std_dev};

/// Anything that can stop a feature from being produced.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub enum FeatureError {
    /// The spec could not be compiled.
    Spec(SpecError),
    /// A column the spec references is absent from the row.
    MissingColumn(String),
    /// Division by an exact zero. Python raises `ZeroDivisionError` here, so
    /// both sides agree this is a hard failure rather than `inf`.
    DivisionByZero,
}

impl std::fmt::Display for FeatureError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        match self {
            Self::Spec(e) => write!(f, "{e}"),
            Self::MissingColumn(c) => write!(f, "missing required column: {c:?}"),
            Self::DivisionByZero => write!(f, "division by zero"),
        }
    }
}

impl std::error::Error for FeatureError {}

impl From<SpecError> for FeatureError {
    fn from(value: SpecError) -> Self {
        Self::Spec(value)
    }
}

/// Per-feature mutable state.
enum FeatureState {
    /// No history needed; evaluate directly.
    Stateless,
    /// A trailing window over the inner expression.
    Window { expr: Expr, window: RollingWindow },
    /// A trailing window plus the mean/std needed for a z-score.
    ZScore { expr: Expr, window: RollingWindow },
    /// A group accumulator for bar reconstruction.
    Reconstruct {
        expr: Expr,
        size: usize,
        aggregation: Aggregation,
        buffer: Vec<f64>,
    },
}

/// Stateful evaluator over a compiled spec.
pub struct FeatureComputer {
    features: Vec<(String, Expr)>,
    state: Vec<FeatureState>,
    rows_seen: u64,
}

impl FeatureComputer {
    /// Build from an already-parsed spec.
    pub fn new(specs: Vec<FeatureSpec>) -> Result<Self, FeatureError> {
        let mut state = Vec::with_capacity(specs.len());
        for spec in &specs {
            state.push(build_state(&spec.expr)?);
        }
        Ok(Self {
            features: specs.into_iter().map(|s| (s.name, s.expr)).collect(),
            state,
            rows_seen: 0,
        })
    }

    /// Compile a spec document and build from it. The normal entry point:
    /// hand it the JSON produced by the Python `FeaturePipeline.compile()`.
    pub fn from_spec_json(json: &str) -> Result<Self, FeatureError> {
        Ok(Self::new(parse_spec(json)?)?)
    }

    /// Feature names, in declaration order.
    pub fn feature_names(&self) -> Vec<&str> {
        self.features.iter().map(|(n, _)| n.as_str()).collect()
    }

    pub fn rows_seen(&self) -> u64 {
        self.rows_seen
    }

    /// Advance every feature by one row and return the computed values.
    ///
    /// A value that is legitimately undefined (for example the standard
    /// deviation of a single sample) is recorded as `Missing` rather than as
    /// `NaN` or `0.0`, so "not enough history" is never mistaken for a real
    /// measurement.
    pub fn compute(&mut self, row: &FeatureRow) -> Result<FeatureRow, FeatureError> {
        let mut out = FeatureRow::new();
        for index in 0..self.features.len() {
            let name = self.features[index].0.clone();
            match self.step(index, row)? {
                Some(value) => out.insert(name, value),
                None => out.insert_missing(name),
            }
        }
        self.rows_seen += 1;
        Ok(out)
    }

    /// Advance one feature, returning `None` when the value is undefined.
    fn step(&mut self, index: usize, row: &FeatureRow) -> Result<Option<f64>, FeatureError> {
        let expr = self.features[index].1.clone();
        match &mut self.state[index] {
            FeatureState::Stateless => eval(&expr, row).map(Some),
            FeatureState::Window { expr, window } => {
                let value = eval(expr, row)?;
                Ok(Some(window.push(value)))
            }
            FeatureState::ZScore { expr, window } => {
                let value = eval(expr, row)?;
                window.push(value);
                // The window now holds the values up to and including this
                // row, so the mean and std-dev are causal.
                let snapshot = window.snapshot();
                let mean = aggregate(&snapshot, Aggregation::Mean);
                let sd = std_dev(&snapshot);
                if sd.is_nan() {
                    return Ok(None);
                }
                Ok(Some((value - mean) / sd))
            }
            FeatureState::Reconstruct {
                expr,
                size,
                aggregation,
                buffer,
            } => {
                buffer.push(eval(expr, row)?);
                // Only completed groups are emitted, matching the Python
                // rule that an incomplete trailing bar is dropped.
                if buffer.len() < *size {
                    return Ok(None);
                }
                let value = aggregate(buffer, *aggregation);
                buffer.clear();
                Ok(Some(value))
            }
        }
    }
}

fn build_state(expr: &Expr) -> Result<FeatureState, FeatureError> {
    Ok(match expr {
        Expr::Window {
            expr,
            size,
            aggregation,
        } => FeatureState::Window {
            expr: (**expr).clone(),
            window: RollingWindow::new(*size, *aggregation)
                .map_err(|e| FeatureError::Spec(SpecError(e)))?,
        },
        Expr::ZScore { expr, size } => FeatureState::ZScore {
            expr: (**expr).clone(),
            // The window only accumulates; the z-score reduces it itself.
            window: RollingWindow::new(*size, Aggregation::Last)
                .map_err(|e| FeatureError::Spec(SpecError(e)))?,
        },
        Expr::Reconstruct {
            expr,
            size,
            aggregation,
        } => FeatureState::Reconstruct {
            expr: (**expr).clone(),
            size: *size,
            aggregation: *aggregation,
            buffer: Vec::with_capacity(*size),
        },
        _ => FeatureState::Stateless,
    })
}

/// Evaluate a stateless expression against one row.
fn eval(expr: &Expr, row: &FeatureRow) -> Result<f64, FeatureError> {
    match expr {
        Expr::Column(name) => row
            .get(name)
            .ok_or_else(|| FeatureError::MissingColumn(name.clone())),
        Expr::Literal(v) => Ok(*v),
        Expr::Add(a, b) => Ok(eval(a, row)? + eval(b, row)?),
        Expr::Sub(a, b) => Ok(eval(a, row)? - eval(b, row)?),
        Expr::Mul(a, b) => Ok(eval(a, row)? * eval(b, row)?),
        Expr::Div(a, b) => {
            let d = eval(b, row)?;
            if d == 0.0 {
                return Err(FeatureError::DivisionByZero);
            }
            Ok(eval(a, row)? / d)
        }
        // Stateful nodes are folded into a `FeatureState` at build time, so
        // reaching one here means the spec nests them.
        Expr::Window { .. } | Expr::ZScore { .. } | Expr::Reconstruct { .. } => {
            Err(FeatureError::Spec(SpecError(
                "nested stateful expressions are not supported".to_string(),
            )))
        }
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/feature/ingest.rs =====
//! Turn a raw market-data event into a [`FeatureRow`].
//!
//! This is the "query" half of `core::feature`, kept strictly separate from
//! `compute`: it knows about *where numbers come from*, never about *what is
//! computed from them*. That split is what lets the same feature definitions
//! run over a live websocket, a recorded file, or the parity harness.
//!
//! Binance sends prices and sizes as JSON **strings**, so they are parsed
//! explicitly here rather than being coerced by a generic deserializer.

use super::row::FeatureRow;

/// Canonical column names produced by [`row_from_frame`].
///
/// A feature spec refers to these, not to Binance's wire keys, so renaming a
/// wire field is a one-line change here instead of a spec rewrite.
pub const COL_EVENT_TIME: &str = "event_time_ms";
pub const COL_AGG_TRADE_ID: &str = "agg_trade_id";
pub const COL_PRICE: &str = "price";
pub const COL_QUANTITY: &str = "quantity";
pub const COL_BEST_BID_PRICE: &str = "best_bid_price";
pub const COL_BEST_BID_QTY: &str = "best_bid_qty";
pub const COL_BEST_ASK_PRICE: &str = "best_ask_price";
pub const COL_BEST_ASK_QTY: &str = "best_ask_qty";

/// Canonical column names taken from the merged table.
///
/// These are the `schema()` names of the live/historical merger, so a feature
/// spec written against a recorded capture names exactly the same columns the
/// live path produces. That shared naming is what lets the Python research
/// spec and the Rust executor agree without a remapping table.
pub const CANON_TIMESTAMP_NS: &str = "timestamp";
pub const CANON_EVENT_TYPE: &str = "event_type";
pub const CANON_EVENT_SEQUENCE: &str = "event_sequence";
pub const CANON_LOB_EVENT_TIME: &str = "lob__event_time";
pub const CANON_LOB_FINAL_UPDATE_ID: &str = "lob__final_update_id";
pub const CANON_AGG_TRADE_ID: &str = "agg__agg_trade_id";
pub const CANON_AGG_PRICE: &str = "agg__price";
pub const CANON_AGG_QUANTITY: &str = "agg__quantity";
pub const CANON_AGG_TRANSACT_TIME: &str = "agg__transact_time";
pub const CANON_AGG_IS_BUYER_MAKER: &str = "agg__is_buyer_maker";

/// `event_type` value on a row that carried a book update.
pub const EVENT_TYPE_LOB: &str = "LOB";
/// `event_type` value on a row that carried a trade.
pub const EVENT_TYPE_TRADE: &str = "TRADE";

/// `book_state__*` column names for a specific depth level.
pub fn book_price_column(level: usize) -> String {
    format!("book_state__bid_price_{level}")
}

pub fn book_qty_column(level: usize) -> String {
    format!("book_state__bid_qty_{level}")
}

pub fn ask_price_column(level: usize) -> String {
    format!("book_state__ask_price_{level}")
}

pub fn ask_qty_column(level: usize) -> String {
    format!("book_state__ask_qty_{level}")
}

pub const CANON_BEST_BID_PRICE: &str = "book_state__best_bid_price";
pub const CANON_BEST_BID_QTY: &str = "book_state__best_bid_qty";
pub const CANON_BEST_ASK_PRICE: &str = "book_state__best_ask_price";
pub const CANON_BEST_ASK_QTY: &str = "book_state__best_ask_qty";

/// One canonical row, encoded as the merge table's column values.
///
/// `None` means *absent*, which is not the same as `Some(0.0)`.
pub type CanonicalValues = std::collections::BTreeMap<String, Option<f64>>;

/// Identity fields a canonical row carries alongside the float map.
///
/// Kept separate because a timestamp or sequence number is an identity, not a
/// measurement: feeding `event_sequence` into a `mean()` is meaningless in a
/// way that feeding a price is not.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq)]
pub struct CanonicalIntegers {
    pub timestamp_ns: i64,
    pub event_sequence: u64,
    pub event_time_ms: Option<i64>,
    pub lob_final_update_id: Option<i64>,
    pub agg_trade_id: Option<u64>,
    pub agg_transact_time_ms: Option<i64>,
}
/// Build a [`FeatureRow`] from one canonical row.
///
/// Every column the row does not carry is recorded as `Missing`, never omitted
/// and never zero. A TRADE row genuinely has no `lob__*` identity; writing
/// `0.0` there would let a feature read "last update at time 0" and compute
/// garbage instead of failing loudly.
pub fn row_from_canonical(values: &CanonicalValues, integers: &CanonicalIntegers) -> FeatureRow {
    let mut row = FeatureRow::new();
    for (name, value) in values {
        match value {
            Some(v) => row.insert(name.clone(), *v),
            None => row.insert_missing(name.clone()),
        }
    }

    // Identity columns are always present, so features over them are total
    // functions of the stream rather than of whatever the row happened to hold.
    set(
        &mut row,
        CANON_TIMESTAMP_NS,
        Some(integers.timestamp_ns as f64),
    );
    set(
        &mut row,
        CANON_EVENT_SEQUENCE,
        Some(integers.event_sequence as f64),
    );
    set(&mut row, CANON_EVENT_TYPE, None);
    set(
        &mut row,
        CANON_LOB_EVENT_TIME,
        integers.event_time_ms.map(|v| v as f64),
    );
    set(
        &mut row,
        CANON_LOB_FINAL_UPDATE_ID,
        integers.lob_final_update_id.map(|v| v as f64),
    );
    set(
        &mut row,
        CANON_AGG_TRADE_ID,
        integers.agg_trade_id.map(|v| v as f64),
    );
    set(
        &mut row,
        CANON_AGG_TRANSACT_TIME,
        integers.agg_transact_time_ms.map(|v| v as f64),
    );
    row
}

/// The top of book, read from a canonical row while preserving absence.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq)]
pub struct CanonicalTop {
    pub best_bid_price: Option<f64>,
    pub best_bid_qty: Option<f64>,
    pub best_ask_price: Option<f64>,
    pub best_ask_qty: Option<f64>,
}

impl CanonicalTop {
    pub fn from_values(values: &CanonicalValues) -> Self {
        let get = |name: &str| values.get(name).copied().flatten();
        Self {
            best_bid_price: get(CANON_BEST_BID_PRICE),
            best_bid_qty: get(CANON_BEST_BID_QTY),
            best_ask_price: get(CANON_BEST_ASK_PRICE),
            best_ask_qty: get(CANON_BEST_ASK_QTY),
        }
    }

    /// Mid price, or `None` while either side is unknown.
    pub fn mid(&self) -> Option<f64> {
        match (self.best_bid_price, self.best_ask_price) {
            (Some(b), Some(a)) => Some((b + a) / 2.0),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// Top-of-book spread, or `None` while either side is unknown.
    ///
    /// Returning `None` rather than a clamped zero matters: an inverted book
    /// is a data fault, and reporting it as a real number would hide that
    /// fault inside a downstream feature.
    pub fn spread(&self) -> Option<f64> {
        match (self.best_bid_price, self.best_ask_price) {
            (Some(b), Some(a)) => Some(a - b),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// Order-book imbalance in [-1, 1], or `None` without both quantities.
    pub fn imbalance(&self) -> Option<f64> {
        let (b, a) = (self.best_bid_qty?, self.best_ask_qty?);
        let total = b + a;
        if total <= 0.0 {
            return None;
        }
        Some((b - a) / total)
    }
}

/// Top-of-book carried across events.
///
/// Kept separate from `FeatureRow` so a row holds only what a single frame
/// actually observed, and carried levels stay clearly derived.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq)]
pub struct BookTop {
    pub best_bid_price: Option<f64>,
    pub best_bid_qty: Option<f64>,
    pub best_ask_price: Option<f64>,
    pub best_ask_qty: Option<f64>,
}

impl BookTop {
    /// Update the top of book from one partial-book frame.
    ///
    /// `bids` arrives price-descending and `asks` price-ascending, so the
    /// first entry on each side is the best.
    pub fn apply_partial(&mut self, bids: &[(f64, f64)], asks: &[(f64, f64)]) {
        if let Some((price, qty)) = bids.first() {
            self.best_bid_price = Some(*price);
            self.best_bid_qty = Some(*qty);
        }
        if let Some((price, qty)) = asks.first() {
            self.best_ask_price = Some(*price);
            self.best_ask_qty = Some(*qty);
        }
    }
}

/// Build one row from a parsed websocket frame plus the current top of book.
///
/// A value the frame does not carry is recorded as `Missing` rather than
/// omitted, so a feature needing it fails loudly instead of reading a
/// silently-absent column as zero.
pub fn row_from_frame(frame: &serde_json::Value, book: &BookTop) -> FeatureRow {
    let mut row = FeatureRow::new();

    let event_type = frame
        .get("e")
        .and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)
        .unwrap_or_default();

    match event_type {
        "aggTrade" => {
            set(&mut row, COL_EVENT_TIME, num(frame.get("E")));
            set(&mut row, CANON_AGG_TRADE_ID, num(frame.get("a")));
            set(&mut row, COL_PRICE, num(frame.get("p")));
            set(&mut row, COL_QUANTITY, num(frame.get("q")));
        }
        "depthUpdate" | "bookTicker" => {
            set(&mut row, COL_EVENT_TIME, num(frame.get("E")));
        }
        _ => {}
    }

    // The top of book is carried onto every row: a trade row still has a book.
    for (name, value) in [
        (CANON_BEST_BID_PRICE, book.best_bid_price),
        (CANON_BEST_BID_QTY, book.best_bid_qty),
        (CANON_BEST_ASK_PRICE, book.best_ask_price),
        (CANON_BEST_ASK_QTY, book.best_ask_qty),
    ] {
        match value {
            Some(v) => row.insert(name, v),
            None => row.insert_missing(name),
        }
    }
    row
}

fn set(row: &mut FeatureRow, name: &str, value: Option<f64>) {
    match value {
        Some(v) => row.insert(name, v),
        None => row.insert_missing(name),
    }
}

/// Parse a Binance numeric field, which may arrive as a string or a number.
pub fn num(value: Option<&serde_json::Value>) -> Option<f64> {
    match value? {
        serde_json::Value::Number(n) => n.as_f64(),
        serde_json::Value::String(s) => s.parse().ok(),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// Parse a `"[["price","qty"], ...]` level array.
pub fn parse_levels(value: Option<&serde_json::Value>) -> Vec<(f64, f64)> {
    let Some(serde_json::Value::Array(levels)) = value else {
        return Vec::new();
    };
    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(levels.len());
    for entry in levels {
        let serde_json::Value::Array(pair) = entry else {
            continue;
        };
        if pair.len() < 2 {
            continue;
        }
        let Some(price) = num(Some(&pair[0])) else {
            continue;
        };
        let Some(qty) = num(Some(&pair[1])) else {
            continue;
        };
        out.push((price, qty));
    }
    out
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn agg_trade_string_fields_are_parsed() {
        let frame: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(
            r#"{"e":"aggTrade","E":1751245200354,"a":2773899591,"p":"108665.6","q":"0.002"}"#,
        )
        .unwrap();
        let row = row_from_frame(&frame, &BookTop::default());
        assert_eq!(row.get(COL_PRICE), Some(108665.6));
        assert_eq!(row.get(COL_QUANTITY), Some(0.002));
        assert_eq!(row.get(CANON_AGG_TRADE_ID), Some(2773899591.0));
        // No book supplied: recorded as missing, not zero.
        assert_eq!(row.get(CANON_BEST_BID_PRICE), None);
        assert!(row.get_value(CANON_BEST_BID_PRICE).unwrap().is_missing());
    }

    #[test]
    fn book_top_is_carried_onto_a_trade_row() {
        let book = BookTop {
            best_bid_price: Some(100.0),
            best_bid_qty: Some(1.0),
            best_ask_price: Some(101.0),
            best_ask_qty: Some(2.0),
        };
        let frame: serde_json::Value =
            serde_json::from_str(r#"{"e":"aggTrade","E":1,"a":2,"p":"100.5","q":"1"}"#).unwrap();
        let row = row_from_frame(&frame, &book);
        assert_eq!(row.get(CANON_BEST_BID_PRICE), Some(100.0));
        assert_eq!(row.get(CANON_BEST_ASK_PRICE), Some(101.0));
    }

    #[test]
    fn parses_string_level_arrays() {
        let value: serde_json::Value =
            serde_json::from_str(r#"[["108665.6","0.002"],["108664.0","1.5"]]"#).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            parse_levels(Some(&value)),
            vec![(108665.6, 0.002), (108664.0, 1.5)]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn partial_book_takes_the_first_level_per_side() {
        let mut book = BookTop::default();
        book.apply_partial(&[(99.0, 1.0), (98.0, 2.0)], &[(101.0, 3.0), (102.0, 4.0)]);
        assert_eq!(book.best_bid_price, Some(99.0));
        assert_eq!(book.best_ask_price, Some(101.0));
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/feature/mod.rs =====
//! Runtime feature computation.
//!
//! The module is split by responsibility so each half can be replaced without
//! touching the other:
//!
//! | file | role |
//! |------|------|
//! | [`ingest`] | where numbers come from (websocket frame -> row) |
//! | [`spec`] | the Python -> Rust bridge (JSON spec -> executable [`Expr`]) |
//! | [`compute`] | what is computed from them (row -> features) |
//! | [`window`] | causal trailing-window state |
//! | [`row`] | the row type shared by both halves |
//!
//! `ingest` and `compute` are deliberately independent: the same
//! [`FeatureComputer`] serves a live socket, a recorded file, and the parity
//! harness in `researcher-toolkit/stresstest/`.

mod compute;
mod ingest;
mod row;
mod spec;
mod window;

pub use compute::{FeatureComputer, FeatureError};
pub use ingest::{
    BookTop, CANON_AGG_IS_BUYER_MAKER, CANON_AGG_PRICE, CANON_AGG_QUANTITY, CANON_AGG_TRADE_ID,
    CANON_AGG_TRANSACT_TIME, CANON_BEST_ASK_PRICE, CANON_BEST_ASK_QTY, CANON_BEST_BID_PRICE,
    CANON_BEST_BID_QTY, CANON_EVENT_SEQUENCE, CANON_EVENT_TYPE, CANON_LOB_EVENT_TIME,
    CANON_LOB_FINAL_UPDATE_ID, CANON_TIMESTAMP_NS, COL_AGG_TRADE_ID, COL_BEST_ASK_PRICE,
    COL_BEST_ASK_QTY, COL_BEST_BID_PRICE, COL_BEST_BID_QTY, COL_EVENT_TIME, COL_PRICE,
    COL_QUANTITY, CanonicalIntegers, CanonicalTop, CanonicalValues, EVENT_TYPE_LOB,
    EVENT_TYPE_TRADE, ask_price_column, ask_qty_column, book_price_column, book_qty_column, num,
    parse_levels, row_from_canonical, row_from_frame,
};
pub use row::{FeatureRow, FeatureValue};
pub use spec::{Expr, FeatureSpec, SpecError, parse_expr, parse_spec};
pub use window::{Aggregation, RollingWindow, aggregate, std_dev};


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/feature/row.rs =====
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

/// A single feature or input value.
///
/// `Missing` is distinct from `Number(0.0)`: an absent input must never be
/// silently coerced into a zero, which would read as "no liquidity"/"flat book".
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub enum FeatureValue {
    Number(f64),
    Missing,
}

impl FeatureValue {
    pub fn number(&self) -> Option<f64> {
        match self {
            Self::Number(v) => Some(*v),
            Self::Missing => None,
        }
    }

    pub fn is_missing(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::Missing)
    }
}

impl From<f64> for FeatureValue {
    fn from(v: f64) -> Self {
        Self::Number(v)
    }
}

/// One observation: a flat map from column name to value.
///
/// This is the seam between the two halves of `core::feature`: `ingest` builds
/// rows from a websocket frame, `compute` consumes the same row shape and
/// returns a row of features. Neither knows about the other.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq)]
pub struct FeatureRow {
    values: BTreeMap<String, FeatureValue>,
}

impl FeatureRow {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self::default()
    }

    pub fn insert(&mut self, name: impl Into<String>, value: f64) {
        self.values.insert(name.into(), FeatureValue::Number(value));
    }

    /// Record an explicitly absent value, keeping it distinguishable from zero.
    pub fn insert_missing(&mut self, name: impl Into<String>) {
        self.values.insert(name.into(), FeatureValue::Missing);
    }

    pub fn insert_value(&mut self, name: impl Into<String>, value: FeatureValue) {
        self.values.insert(name.into(), value);
    }

    pub fn get(&self, name: &str) -> Option<f64> {
        self.values.get(name).and_then(FeatureValue::number)
    }

    pub fn get_value(&self, name: &str) -> Option<FeatureValue> {
        self.values.get(name).copied()
    }

    pub fn contains(&self, name: &str) -> bool {
        self.values.contains_key(name)
    }

    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
        self.values.len()
    }

    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.values.is_empty()
    }

    pub fn names(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &str> {
        self.values.keys().map(String::as_str)
    }

    pub fn iter(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&str, FeatureValue)> {
        self.values.iter().map(|(k, v)| (k.as_str(), *v))
    }
}

impl FromIterator<(String, FeatureValue)> for FeatureRow {
    fn from_iter<I: IntoIterator<Item = (String, FeatureValue)>>(iter: I) -> Self {
        Self {
            values: iter.into_iter().collect(),
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn missing_is_not_zero() {
        let mut row = FeatureRow::new();
        row.insert("bid", 0.0);
        row.insert_missing("ask");
        assert_eq!(row.get("bid"), Some(0.0));
        assert_eq!(row.get("ask"), None);
        assert!(row.get_value("ask").unwrap().is_missing());
    }

    #[test]
    fn round_trips_through_name_value_pairs() {
        let mut row = FeatureRow::new();
        row.insert("a", 1.0);
        row.insert_missing("b");
        let rebuilt: FeatureRow = row.iter().map(|(k, v)| (k.to_string(), v)).collect();
        assert_eq!(rebuilt, row);
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/feature/spec.rs =====
//! Parse the feature spec compiled by the Python side.
//!
//! This is the bridge that makes the pipeline lossless. Python builds a
//! deterministic JSON document (`FeaturePipeline.compile()`); this module
//! turns it back into an executable [`Expr`]. The op vocabulary is mirrored
//! exactly from `researcher-toolkit/live/feature.py`, and `stresstest/`
//! asserts the two evaluate identically.
//!
//! An unknown op is a hard error rather than a silent skip: a feature that
//! quietly evaluates to nothing is far worse than a startup failure.

use super::window::Aggregation;

/// A compiled feature expression.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub enum Expr {
    Column(String),
    Literal(f64),
    Add(Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>),
    Sub(Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>),
    Mul(Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>),
    Div(Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>),
    /// Causal trailing window over the inner expression.
    Window {
        expr: Box<Expr>,
        size: usize,
        aggregation: Aggregation,
    },
    /// Causal z-score of the inner expression over a trailing window.
    ZScore {
        expr: Box<Expr>,
        size: usize,
    },
    /// Bar reconstruction: group `size` consecutive rows, aggregate, and emit
    /// only completed groups. Mirrors `FeaturePipeline.reconstruct()`.
    Reconstruct {
        expr: Box<Expr>,
        size: usize,
        aggregation: Aggregation,
    },
}

/// Why a spec could not be compiled.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct SpecError(pub String);

impl std::fmt::Display for SpecError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "invalid feature spec: {}", self.0)
    }
}

impl std::error::Error for SpecError {}

fn err<T>(message: impl Into<String>) -> Result<T, SpecError> {
    Err(SpecError(message.into()))
}

/// One `(name, expression)` pair from the spec.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct FeatureSpec {
    pub name: String,
    pub expr: Expr,
}

/// Compile a whole spec document into executable features.
pub fn parse_spec(json: &str) -> Result<Vec<FeatureSpec>, SpecError> {
    let value: serde_json::Value =
        serde_json::from_str(json).map_err(|e| SpecError(e.to_string()))?;

    let version = value
        .get("version")
        .and_then(serde_json::Value::as_u64)
        .unwrap_or(1);
    if version != 1 {
        return err(format!("unsupported spec version {version}; expected 1"));
    }

    let features = value
        .get("features")
        .and_then(serde_json::Value::as_array)
        .ok_or_else(|| SpecError("spec is missing a 'features' array".to_string()))?;

    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(features.len());
    for entry in features {
        let name = entry
            .get("name")
            .and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)
            .ok_or_else(|| SpecError("feature is missing 'name'".to_string()))?
            .to_string();
        if name.is_empty() {
            return err("feature name must be non-empty");
        }
        let expr = parse_expr(
            entry
                .get("expr")
                .ok_or_else(|| SpecError(format!("feature '{name}' is missing 'expr'")))?,
        )?;
        out.push(FeatureSpec { name, expr });
    }
    Ok(out)
}

/// Compile one expression node.
pub fn parse_expr(value: &serde_json::Value) -> Result<Expr, SpecError> {
    let op = value
        .get("op")
        .and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)
        .ok_or_else(|| SpecError("expression is missing 'op'".to_string()))?;

    let args = value
        .get("args")
        .and_then(serde_json::Value::as_array)
        .ok_or_else(|| SpecError(format!("op '{op}' is missing 'args'")))?;

    match op {
        "column" => {
            if args.len() != 1 {
                return err(format!("column expects 1 arg, got {}", args.len()));
            }
            let name = args[0]
                .as_str()
                .ok_or_else(|| SpecError("column name must be a string".to_string()))?;
            if name.is_empty() {
                return err("column name must be non-empty");
            }
            Ok(Expr::Column(name.to_string()))
        }
        "literal" => {
            if args.len() != 1 {
                return err(format!("literal expects 1 arg, got {}", args.len()));
            }
            let v = args[0]
                .as_f64()
                .ok_or_else(|| SpecError("literal must be a number".to_string()))?;
            Ok(Expr::Literal(v))
        }
        "add" | "sub" | "mul" | "div" => binary(op, args),
        "window" => {
            if args.len() != 3 {
                return err(format!("window expects 3 args, got {}", args.len()));
            }
            Ok(Expr::Window {
                expr: Box::new(parse_expr(&args[0])?),
                size: size_arg(&args[1])?,
                aggregation: aggregation_arg(&args[2])?,
            })
        }
        "zscore" => {
            if args.len() != 2 {
                return err(format!("zscore expects 2 args, got {}", args.len()));
            }
            Ok(Expr::ZScore {
                expr: Box::new(parse_expr(&args[0])?),
                size: size_arg(&args[1])?,
            })
        }
        "reconstruct" => {
            if args.len() != 3 {
                return err(format!("reconstruct expects 3 args, got {}", args.len()));
            }
            Ok(Expr::Reconstruct {
                expr: Box::new(parse_expr(&args[0])?),
                size: size_arg(&args[1])?,
                aggregation: aggregation_arg(&args[2])?,
            })
        }
        other => err(format!("unknown op '{other}'")),
    }
}

fn binary(op: &str, args: &[serde_json::Value]) -> Result<Expr, SpecError> {
    if args.len() != 2 {
        return err(format!("{op} expects 2 args, got {}", args.len()));
    }
    let lhs = Box::new(parse_expr(&args[0])?);
    let rhs = Box::new(parse_expr(&args[1])?);
    Ok(match op {
        "add" => Expr::Add(lhs, rhs),
        "sub" => Expr::Sub(lhs, rhs),
        "mul" => Expr::Mul(lhs, rhs),
        _ => Expr::Div(lhs, rhs),
    })
}

fn size_arg(value: &serde_json::Value) -> Result<usize, SpecError> {
    let n = value
        .as_u64()
        .ok_or_else(|| SpecError("window size must be a non-negative integer".to_string()))?;
    if n == 0 {
        return err("window size must be positive");
    }
    Ok(n as usize)
}

fn aggregation_arg(value: &serde_json::Value) -> Result<Aggregation, SpecError> {
    let name = value
        .as_str()
        .ok_or_else(|| SpecError("aggregation must be a string".to_string()))?;
    Aggregation::parse(name).map_err(SpecError)
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn parses_the_bid_ask_example() {
        let json = r#"{"version":1,"features":[
            {"name":"bid_ask","expr":{"op":"sub","args":[
                {"op":"column","args":["ask_price"]},
                {"op":"column","args":["bid_price"]}]}}]}"#;
        let specs = parse_spec(json).expect("valid spec");
        assert_eq!(specs.len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(specs[0].name, "bid_ask");
        assert_eq!(
            specs[0].expr,
            Expr::Sub(
                Box::new(Expr::Column("ask_price".into())),
                Box::new(Expr::Column("bid_price".into()))
            )
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn parses_window_and_reconstruct() {
        let json = r#"{"version":1,"features":[
            {"name":"w","expr":{"op":"window","args":[
                {"op":"column","args":["bid_size"]},3,"mean"]}},
            {"name":"r","expr":{"op":"reconstruct","args":[
                {"op":"column","args":["price"]},2,"last"]}}]}"#;
        let specs = parse_spec(json).expect("valid spec");
        assert!(matches!(specs[0].expr, Expr::Window { size: 3, .. }));
        assert!(matches!(specs[1].expr, Expr::Reconstruct { size: 2, .. }));
    }

    #[test]
    fn unknown_op_is_an_error() {
        let json = r#"{"version":1,"features":[
            {"name":"x","expr":{"op":"skew","args":[]}}]}"#;
        assert!(parse_spec(json).is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn zero_window_size_is_an_error() {
        let json = r#"{"version":1,"features":[
            {"name":"x","expr":{"op":"window","args":[
                {"op":"column","args":["a"]},0,"mean"]}}]}"#;
        assert!(parse_spec(json).is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn unknown_version_is_an_error() {
        assert!(parse_spec(r#"{"version":99,"features":[]}"#).is_err());
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/feature/window.rs =====
//! Rolling window state for causal features.
//!
//! The window is strictly **causal and trailing**: after pushing the value for
//! row `i`, it holds the values for rows `max(0, i - size + 1) ..= i`. A short
//! window at the start of a stream aggregates over whatever exists so far
//! (equivalent to `min_periods = 1`), so a feature is defined from the very
//! first row rather than emitting nothing.
//!
//! This is the single definition of window semantics shared with the Python
//! side; `stresstest/` asserts the two agree exactly.

/// Aggregation applied to the values currently inside a window.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Aggregation {
    Mean,
    Sum,
    Min,
    Max,
    Last,
    /// Sample standard deviation (Bessel-corrected), matching pandas `.std()`.
    Std,
}

impl Aggregation {
    /// Parse the wire name emitted by the Python compiler.
    pub fn parse(name: &str) -> Result<Self, String> {
        match name {
            "mean" => Ok(Self::Mean),
            "sum" => Ok(Self::Sum),
            "min" => Ok(Self::Min),
            "max" => Ok(Self::Max),
            "last" => Ok(Self::Last),
            "std" => Ok(Self::Std),
            other => Err(format!("unsupported window aggregation: {other}")),
        }
    }

    pub fn as_str(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Self::Mean => "mean",
            Self::Sum => "sum",
            Self::Min => "min",
            Self::Max => "max",
            Self::Last => "last",
            Self::Std => "std",
        }
    }
}

/// Sample standard deviation (Bessel-corrected), matching pandas `.std()`.
///
/// A single sample has no spread, so it yields `NaN` rather than panicking;
/// callers propagate that as a missing feature value.
pub fn std_dev(values: &[f64]) -> f64 {
    let n = values.len();
    if n < 2 {
        return f64::NAN;
    }
    let mean = values.iter().sum::<f64>() / n as f64;
    let sq: f64 = values.iter().map(|v| (v - mean) * (v - mean)).sum();
    (sq / (n as f64 - 1.0)).sqrt()
}

/// A fixed-capacity trailing window.
///
/// Storage is a ring buffer, so `push` is `O(1)` amortized and never reallocates
/// after the first `size` pushes.
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct RollingWindow {
    size: usize,
    aggregation: Aggregation,
    values: Vec<f64>,
    /// Next slot to overwrite once the window is full.
    head: usize,
    filled: bool,
}

impl RollingWindow {
    pub fn new(size: usize, aggregation: Aggregation) -> Result<Self, String> {
        if size == 0 {
            return Err("window size must be positive".to_string());
        }
        Ok(Self {
            size,
            aggregation,
            values: vec![0.0; size],
            head: 0,
            filled: false,
        })
    }

    pub fn size(&self) -> usize {
        self.size
    }

    pub fn aggregation(&self) -> Aggregation {
        self.aggregation
    }

    /// Number of values currently held, `<= size`.
    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
        if self.filled { self.size } else { self.head }
    }

    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.len() == 0
    }

    /// Append the value for the current row and return the aggregation.
    ///
    /// `O(1)`: the outgoing value is overwritten in place once full.
    pub fn push(&mut self, value: f64) -> f64 {
        self.values[self.head] = value;
        self.head += 1;
        if self.head == self.size {
            self.head = 0;
            self.filled = true;
        }
        self.aggregate()
    }

    /// The values in chronological order (oldest first).
    pub fn snapshot(&self) -> Vec<f64> {
        let len = self.len();
        if !self.filled {
            return self.values[..len].to_vec();
        }
        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(self.size);
        // `head` is the oldest slot once the window is full.
        out.extend_from_slice(&self.values[self.head..]);
        out.extend_from_slice(&self.values[..self.head]);
        out
    }

    /// Current aggregate over the values currently in the window.
    pub fn aggregate(&self) -> f64 {
        aggregate(&self.snapshot(), self.aggregation)
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn mean_over_partial_then_full_window() {
        let mut w = RollingWindow::new(3, Aggregation::Mean).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(w.push(1.0), 1.0);
        assert_eq!(w.push(2.0), 1.5);
        assert_eq!(w.push(3.0), 2.0);
        // 1.0 falls out; 2, 3, 4 remain.
        assert_eq!(w.push(4.0), 3.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn ring_order_is_chronological() {
        let mut w = RollingWindow::new(3, Aggregation::Sum).unwrap();
        for v in [1.0, 2.0, 3.0, 4.0] {
            w.push(v);
        }
        assert_eq!(w.snapshot(), vec![2.0, 3.0, 4.0]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn last_min_max_track_trailing_window() {
        let mut w = RollingWindow::new(2, Aggregation::Last).unwrap();
        w.push(5.0);
        w.push(7.0);
        assert_eq!(w.aggregate(), 7.0);

        let mut w = RollingWindow::new(2, Aggregation::Min).unwrap();
        w.push(5.0);
        w.push(7.0);
        assert_eq!(w.aggregate(), 5.0);

        let mut w = RollingWindow::new(2, Aggregation::Max).unwrap();
        w.push(5.0);
        w.push(7.0);
        assert_eq!(w.aggregate(), 7.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn std_matches_sample_formula() {
        let values = [2.0, 4.0, 4.0, 4.0, 5.0, 5.0, 7.0, 9.0];
        assert!((std_dev(&values) - 2.13808993529939).abs() < 1e-12);
    }

    #[test]
    fn single_sample_std_is_nan() {
        assert!(std_dev(&[1.0]).is_nan());
    }

    #[test]
    fn zero_size_is_rejected() {
        assert!(RollingWindow::new(0, Aggregation::Mean).is_err());
    }
}
/// Aggregate a slice in chronological order.
///
/// The single reduction shared by [`RollingWindow::aggregate`] and
/// `compute.rs`, so the summation order â€” and therefore the floating-point
/// result â€” is identical in both places.
pub fn aggregate(values: &[f64], aggregation: Aggregation) -> f64 {
    if values.is_empty() {
        return f64::NAN;
    }
    match aggregation {
        Aggregation::Mean => values.iter().sum::<f64>() / values.len() as f64,
        Aggregation::Sum => values.iter().sum(),
        Aggregation::Min => values.iter().copied().fold(f64::INFINITY, f64::min),
        Aggregation::Max => values.iter().copied().fold(f64::NEG_INFINITY, f64::max),
        Aggregation::Last => values[values.len() - 1],
        Aggregation::Std => std_dev(values),
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/gui.rs =====
//! The GUI bridge: a directory of files, not a protocol.
//!
//! The dashboard is a vim window (or anything else that can `cat` a file), so
//! the interface is deliberately the dumbest one that works:
//!
//! ```text
//!   out/live/state.json     <- whole-frame rewrite, every publish  (rust -> gui)
//!   out/live/trades.jsonl   <- append-only, one trade per line     (rust -> gui)
//!   out/live/command.json   <- dashboard drops a line here         (gui -> rust)
//! ```
//!
//! Three properties this buys, and why each is worth a file:
//!
//! * **Crash-visibility.** State is on disk, so a process that dies mid-session
//!   leaves a readable last frame instead of an empty terminal.
//! * **No runtime coupling.** The dashboard can attach late, detach, or restart
//!   without the executor noticing, and neither side links the other.
//! * **One-directional risk.** Only [`DashboardCommand`] travels back, and the
//!   loop decides what to do with it — the dashboard can ask, never act.
//!
//! Writes go through `telemetry::write_json_atomic`, so a reader never sees a
//! half-written frame (the same guarantee `monitor.rs` gives `display.py`).

use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::fs::{self, OpenOptions};
use std::io::{self, Write};
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

use super::order::Side;
use super::portfolio::Portfolio;
use super::stats::PerformanceStats;
use super::telemetry::{TelemetrySnapshot, json_number, json_string, now_ms, write_json_atomic};

pub const STATE_FILE: &str = "state.json";
pub const TRADES_FILE: &str = "trades.jsonl";
pub const COMMAND_FILE: &str = "command.json";

/// What the dashboard draws for one open symbol.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct OpenPosition {
    pub symbol: String,
    pub side: Side,
    pub size: f64,
    pub avg_entry: f64,
    /// The price this row was valued at, so the dashboard can show whether the
    /// mark is fresh or just the entry carried forward.
    pub mark: f64,
    pub unrealized_pnl: f64,
}

/// One complete frame of what the dashboard shows.
///
/// Built by [`DashboardState::capture`] from live objects, so nothing on screen
/// comes from a different instant than anything else on screen.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct DashboardState {
    pub active_strategy: String,
    pub venue: &'static str,
    pub symbol: String,
    pub starting_equity: f64,
    pub equity: f64,
    pub cash: f64,
    pub realized_pnl: f64,
    pub unrealized_pnl: f64,
    pub gross_exposure: f64,
    pub positions: Vec<OpenPosition>,
    pub stats: PerformanceStats,
    pub telemetry: TelemetrySnapshot,
    pub updated_ms: u64,
}

impl DashboardState {
    /// Read the strategy, the book and the counters into one frame.
    ///
    /// `symbol` is the instrument being traded (the dashboard is per-symbol);
    /// every symbol in the book is still listed, because a hedge on a second
    /// instrument must not be invisible.
    ///
    /// Call `Portfolio::mark(&marks)` before this: `PerformanceStats` reads the
    /// equity curve, so a portfolio that is never marked reports a cumulative
    /// return of zero however much it has actually made.
    pub fn capture(
        active_strategy: impl Into<String>,
        venue: &'static str,
        symbol: impl Into<String>,
        portfolio: &Portfolio,
        marks: &BTreeMap<String, f64>,
        telemetry: &TelemetrySnapshot,
    ) -> Self {
        let positions = portfolio
            .holdings()
            .open_holdings()
            .map(|(symbol, holding)| {
                // Same rule as the book: an unmarked symbol contributes its
                // entry price rather than a fabricated mark.
                let mark = marks.get(symbol).copied().unwrap_or(holding.avg_entry);
                OpenPosition {
                    symbol: symbol.clone(),
                    side: holding.side().unwrap_or(Side::Buy),
                    size: holding.size,
                    avg_entry: holding.avg_entry,
                    mark,
                    unrealized_pnl: holding.unrealized_pnl(mark),
                }
            })
            .collect();
        Self {
            active_strategy: active_strategy.into(),
            venue,
            symbol: symbol.into(),
            starting_equity: portfolio.starting_cash(),
            equity: portfolio.equity(marks),
            cash: portfolio.cash(),
            realized_pnl: portfolio.ledger().realized_pnl(),
            unrealized_pnl: portfolio.unrealized_pnl(marks),
            gross_exposure: portfolio.gross_exposure(marks),
            positions,
            // Un-annualised: an intraday session is not a yearly sample, and
            // multiplying it by sqrt(252) would flatter it on screen.
            stats: PerformanceStats::from_ledger(portfolio.ledger(), 1.0),
            telemetry: *telemetry,
            updated_ms: now_ms(),
        }
    }
}
impl DashboardState {
    /// The frame as one JSON object. This is the file a vim dashboard reads.
    pub fn to_json(&self) -> String {
        let positions = self
            .positions
            .iter()
            .map(|p| {
                format!(
                    "{{\"symbol\":{},\"side\":{},\"size\":{},\"avg_entry\":{},\"mark\":{},\"unrealized_pnl\":{}}}",
                    json_string(&p.symbol),
                    json_string(if p.side.is_buy() { "long" } else { "short" }),
                    json_number(p.size),
                    json_number(p.avg_entry),
                    json_number(p.mark),
                    json_number(p.unrealized_pnl),
                )
            })
            .collect::<Vec<_>>()
            .join(",");
        format!(
            concat!(
                "{{\"updated_ms\":{},\"active_strategy\":{},\"venue\":{},\"symbol\":{},",
                "\"starting_equity\":{},\"equity\":{},\"cash\":{},\"realized_pnl\":{},",
                "\"unrealized_pnl\":{},\"gross_exposure\":{},\"positions\":[{}],",
                "\"sortino\":{},\"win_rate\":{},\"cumulative_return\":{},\"max_drawdown\":{},",
                "\"trades\":{},\"wins\":{},\"losses\":{},\"telemetry\":{}}}"
            ),
            self.updated_ms,
            json_string(&self.active_strategy),
            json_string(self.venue),
            json_string(&self.symbol),
            json_number(self.starting_equity),
            json_number(self.equity),
            json_number(self.cash),
            json_number(self.realized_pnl),
            json_number(self.unrealized_pnl),
            json_number(self.gross_exposure),
            positions,
            json_number(self.stats.sortino),
            json_number(self.stats.win_rate),
            json_number(self.stats.cumulative_return),
            json_number(self.stats.max_drawdown),
            self.stats.trades,
            self.stats.wins,
            self.stats.losses,
            self.telemetry.to_json(),
        )
    }
}

/// What the dashboard is allowed to ask for.
///
/// A deliberately closed set. Every variant is something the *loop* may decide
/// to ignore — the dashboard has no way to reach a connector directly, which is
/// what keeps a display bug from becoming a trading bug.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum DashboardCommand {
    /// Close everything and stop opening new risk.
    Flatten,
    /// Stop opening new risk; leave what is open alone.
    Pause,
    Resume,
    /// Switch the active strategy by registry name.
    SetStrategy(String),
    /// Forget the trade log (a fresh session's numbers, not a fresh account).
    ClearTrades,
}

impl DashboardCommand {
    pub fn as_str(&self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Self::Flatten => "flatten",
            Self::Pause => "pause",
            Self::Resume => "resume",
            Self::SetStrategy(_) => "set_strategy",
