            Self::ClearTrades => "clear_trades",
        }
    }

    /// Parse one command line: `{"action":"pause"}` or
    /// `{"action":"set_strategy","value":"strategy2"}`.
    ///
    /// Hand-parsed for the same reason the writer is hand-rolled: the dashboard
    /// side of this contract is a text editor, and one `find` is a smaller
    /// dependency than a deserialiser. Unknown fields are ignored, so a richer
    /// dashboard stays compatible with an older loop.
    pub fn parse(line: &str) -> Result<Self, CommandError> {
        let line = line.trim();
        if line.is_empty() {
            return Err(CommandError::Empty);
        }
        let action = field(line, "action").ok_or(CommandError::MissingAction)?;
        match action {
            "flatten" => Ok(Self::Flatten),
            "pause" => Ok(Self::Pause),
            "resume" => Ok(Self::Resume),
            "clear_trades" => Ok(Self::ClearTrades),
            "set_strategy" => field(line, "value")
                .map(|value| Self::SetStrategy(value.to_string()))
                .ok_or(CommandError::MissingValue("set_strategy")),
            other => Err(CommandError::UnknownAction(other.to_string())),
        }
    }
}

/// Why a command file could not be honoured.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub enum CommandError {
    Empty,
    MissingAction,
    MissingValue(&'static str),
    UnknownAction(String),
}

impl std::fmt::Display for CommandError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        match self {
            Self::Empty => write!(f, "command file was empty"),
            Self::MissingAction => write!(f, "command has no \"action\" field"),
            Self::MissingValue(action) => write!(f, "{action} needs a \"value\" field"),
            Self::UnknownAction(action) => write!(f, "unknown action {action:?}"),
        }
    }
}

impl std::error::Error for CommandError {}

/// The value of `"key": "..."` in a flat JSON object, if present.
///
/// Escaped quotes are skipped rather than unescaped: the actions and strategy
/// names this is used for are plain identifiers, and inventing an unescaper that
/// is never exercised would be untested code on the hot path.
fn field<'a>(json: &'a str, key: &str) -> Option<&'a str> {
    let needle = format!("\"{key}\"");
    let after_key = &json[json.find(&needle)? + needle.len()..];
    let after_colon = after_key.get(after_key.find(':')? + 1..)?.trim_start();
    let quoted = after_colon.strip_prefix('"')?;
    let bytes = quoted.as_bytes();
    let mut end = 0;
    while end < bytes.len() {
        match bytes[end] {
            // `\` and `"` are ASCII, so a UTF-8 continuation byte can never be
            // mistaken for either and these offsets always land on char bounds.
            b'\\' => end += 2,
            b'"' => return Some(&quoted[..end]),
            _ => end += 1,
        }
    }
    None
}
/// The directory the dashboard reads and writes.
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct GuiBridge {
    dir: PathBuf,
}

impl GuiBridge {
    pub fn new(dir: impl Into<PathBuf>) -> Self {
        Self { dir: dir.into() }
    }

    /// The same `out/live` directory `monitor.rs` publishes frames into, so a
    /// dashboard has one place to watch instead of two.
    pub fn in_out_dir() -> Self {
        Self::new(
            PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"))
                .join("out")
                .join("live"),
        )
    }

    pub fn dir(&self) -> &Path {
        &self.dir
    }

    pub fn state_path(&self) -> PathBuf {
        self.dir.join(STATE_FILE)
    }

    pub fn trades_path(&self) -> PathBuf {
        self.dir.join(TRADES_FILE)
    }

    pub fn command_path(&self) -> PathBuf {
        self.dir.join(COMMAND_FILE)
    }

    /// Publish the frame and return where it landed, for the log line.
    pub fn publish(&self, state: &DashboardState) -> io::Result<PathBuf> {
        let path = self.state_path();
        write_json_atomic(&path, &state.to_json())?;
        Ok(path)
    }

    /// Append one closed trade as a line.
    ///
    /// Append-only and never rewritten: it is the session's audit trail, and a
    /// log a crash can truncate mid-line is still readable up to the last
    /// complete entry. A malformed tail is the reader's problem, not the
    /// writer's, which is why this is not JSON-arrayed into `state.json`.
    pub fn append_trade(&self, trade: &super::pnl::Trade) -> io::Result<()> {
        fs::create_dir_all(&self.dir)?;
        let mut file = OpenOptions::new()
            .create(true)
            .append(true)
            .open(self.trades_path())?;
        writeln!(file, "{}", trade_json(trade))
    }

    /// Empty the trade log. Truncates rather than deletes so a `tail -f` on the
    /// dashboard keeps its file handle.
    pub fn clear_trades(&self) -> io::Result<()> {
        fs::create_dir_all(&self.dir)?;
        fs::write(self.trades_path(), b"")
    }

    /// Read a command without consuming it.
    ///
    /// `Ok(None)` covers both "no command file" and "command file is blank" —
    /// the dashboard clears the file after acting, and neither state is an
    /// error.
    pub fn read_command(&self) -> io::Result<Option<Result<DashboardCommand, CommandError>>> {
        match fs::read_to_string(self.command_path()) {
            Ok(text) => {
                let text = text.trim();
                if text.is_empty() {
                    Ok(None)
                } else {
                    Ok(Some(DashboardCommand::parse(text)))
                }
            }
            Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(None),
            Err(e) => Err(e),
        }
    }

    /// Read the command and delete the file, so one keystroke is one action.
    ///
    /// The file is removed even when the parse failed: a dashboard that writes
    /// garbage would otherwise wedge every later command behind it, and the
    /// error is returned either way.
    pub fn take_command(&self) -> io::Result<Option<Result<DashboardCommand, CommandError>>> {
        let path = self.command_path();
        let taken = match self.read_command() {
            Ok(taken) => taken,
            Err(e) => return Err(e),
        };
        match (taken, fs::remove_file(&path)) {
            (Some(command), Ok(())) => Ok(Some(command)),
            (Some(command), Err(e)) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(Some(command)),
            (Some(_), Err(e)) => Err(e),
            (None, _) => Ok(None),
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::core::order::{Fill, OrderId};
    use crate::core::pnl::Trade;
    use crate::core::telemetry::Telemetry;
    use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering as AtomicOrdering};

    fn scratch(name: &str) -> PathBuf {
        static SEQ: AtomicU32 = AtomicU32::new(0);
        std::env::temp_dir()
            .join(format!("uets-gui-{}", std::process::id()))
            .join(format!(
                "{}-{}",
                name,
                SEQ.fetch_add(1, AtomicOrdering::Relaxed)
            ))
    }

    fn marks(price: f64) -> BTreeMap<String, f64> {
        let mut m = BTreeMap::new();
        m.insert("BTCUSDT".to_string(), price);
        m
    }

    /// A portfolio holding one long, one banked winner, marked at 150.
    fn booked() -> (Portfolio, BTreeMap<String, f64>) {
        let mut portfolio = Portfolio::new(1_000.0, 10.0).unwrap();
        portfolio.apply_fill(&Fill::new(
            OrderId::new("a"),
            "BTCUSDT",
            Side::Buy,
            1.0,
            100.0,
            1.0,
        ));
        portfolio.record_trade(Trade::new(
            "BTCUSDT",
            Side::Buy,
            100.0,
            120.0,
            1.0,
            1.0,
            1,
            2,
        ));
        let marks = marks(150.0);
        portfolio.mark(&marks);
        (portfolio, marks)
    }

    #[test]
    fn capture_reads_one_consistent_frame_from_the_live_objects() {
        let (portfolio, marks) = booked();
        let telemetry = Telemetry::new();
        telemetry.record_row();
        telemetry.record_event(telemetry.start_ms());
        let state = DashboardState::capture(
            "strategy1",
            "binance",
            "BTCUSDT",
            &portfolio,
            &marks,
            &telemetry.snapshot(),
        );

        assert_eq!(state.active_strategy, "strategy1");
        assert_eq!(state.starting_equity, 1_000.0);
        // Cash follows the fills: only the open fill's fee lands here, while
        // the banked trade's own fee belongs to the ledger's net pnl.
        assert_eq!(state.cash, 999.0);
        assert_eq!(state.unrealized_pnl, 50.0);
        assert_eq!(state.equity, 1_049.0, "cash plus the open 50");
        assert_eq!(state.gross_exposure, 150.0);
        assert_eq!(state.realized_pnl, 19.0, "+20 gross less 2.0 of fees");
        assert_eq!(state.telemetry.rows, 1);

        assert_eq!(state.positions.len(), 1);
        let position = &state.positions[0];
        assert_eq!(position.symbol, "BTCUSDT");
        assert_eq!(position.side, Side::Buy);
        assert_eq!(position.mark, 150.0);
        assert_eq!(position.unrealized_pnl, 50.0);

        // `mark()` above recorded 1_049 into the curve, which is what makes the
        // dashboard's cumulative return the live figure rather than a zero.
        assert!((state.stats.cumulative_return - 0.049).abs() < 1e-12);
        assert_eq!(state.stats.wins, 1);
        assert_eq!(state.stats.losses, 0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_open_position_without_a_mark_reports_its_entry_rather_than_a_number() {
        let mut portfolio = Portfolio::new(1_000.0, 10.0).unwrap();
        portfolio.apply_fill(&Fill::new(
            OrderId::new("a"),
            "ETHUSDT",
            Side::Sell,
            2.0,
            10.0,
            0.0,
        ));
        let state = DashboardState::capture(
            "s",
            "stub",
            "ETHUSDT",
            &portfolio,
            &BTreeMap::new(),
            &TelemetrySnapshot::default(),
        );
        let position = &state.positions[0];
        assert_eq!(position.side, Side::Sell);
        assert_eq!(position.mark, position.avg_entry);
        assert_eq!(position.unrealized_pnl, 0.0, "no mark, no invented pnl");
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_frame_is_json_a_text_editor_can_read() {
        let (portfolio, marks) = booked();
        let state = DashboardState::capture(
            "strategy1",
            "binance",
            "BTCUSDT",
            &portfolio,
            &marks,
            &TelemetrySnapshot::default(),
        );
        let json = state.to_json();
        assert!(json.starts_with('{') && json.ends_with('}'));
        assert!(json.contains("\"active_strategy\":\"strategy1\""));
        assert!(json.contains("\"cumulative_return\":0.049"));
        assert!(json.contains("\"win_rate\":1"));
        assert!(json.contains("\"positions\":[{\"symbol\":\"BTCUSDT\",\"side\":\"long\""));
        assert!(
            json.contains("\"sortino\":"),
            "the headline ratio is present"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_trade_log_appends_a_line_per_trade_and_can_be_cleared() {
        let dir = scratch("trades");
        let bridge = GuiBridge::new(&dir);
        let trade = Trade::new("BTCUSDT", Side::Buy, 100.0, 120.0, 1.0, 1.0, 4, 9);
        bridge.append_trade(&trade).unwrap();
        bridge.append_trade(&trade).unwrap();
        let lines: Vec<String> = fs::read_to_string(bridge.trades_path())
            .unwrap()
            .lines()
            .map(str::to_string)
            .collect();
        assert_eq!(lines.len(), 2, "append, never rewrite");
        assert!(lines[0].starts_with("{\"symbol\":\"BTCUSDT\""));
        assert!(lines[0].contains("\"net_pnl\":19"));
        assert!(lines[0].contains("\"win\":true"));
        assert!(lines[0].contains("\"exit_seq\":9"));

        bridge.clear_trades().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(fs::read_to_string(bridge.trades_path()).unwrap(), "");
        let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
    }

    #[test]
    fn publishing_creates_the_directory_and_rewrites_the_frame() {
        let dir = scratch("publish");
        let bridge = GuiBridge::new(dir.join("nested"));
        let (portfolio, marks) = booked();
        let state = DashboardState::capture(
            "strategy1",
            "binance",
            "BTCUSDT",
            &portfolio,
            &marks,
            &TelemetrySnapshot::default(),
        );
        let path = bridge.publish(&state).unwrap();
        assert!(path.exists());
        assert!(
            fs::read_to_string(&path)
                .unwrap()
                .contains("\"equity\":1049")
        );
        let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_command_parses_from_the_line_a_dashboard_would_write() {
        assert_eq!(
            DashboardCommand::parse("{\"action\":\"pause\"}").unwrap(),
            DashboardCommand::Pause
        );
        assert_eq!(
            DashboardCommand::parse(" {\"action\":\"flatten\"} \n").unwrap(),
            DashboardCommand::Flatten
        );
        assert_eq!(
            DashboardCommand::parse(r#"{"action":"set_strategy","value":"strategy2"}"#).unwrap(),
            DashboardCommand::SetStrategy("strategy2".to_string())
        );
        // Unknown fields are ignored, so a newer dashboard still works here.
        assert_eq!(
            DashboardCommand::parse("{\"ts\":12,\"action\":\"resume\"}").unwrap(),
            DashboardCommand::Resume
        );
        // Escapes are passed through raw: identifiers need no unescaper.
        assert_eq!(
            DashboardCommand::parse("{\"action\":\"set_strategy\",\"value\":\"a\\\"b\"}").unwrap(),
            DashboardCommand::SetStrategy("a\\\"b".to_string())
        );
        for command in [
            DashboardCommand::Flatten,
            DashboardCommand::Pause,
            DashboardCommand::Resume,
            DashboardCommand::ClearTrades,
            DashboardCommand::SetStrategy("s".to_string()),
        ] {
            let line = format!("{{\"action\":\"{}\"}}", command.as_str());
            if let DashboardCommand::SetStrategy(name) = &command {
                assert_eq!(
                    DashboardCommand::parse(&line),
                    Err(CommandError::MissingValue("set_strategy")),
                    "a strategy switch without a name ({name}) must not silently pass"
                );
            } else {
                assert_eq!(DashboardCommand::parse(&line).unwrap(), command);
            }
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_bad_command_says_which_kind_of_bad_it_is() {
        assert_eq!(DashboardCommand::parse("   "), Err(CommandError::Empty));
        assert_eq!(
            DashboardCommand::parse("{\"note\":\"hello\"}"),
            Err(CommandError::MissingAction)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            DashboardCommand::parse("{\"action\":\"explode\"}"),
            Err(CommandError::UnknownAction("explode".to_string()))
        );
        assert_eq!(
            DashboardCommand::parse("{\"action\":\"set_strategy\"}")
                .unwrap_err()
                .to_string(),
            "set_strategy needs a \"value\" field"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn take_command_consumes_the_file_exactly_once_even_when_it_is_garbage() {
        let dir = scratch("command");
        let bridge = GuiBridge::new(&dir);
        assert_eq!(
            bridge.take_command().unwrap(),
            None,
            "no file is not an error"
        );

        fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
        fs::write(bridge.command_path(), b"{\"action\":\"flatten\"}\n").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            bridge.read_command().unwrap().unwrap().unwrap(),
            DashboardCommand::Flatten
        );
        assert!(bridge.command_path().exists(), "reading leaves it in place");
        assert_eq!(
            bridge.take_command().unwrap().unwrap().unwrap(),
            DashboardCommand::Flatten
        );
        assert!(!bridge.command_path().exists(), "taking removes it");
        assert_eq!(bridge.take_command().unwrap(), None);

        // Garbage is consumed *and* reported: a poison file must not wedge the
        // loop behind it.
        fs::write(bridge.command_path(), b"{ not json").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            bridge.take_command().unwrap().unwrap(),
            Err(CommandError::MissingAction)
        );
        assert!(!bridge.command_path().exists());

        fs::write(bridge.command_path(), b"   \n").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            bridge.take_command().unwrap(),
            None,
            "a blank file is no command"
        );
        let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
    }
}

/// One trade as a JSON line.
fn trade_json(trade: &super::pnl::Trade) -> String {
    format!(
        concat!(
            "{{\"symbol\":{},\"side\":{},\"entry_price\":{},\"exit_price\":{},\"qty\":{},",
            "\"fee\":{},\"gross_pnl\":{},\"net_pnl\":{},\"win\":{},\"entry_seq\":{},",
            "\"exit_seq\":{}}}"
        ),
        json_string(&trade.symbol),
        json_string(if trade.side.is_buy() { "long" } else { "short" }),
        json_number(trade.entry_price),
        json_number(trade.exit_price),
        json_number(trade.qty),
        json_number(trade.fee),
        json_number(trade.gross_pnl()),
        json_number(trade.net_pnl()),
        trade.is_win(),
        trade.entry_sequence,
        trade.exit_sequence,
    )
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/mod.rs =====
//! `core` — the custom-made engine: everything this project owns.
//!
//! Nothing here talks to a broker. Buying, selling, marks, and reading the
//! account all live behind the connector traits in [`crate::exchange`]; `core`
//! consumes their results (`Fill`, `AccountSnapshot`) as *data*. What remains
//! is pure logic plus local file I/O:
//!
//! | module | role |
//! |--------|------|
//! | [`order`] | `Side` / `OrderType` / `OrderRequest` / `Order` / `Fill` — the vocabulary |
//! | [`position`] | `Holding` + `PositionBook`: net size per symbol, marked to market |
//! | [`pnl`] | `Trade` + `PnlLedger`: realized pnl, equity curve, drawdown |
//! | [`portfolio`] | `Portfolio`: cash, margin and equity over the book + ledger |
//! | [`risk`] | `RiskLimits` + pre-trade checks, applied to every venue alike |
//! | [`stats`] | `sortino`, `win_rate`, `cumulative_return`, `max_drawdown` |
//! | [`telemetry`] | counters + JSON writer, plus [`telemetry::latency`] and [`telemetry::skew`] diagnostics |
//! | [`gui`] | the file bridge a vim (or any) dashboard reads and writes |
//! | [`feature`] | the feature engine: ingest -> spec -> compute -> row |
//! | [`strategy`] | strategy primitives: `Decision`, `MarketState`, model choice |
//!
//! The split that matters: **`core` never knows a venue's wire format**, and
//! `connectors/<venue>` never knows about pnl or risk. `Portfolio` is the
//! system's own view of the account; `crate::exchange::AccountConnector::snapshot`
//! returns the venue's own figures, and the two are reconciled rather than
//! conflated. The boundary is enforced by `tests/architecture.rs`, which
//! scans these sources from the outside.

pub mod feature;
pub mod gui;
pub mod order;
pub mod pnl;
pub mod portfolio;
pub mod position;
pub mod risk;
pub mod stats;
pub mod strategy;
pub mod telemetry;


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/order.rs =====
//! Exchange-agnostic order vocabulary.
//!
//! Binance spells order states `NEW`/`FILLED` in uppercase strings, Bybit uses
//! its own `OrderStatus` enum, Bitfinex numeric flags. All of that translation
//! belongs in `connectors/<venue>`; everything above it speaks the types below,
//! so the executor never matches on a venue string.

use std::fmt;

/// Which side of the book an order takes.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum Side {
    Buy,
    Sell,
}

impl Side {
    pub fn as_str(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Self::Buy => "buy",
            Self::Sell => "sell",
        }
    }

    /// The side that *closes* a position held on `self`.
    ///
    /// Every venue closes by trading the opposite side; encoding that once here
    /// is what keeps `ExecutionConnector::exit` venue-independent.
    pub fn opposite(self) -> Self {
        match self {
            Self::Buy => Self::Sell,
            Self::Sell => Self::Buy,
        }
    }

    pub fn is_buy(self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::Buy)
    }

    /// How much this side adds to the signed position size.
    pub fn signed(self, qty: f64) -> f64 {
        if self.is_buy() { qty } else { -qty }
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for Side {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(self.as_str())
    }
}

/// How the order is priced.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum OrderType {
    Market,
    Limit,
}

impl OrderType {
    pub fn as_str(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Self::Market => "market",
            Self::Limit => "limit",
        }
    }
}

/// How long a resting order may wait for its opposite flow.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum TimeInForce {
    Gtc,
    Ioc,
    Fok,
}

/// Where an order is in its lifecycle.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum OrderStatus {
    Pending,
    Open,
    PartiallyFilled,
    Filled,
    Cancelled,
    Rejected,
}

impl OrderStatus {
    /// Still capable of producing another fill.
    pub fn is_live(self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::Pending | Self::Open | Self::PartiallyFilled)
    }

    /// No further change is possible.
    pub fn is_terminal(self) -> bool {
        !self.is_live()
    }
}

/// A client-side order id.
///
/// A `String`, not a `u64`: Binance only reveals its numeric `order_id` after
/// the round trip, and a client id is what survives a reconnect or a restart.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)]
pub struct OrderId(pub String);

impl OrderId {
    pub fn new(id: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self(id.into())
    }

    pub fn as_str(&self) -> &str {
        &self.0
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for OrderId {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(&self.0)
    }
}

impl From<&str> for OrderId {
    fn from(s: &str) -> Self {
        Self(s.to_string())
    }
}

impl From<String> for OrderId {
    fn from(s: String) -> Self {
        Self(s)
    }
}

/// What the executor hands a venue to open or close exposure.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct OrderRequest {
    pub symbol: String,
    pub side: Side,
    pub qty: f64,
    pub order_type: OrderType,
    /// Required for [`OrderType::Limit`], ignored for [`OrderType::Market`].
    pub limit_price: Option<f64>,
    pub time_in_force: TimeInForce,
    /// Venue hint: refuse to *increase* exposure, only reduce it. Set by exits.
    pub reduce_only: bool,
}

impl OrderRequest {
    /// A market order — what enter/exit use.
    pub fn market(symbol: impl Into<String>, side: Side, qty: f64) -> Self {
        Self {
            symbol: symbol.into(),
            side,
            qty,
            order_type: OrderType::Market,
            limit_price: None,
            time_in_force: TimeInForce::Gtc,
            reduce_only: false,
        }
    }

    /// A resting limit order.
    ///
    /// The price is snapped to `tick_size` with `(price / tick).round() *
    /// tick` at construction, so an order built from a dusty float (e.g.
    /// `108613.20000000001`) can never reach the venue off-tick and every
    /// `==` comparison downstream sees the tick-grid value. Non-finite or
    /// non-positive prices are left for `validate()` to reject.
    pub fn limit(
        symbol: impl Into<String>,
        side: Side,
        qty: f64,
        price: f64,
        tick_size: f64,
    ) -> Self {
        let snapped =
            if tick_size.is_finite() && tick_size > 0.0 && price.is_finite() && price > 0.0 {
                (price / tick_size).round() * tick_size
            } else {
                price
            };
        Self {
            symbol: symbol.into(),
            side,
            qty,
            order_type: OrderType::Limit,
            limit_price: Some(snapped),
            time_in_force: TimeInForce::Gtc,
            reduce_only: false,
        }
    }

    /// Backwards-compatible constructor without a tick grid: prefers the
    /// canonical BTCUSDT cent tick (0.01). Prefer `limit(.., tick_size)`.
    pub fn limit_tick(symbol: impl Into<String>, side: Side, qty: f64, price: f64) -> Self {
        Self::limit(symbol, side, qty, price, 0.01)
    }

    /// Mark the request as an exit: the venue must not flip the position.
    pub fn reducing(mut self) -> Self {
        self.reduce_only = true;
        self
    }

    /// The notional, when a price is known (limits only; a market order is
    /// priced by the venue at fill time).
    pub fn notional(&self) -> Option<f64> {
        self.limit_price.map(|p| p * self.qty)
    }

    /// Reject a malformed request before it ever reaches a venue.
    ///
    /// Cheap, total, and venue-independent: a zero or `NaN` quantity costs a
    /// round trip and can leave a real order resting on the book.
    pub fn validate(&self) -> Result<(), OrderError> {
        if self.symbol.trim().is_empty() {
            return Err(OrderError::EmptySymbol);
        }
        if !self.qty.is_finite() || self.qty <= 0.0 {
            return Err(OrderError::NonPositiveQuantity { qty: self.qty });
        }
        match (self.order_type, self.limit_price) {
            (OrderType::Limit, None) => Err(OrderError::MissingLimitPrice),
            (OrderType::Limit, Some(price)) if !price.is_finite() || price <= 0.0 => {
                Err(OrderError::LimitPriceNotPositive { price })
            }
            _ => Ok(()),
        }
    }

    /// Venue-aware validation: as `validate()`, plus rejects a limit price
    /// that is not on the venue tick grid (within 1e-9). Call this at every
    /// order-routing boundary with the destination venue's `price_tick`, so
    /// an off-tick price fails locally instead of as INVALID_TICK_SIZE.
    pub fn validate_on_tick(&self, price_tick: Option<f64>) -> Result<(), OrderError> {
        self.validate()?;
        if self.order_type == OrderType::Limit {
            if let (Some(tick), Some(price)) = (price_tick, self.limit_price) {
                if tick.is_finite() && tick > 0.0 && price.is_finite() && price > 0.0 {
                    let snapped = (price / tick).round() * tick;
                    if (price - snapped).abs() > 1e-9 {
                        return Err(OrderError::OffTickPrice { price, tick });
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

/// Why a request is malformed. Caught locally, never by the venue.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub enum OrderError {
    EmptySymbol,
    NonPositiveQuantity {
        qty: f64,
    },
    MissingLimitPrice,
    LimitPriceNotPositive {
        price: f64,
    },
    /// Limit price is not on the venue tick grid: reject locally instead of
    /// paying a round trip for the venue's INVALID_TICK_SIZE.
    OffTickPrice {
        price: f64,
        tick: f64,
    },
}

impl fmt::Display for OrderError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        match self {
            Self::EmptySymbol => write!(f, "order symbol must not be empty"),
            Self::NonPositiveQuantity { qty } => {
                write!(f, "order quantity must be finite and positive, got {qty}")
            }
            Self::MissingLimitPrice => write!(f, "a limit order needs a price"),
            Self::LimitPriceNotPositive { price } => {
                write!(f, "limit price must be positive, got {price}")
            }
            Self::OffTickPrice { price, tick } => {
                write!(f, "limit price {price} is off the {tick} tick grid")
            }
        }
    }
}

impl std::error::Error for OrderError {}

/// An order as tracked locally, fed by the venue's [`Fill`]s.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct Order {
    pub id: OrderId,
    pub request: OrderRequest,
    pub status: OrderStatus,
    pub filled_qty: f64,
    pub avg_fill_price: Option<f64>,
    pub fee_paid: f64,
}

impl Order {
    pub fn new(id: impl Into<OrderId>, request: OrderRequest) -> Self {
        Self {
            id: id.into(),
            request,
            status: OrderStatus::Pending,
            filled_qty: 0.0,
            avg_fill_price: None,
            fee_paid: 0.0,
        }
    }

    /// Quantity still working on the book.
    pub fn remaining(&self) -> f64 {
        (self.request.qty - self.filled_qty).max(0.0)
    }

    /// Fold an execution into this order.
    ///
    /// The average fill price is a quantity-weighted mean of the executions,
    /// not of the last one: a partially filled order that completes in two
    /// prints has one meaningful entry price, and this is it.
    pub fn record_fill(&mut self, qty: f64, price: f64, fee: f64) {
        let prior_notional = self.avg_fill_price.unwrap_or(0.0) * self.filled_qty;
        self.filled_qty += qty;
        self.fee_paid += fee;
        self.avg_fill_price = if self.filled_qty > 0.0 {
            Some((prior_notional + price * qty) / self.filled_qty)
        } else {
            None
        };
        // `+ 1e-12`: a venue that rounds the final lot down must not leave the
        // order stuck at `PartiallyFilled` forever.
        self.status = if self.filled_qty + 1e-12 >= self.request.qty {
            OrderStatus::Filled
        } else {
            OrderStatus::PartiallyFilled
        };
    }
}

/// One execution report from the venue.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct Fill {
    pub order_id: OrderId,
    pub symbol: String,
    pub side: Side,
    pub qty: f64,
    pub price: f64,
    pub fee: f64,
}

impl Fill {
    pub fn new(
        order_id: impl Into<OrderId>,
        symbol: impl Into<String>,
        side: Side,
        qty: f64,
        price: f64,
        fee: f64,
    ) -> Self {
        Self {
            order_id: order_id.into(),
            symbol: symbol.into(),
            side,
            qty,
            price,
            fee,
        }
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/pnl.rs =====
//! Realized-pnl ledger: closed trades and the equity curve.
//!
//! The ledger is the only place a win is decided. Keeping that judgement here
//! means the dashboard's win rate, the Sortino ratio and the drawdown limit all
//! read the same number instead of three approximations of it.

use super::order::Side;

/// One completed round trip.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct Trade {
    pub symbol: String,
    /// Which position was opened: `Buy` = long, `Sell` = short.
    pub side: Side,
    pub entry_price: f64,
    pub exit_price: f64,
    pub qty: f64,
    /// Commission on both legs, already summed.
    pub fee: f64,
    pub entry_sequence: u64,
    pub exit_sequence: u64,
}

impl Trade {
    #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
    pub fn new(
        symbol: impl Into<String>,
        side: Side,
        entry_price: f64,
        exit_price: f64,
        qty: f64,
        fee: f64,
        entry_sequence: u64,
        exit_sequence: u64,
    ) -> Self {
        Self {
            symbol: symbol.into(),
            side,
            entry_price,
            exit_price,
            qty,
            fee,
            entry_sequence,
            exit_sequence,
        }
    }

    /// Pnl before fees.
    pub fn gross_pnl(&self) -> f64 {
        let direction = if self.side.is_buy() { 1.0 } else { -1.0 };
        (self.exit_price - self.entry_price) * self.qty * direction
    }

    /// Pnl after fees: the number a win/loss judgement must use. A trade that
    /// cleared 5 and paid 6 in commission was not a win.
    pub fn net_pnl(&self) -> f64 {
        self.gross_pnl() - self.fee
    }

    pub fn is_win(&self) -> bool {
        self.net_pnl() > 0.0
    }

    /// Return in units of the initial risk, when the stop distance is known.
    ///
    /// `risk_per_unit` is the price distance from entry to stop. `None` for a
    /// non-positive distance: an R-multiple against no risk is meaningless.
    pub fn r_multiple(&self, risk_per_unit: f64) -> Option<f64> {
        if !risk_per_unit.is_finite() || risk_per_unit <= 0.0 {
            return None;
        }
        Some(self.net_pnl() / (risk_per_unit * self.qty))
    }
}

/// Append-only record of what the account earned and how deep it drew down.
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct PnlLedger {
    starting_equity: f64,
    trades: Vec<Trade>,
    equity: Vec<f64>,
    peak: f64,
    max_drawdown: f64,
}

impl PnlLedger {
    pub fn new(starting_equity: f64) -> Self {
        Self {
            starting_equity,
            trades: Vec::new(),
            equity: vec![starting_equity],
            peak: starting_equity,
            max_drawdown: 0.0,
        }
    }

    pub fn record_trade(&mut self, trade: Trade) {
        self.trades.push(trade);
    }

    /// Append a mark-to-market equity sample and fold it into the drawdown.
    pub fn mark(&mut self, equity: f64) {
        self.equity.push(equity);
        if equity > self.peak {
            self.peak = equity;
        }
        if self.peak > 0.0 {
            let drawdown = (self.peak - equity) / self.peak;
            if drawdown > self.max_drawdown {
                self.max_drawdown = drawdown;
            }
        }
    }

    pub fn starting_equity(&self) -> f64 {
        self.starting_equity
    }

    pub fn current_equity(&self) -> f64 {
        self.equity.last().copied().unwrap_or(self.starting_equity)
    }

    pub fn trades(&self) -> &[Trade] {
        &self.trades
    }

    pub fn equity_curve(&self) -> &[f64] {
        &self.equity
    }

    pub fn realized_pnl(&self) -> f64 {
        self.trades.iter().map(Trade::net_pnl).sum()
    }

    pub fn gross_pnl(&self) -> f64 {
        self.trades.iter().map(Trade::gross_pnl).sum()
    }

    pub fn fees_paid(&self) -> f64 {
        self.trades.iter().map(|t| t.fee).sum()
    }

    /// Largest peak-to-trough fall of the equity curve, as a fraction.
    pub fn max_drawdown(&self) -> f64 {
        self.max_drawdown
    }

    pub fn wins(&self) -> usize {
        self.trades.iter().filter(|t| t.is_win()).count()
    }

    /// Strictly losing trades; a breakeven is counted as neither.
    pub fn losses(&self) -> usize {
        self.trades.iter().filter(|t| t.net_pnl() < 0.0).count()
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn long(entry: f64, exit: f64, qty: f64, fee: f64) -> Trade {
        Trade::new("BTCUSDT", Side::Buy, entry, exit, qty, fee, 1, 2)
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_long_profits_when_price_rises_and_a_short_when_it_falls() {
        assert_eq!(long(100.0, 110.0, 2.0, 0.0).gross_pnl(), 20.0);
        let short = Trade::new("BTCUSDT", Side::Sell, 100.0, 90.0, 2.0, 0.0, 1, 2);
        assert_eq!(short.gross_pnl(), 20.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn fees_turn_a_small_gross_win_into_a_loss() {
        let t = long(100.0, 101.0, 1.0, 2.0);
        assert_eq!(t.gross_pnl(), 1.0);
        assert_eq!(t.net_pnl(), -1.0);
        assert!(!t.is_win(), "cleared 1, paid 2: not a win");
    }

    #[test]
    fn r_multiple_is_measured_against_the_stop_distance() {
        let t = long(100.0, 110.0, 1.0, 0.0);
        assert_eq!(t.r_multiple(5.0), Some(2.0));
        assert_eq!(t.r_multiple(0.0), None);
        assert_eq!(t.r_multiple(f64::NAN), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_fresh_ledger_starts_at_its_starting_equity() {
        let ledger = PnlLedger::new(10_000.0);
        assert_eq!(ledger.current_equity(), 10_000.0);
        assert_eq!(ledger.equity_curve(), &[10_000.0]);
        assert_eq!(ledger.max_drawdown(), 0.0);
        assert!(ledger.trades().is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn marks_track_the_peak_and_the_worst_drawdown() {
        let mut ledger = PnlLedger::new(100.0);
        ledger.mark(120.0);
        ledger.mark(90.0); // 25% below the 120 peak
        ledger.mark(110.0); // recovering does not erase the drawdown
        assert_eq!(ledger.max_drawdown(), 0.25);
        assert_eq!(ledger.current_equity(), 110.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn trades_are_summed_net_of_fees() {
        let mut ledger = PnlLedger::new(1_000.0);
        ledger.record_trade(long(100.0, 110.0, 1.0, 1.0));
        ledger.record_trade(long(100.0, 90.0, 1.0, 1.0));
        assert_eq!(ledger.gross_pnl(), 0.0, "+10 then -10");
        assert_eq!(ledger.fees_paid(), 2.0);
        assert_eq!(ledger.realized_pnl(), -2.0);
        assert_eq!(ledger.wins(), 1);
        assert_eq!(ledger.losses(), 1);
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/portfolio.rs =====
//! Cash, margin and equity for one account.
//!
//! This is deliberately a *risk budget*, not a venue statement:
//! `connectors/<venue>` supplies the venue's own figures through
//! `AccountConnector::snapshot`, and the two are reconciled rather than
//! conflated. What is modelled here is the realized-cash convention —
//!
//! ```text
//!   cash   = starting_cash + realized pnl - fees
//!   equity = cash + unrealized pnl at the current marks
//!   margin = gross exposure / leverage
//! ```
//!
//! — which needs no cross/isolated distinction and therefore behaves the same
//! on every venue.

use std::collections::BTreeMap;

use super::order::Fill;
use super::pnl::{PnlLedger, Trade};
use super::position::PositionBook;

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct Portfolio {
    starting_cash: f64,
    leverage: f64,
    holdings: PositionBook,
    ledger: PnlLedger,
}

impl Portfolio {
    /// `starting_cash` must be finite and positive, `leverage` finite and
    /// positive. Both are refused rather than defaulted: a zero leverage would
    /// divide by zero on the first margin calculation.
    pub fn new(starting_cash: f64, leverage: f64) -> Result<Self, String> {
        if !starting_cash.is_finite() || starting_cash <= 0.0 {
            return Err(format!(
                "starting cash must be positive, got {starting_cash}"
            ));
        }
        if !leverage.is_finite() || leverage <= 0.0 {
            return Err(format!("leverage must be positive, got {leverage}"));
        }
        Ok(Self {
            starting_cash,
            leverage,
            holdings: PositionBook::new(),
            ledger: PnlLedger::new(starting_cash),
        })
    }

    pub fn starting_cash(&self) -> f64 {
        self.starting_cash
    }

    pub fn leverage(&self) -> f64 {
        self.leverage
    }

    pub fn holdings(&self) -> &PositionBook {
        &self.holdings
    }

    pub fn ledger(&self) -> &PnlLedger {
        &self.ledger
    }

    /// Realized cash balance. Unrealized pnl never touches it.
    pub fn cash(&self) -> f64 {
        self.starting_cash + self.holdings.realized_pnl() - self.holdings.fees_paid()
    }

    /// Fold a fill into the book, returning the realized pnl it produced.
    pub fn apply_fill(&mut self, fill: &Fill) -> f64 {
        self.holdings.apply(fill)
    }

    /// Bank a completed trade so win rate and Sortino see it.
    pub fn record_trade(&mut self, trade: Trade) {
        self.ledger.record_trade(trade);
    }

    pub fn unrealized_pnl(&self, marks: &BTreeMap<String, f64>) -> f64 {
        self.holdings.unrealized_pnl(marks)
    }

    pub fn gross_exposure(&self, marks: &BTreeMap<String, f64>) -> f64 {
        self.holdings.gross_exposure(marks)
    }

    pub fn net_exposure(&self, marks: &BTreeMap<String, f64>) -> f64 {
        self.holdings.net_exposure(marks)
    }

    /// Margin the book currently requires.
    pub fn margin_used(&self, marks: &BTreeMap<String, f64>) -> f64 {
        self.gross_exposure(marks) / self.leverage
    }

    pub fn equity(&self, marks: &BTreeMap<String, f64>) -> f64 {
        self.cash() + self.unrealized_pnl(marks)
    }

    /// Equity not already committed to margin.
    pub fn available_margin(&self, marks: &BTreeMap<String, f64>) -> f64 {
        self.equity(marks) - self.margin_used(marks)
    }

    /// Gross exposure as a multiple of equity.
    ///
    /// `INFINITY` when there is exposure but no equity left: a wiped account is
    /// maximally levered, not unlimitedly safe. A flat book is `0.0`.
    pub fn effective_leverage(&self, marks: &BTreeMap<String, f64>) -> f64 {
        let gross = self.gross_exposure(marks);
        if gross == 0.0 {
            return 0.0;
        }
        let equity = self.equity(marks);
        if equity <= 0.0 {
            f64::INFINITY
        } else {
            gross / equity
        }
    }

    /// Mark the account, record the equity sample, return the equity.
    pub fn mark(&mut self, marks: &BTreeMap<String, f64>) -> f64 {
        let equity = self.equity(marks);
        self.ledger.mark(equity);
        equity
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::core::order::{Fill, OrderId, Side};

    fn marks(price: f64) -> BTreeMap<String, f64> {
        let mut m = BTreeMap::new();
        m.insert("BTCUSDT".to_string(), price);
        m
    }

    fn fill(side: Side, qty: f64, price: f64) -> Fill {
        Fill::new(OrderId::new("t"), "BTCUSDT", side, qty, price, 0.0)
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_portfolio_needs_positive_cash_and_leverage() {
        assert!(Portfolio::new(0.0, 10.0).is_err());
        assert!(Portfolio::new(1_000.0, 0.0).is_err());
        assert!(Portfolio::new(f64::NAN, 10.0).is_err());
        assert!(Portfolio::new(1_000.0, 10.0).is_ok());
    }

    #[test]
    fn cash_only_moves_on_a_realized_close() {
        let mut p = Portfolio::new(1_000.0, 10.0).unwrap();
        p.apply_fill(&Fill::new(
            OrderId::new("a"),
            "BTCUSDT",
            Side::Buy,
            2.0,
            100.0,
            1.0,
        ));
        assert_eq!(p.cash(), 999.0, "starting cash less the entry fee");
        p.apply_fill(&Fill::new(
            OrderId::new("b"),
            "BTCUSDT",
            Side::Sell,
            2.0,
            120.0,
            1.0,
        ));
        assert_eq!(p.cash(), 1_038.0, "+40 realized, -2 fees");
    }

    #[test]
    fn equity_adds_unrealized_pnl_to_cash() {
        let mut p = Portfolio::new(1_000.0, 10.0).unwrap();
        p.apply_fill(&fill(Side::Buy, 1.0, 100.0));
        assert_eq!(p.equity(&marks(150.0)), 1_050.0);
        assert_eq!(p.unrealized_pnl(&marks(150.0)), 50.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn margin_and_available_margin_follow_gross_exposure() {
        let mut p = Portfolio::new(1_000.0, 10.0).unwrap();
        p.apply_fill(&fill(Side::Buy, 1.0, 100.0));
        let m = marks(100.0);
        assert_eq!(p.gross_exposure(&m), 100.0);
        assert_eq!(p.margin_used(&m), 10.0, "100 of exposure at 10x");
        assert_eq!(p.available_margin(&m), 990.0);
        assert_eq!(p.effective_leverage(&m), 0.1);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_flat_book_has_no_leverage_and_a_wiped_book_is_infinite() {
        let mut p = Portfolio::new(1_000.0, 10.0).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(p.effective_leverage(&marks(100.0)), 0.0, "nothing at risk");
        p.apply_fill(&fill(Side::Buy, 20.0, 100.0));
        // Mark collapses to 1: equity is -980 while 20 units are still exposed.
        assert_eq!(p.effective_leverage(&marks(1.0)), f64::INFINITY);
    }

    #[test]
    fn mark_records_the_equity_curve() {
        let mut p = Portfolio::new(1_000.0, 10.0).unwrap();
        p.apply_fill(&fill(Side::Buy, 1.0, 100.0));
        assert_eq!(p.mark(&marks(200.0)), 1_100.0);
        assert_eq!(p.ledger().current_equity(), 1_100.0);
        p.mark(&marks(50.0));
        assert!((p.ledger().max_drawdown() - 150.0 / 1_100.0).abs() < 1e-12);
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/position.rs =====
//! Net exposure per symbol, marked to market.
//!
//! `core::strategy::Position` is what the strategy *wants* (flat / long /
//! short). This module is what the account *has*: a signed size, an average
//! entry, and the realized pnl that got it there. Keeping the two apart is what
//! lets a decision stay a pure function while the money stays stateful.

use std::collections::BTreeMap;

use super::order::{Fill, Side};

/// Sizes below this are float dust from an exact close, not a position.
const FLAT_EPS: f64 = 1e-12;

/// Net position in one symbol.
///
/// Named `Holding` rather than `Position` on purpose: with
/// `core::strategy::Position` in scope too, one word for two different things
/// is how a sign error gets written.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Default)]
pub struct Holding {
    /// Signed size: positive long, negative short, zero flat.
    pub size: f64,
    /// Average entry price of the open size. Zero while flat.
    pub avg_entry: f64,
    /// Pnl banked by closes. Fees are tracked separately in `fees_paid`.
    pub realized_pnl: f64,
    /// Commission paid on every fill that touched this symbol.
    pub fees_paid: f64,
}

impl Holding {
    pub fn is_flat(&self) -> bool {
        self.size.abs() < FLAT_EPS
    }

    /// The side the position is held on, or `None` while flat.
    pub fn side(&self) -> Option<Side> {
        if self.is_flat() {
            None
        } else if self.size > 0.0 {
            Some(Side::Buy)
        } else {
            Some(Side::Sell)
        }
    }

    /// Paper value of the open size at `mark`.
    pub fn notional(&self, mark: f64) -> f64 {
        self.size * mark
    }

    /// Pnl if the open size were closed at `mark`.
    pub fn unrealized_pnl(&self, mark: f64) -> f64 {
        self.size * (mark - self.avg_entry)
    }

    /// Realized plus unrealized.
    pub fn equity_pnl(&self, mark: f64) -> f64 {
        self.realized_pnl + self.unrealized_pnl(mark)
    }
}

/// Fold a fill into a holding, returning the realized pnl it produced.
///
/// Three cases, and only three:
///
/// * **opening** from flat — the entry price is the fill price;
/// * **adding** to the same side — the entry becomes the weighted average, so
///   the holding keeps a single meaningful entry;
/// * **reducing or flipping** — the closed part realizes pnl against the old
///   entry; any remainder opens at the fill price.
pub fn apply_fill(holding: &mut Holding, fill: &Fill) -> f64 {
    let signed = fill.side.signed(fill.qty);
    let previous = holding.size;

    let realized = if previous.abs() < FLAT_EPS {
        holding.avg_entry = fill.price;
        0.0
    } else if previous.signum() == signed.signum() {
        let weight = previous.abs() + signed.abs();
        holding.avg_entry =
            (holding.avg_entry * previous.abs() + fill.price * signed.abs()) / weight;
        0.0
    } else {
        let closed = signed.abs().min(previous.abs());
        // `previous.signum()` collapses the long and short cases into one line.
        let pnl = closed * (fill.price - holding.avg_entry) * previous.signum();
        if signed.abs() > previous.abs() {
            // Flipped through flat: the leftover is a new position at this price.
            holding.avg_entry = fill.price;
        }
        pnl
    };

    holding.size = previous + signed;
    if holding.size.abs() < FLAT_EPS {
        // Snap the dust away so `is_flat` is exact and a later open starts clean.
        holding.size = 0.0;
        holding.avg_entry = 0.0;
    }
    holding.realized_pnl += realized;
    holding.fees_paid += fill.fee;
    realized
}

/// Every symbol's holding, keyed by the venue's symbol spelling.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
pub struct PositionBook {
    holdings: BTreeMap<String, Holding>,
}

impl PositionBook {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            holdings: BTreeMap::new(),
        }
    }

    /// A copy of the holding, flat when the symbol has never traded.
    ///
    /// Returns a value rather than an `Option` so callers that only need a size
    /// never have to special-case "no row yet".
    pub fn holding(&self, symbol: &str) -> Holding {
        self.holdings.get(symbol).copied().unwrap_or_default()
    }

    pub fn get(&self, symbol: &str) -> Option<&Holding> {
        self.holdings.get(symbol)
    }

    /// Apply a fill under its own symbol, returning the realized pnl.
    pub fn apply(&mut self, fill: &Fill) -> f64 {
        let entry = self.holdings.entry(fill.symbol.clone()).or_default();
        apply_fill(entry, fill)
    }

    /// Symbols with a non-flat holding, in sorted order.
    pub fn open_holdings(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&String, &Holding)> {
        self.holdings.iter().filter(|(_, h)| !h.is_flat())
    }

    pub fn symbols(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &String> {
        self.holdings.keys()
    }

    pub fn realized_pnl(&self) -> f64 {
        self.holdings.values().map(|h| h.realized_pnl).sum()
    }

    pub fn fees_paid(&self) -> f64 {
        self.holdings.values().map(|h| h.fees_paid).sum()
    }

    /// The mark for `symbol`, falling back to its entry price.
    ///
    /// An unmarked symbol therefore contributes zero unrealized pnl instead of
    /// a fabricated one: a stale mark is worse than an admitted gap.
    fn mark(marks: &BTreeMap<String, f64>, symbol: &str, holding: &Holding) -> f64 {
        marks.get(symbol).copied().unwrap_or(holding.avg_entry)
    }

    pub fn unrealized_pnl(&self, marks: &BTreeMap<String, f64>) -> f64 {
        self.holdings
            .iter()
            .map(|(symbol, holding)| holding.unrealized_pnl(Self::mark(marks, symbol, holding)))
            .sum()
    }

    /// Sum of absolute notionals: what a margin model actually sizes against.
    pub fn gross_exposure(&self, marks: &BTreeMap<String, f64>) -> f64 {
        self.holdings
            .iter()
            .map(|(symbol, holding)| holding.notional(Self::mark(marks, symbol, holding)).abs())
            .sum()
    }

    /// Signed notionals: net directional bias, which can sit near zero while
    /// gross exposure is large.
    pub fn net_exposure(&self, marks: &BTreeMap<String, f64>) -> f64 {
        self.holdings
            .iter()
            .map(|(symbol, holding)| holding.notional(Self::mark(marks, symbol, holding)))
            .sum()
    }

    pub fn is_flat(&self) -> bool {
        self.holdings.values().all(Holding::is_flat)
    }

    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
        self.holdings.len()
    }

    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.holdings.is_empty()
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::core::order::OrderId;

    fn fill(side: Side, qty: f64, price: f64, fee: f64) -> Fill {
        Fill::new(OrderId::new("t"), "BTCUSDT", side, qty, price, fee)
    }

    fn marks(symbol: &str, price: f64) -> BTreeMap<String, f64> {
        let mut m = BTreeMap::new();
        m.insert(symbol.to_string(), price);
        m
    }

    #[test]
    fn opening_from_flat_sets_the_entry_price() {
        let mut book = PositionBook::new();
        assert_eq!(book.apply(&fill(Side::Buy, 1.0, 100.0, 0.5)), 0.0);
        let h = book.holding("BTCUSDT");
        assert_eq!(h.size, 1.0);
        assert_eq!(h.avg_entry, 100.0);
        assert_eq!(h.realized_pnl, 0.0);
        assert_eq!(h.fees_paid, 0.5);
        assert_eq!(h.side(), Some(Side::Buy));
    }

    #[test]
    fn adding_to_the_same_side_weight_averages_the_entry() {
        let mut book = PositionBook::new();
        book.apply(&fill(Side::Buy, 1.0, 100.0, 0.0));
        book.apply(&fill(Side::Buy, 1.0, 110.0, 0.0));
        assert_eq!(book.holding("BTCUSDT").avg_entry, 105.0);
        assert_eq!(book.holding("BTCUSDT").realized_pnl, 0.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn closing_a_long_realizes_against_the_entry() {
        let mut book = PositionBook::new();
        book.apply(&fill(Side::Buy, 1.0, 100.0, 0.0));
        let realized = book.apply(&fill(Side::Sell, 1.0, 120.0, 0.0));
        assert_eq!(realized, 20.0);
        let h = book.holding("BTCUSDT");
        assert!(h.is_flat());
        assert_eq!(h.avg_entry, 0.0, "a flat holding carries no entry");
        assert_eq!(h.realized_pnl, 20.0);
        assert!(book.is_flat());
    }

    #[test]
    fn closing_a_short_profits_when_price_falls() {
        let mut book = PositionBook::new();
        book.apply(&fill(Side::Sell, 1.0, 100.0, 0.0));
        assert_eq!(book.holding("BTCUSDT").size, -1.0);
        let realized = book.apply(&fill(Side::Buy, 1.0, 90.0, 0.0));
        assert_eq!(realized, 10.0);
        assert!(book.holding("BTCUSDT").is_flat());
    }

    #[test]
    fn flipping_through_flat_reopens_at_the_fill_price() {
        let mut book = PositionBook::new();
        book.apply(&fill(Side::Buy, 1.0, 100.0, 0.0));
        let realized = book.apply(&fill(Side::Sell, 3.0, 110.0, 0.0));
        assert_eq!(realized, 10.0, "only the 1.0 that existed was closed");
        let h = book.holding("BTCUSDT");
        assert_eq!(h.size, -2.0);
        assert_eq!(h.avg_entry, 110.0, "the leftover is a fresh short");
    }

    #[test]
    fn unrealized_and_exposure_use_the_mark() {
        let mut book = PositionBook::new();
        book.apply(&fill(Side::Buy, 2.0, 100.0, 0.0));
        let m = marks("BTCUSDT", 130.0);
        assert_eq!(book.unrealized_pnl(&m), 60.0);
        assert_eq!(book.gross_exposure(&m), 260.0);
        assert_eq!(book.net_exposure(&m), 260.0);
        // Without a mark the position contributes nothing rather than a guess.
        assert_eq!(book.unrealized_pnl(&BTreeMap::new()), 0.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn open_holdings_excludes_flat_symbols() {
        let mut book = PositionBook::new();
        book.apply(&fill(Side::Buy, 1.0, 100.0, 0.0));
        let mut eth = fill(Side::Buy, 1.0, 10.0, 0.0);
        eth.symbol = "ETHUSDT".to_string();
        book.apply(&eth);
        let mut close_eth = fill(Side::Sell, 1.0, 10.0, 0.0);
        close_eth.symbol = "ETHUSDT".to_string();
        book.apply(&close_eth);
        let open: Vec<&String> = book.open_holdings().map(|(s, _)| s).collect();
        assert_eq!(open, vec!["BTCUSDT"]);
        assert_eq!(book.len(), 2, "the flat row is still remembered");
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/risk.rs =====
//! Pre-trade limits — one set of guardrails for every venue.
//!
//! The check runs in `core`, before a request reaches `connectors/<venue>`, so
//! a limit can never be enforced on Binance and forgotten on Bybit. Each breach
//! is a named variant, because "order rejected" with no reason is how a live
//! system quietly stops trading.

use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::fmt;

use super::order::{Fill, OrderError, OrderId, OrderRequest};
use super::portfolio::Portfolio;

/// Ceilings a single order, and the book it would create, must respect.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct RiskLimits {
    /// Absolute size allowed in one symbol, in base units.
    pub max_position_qty: f64,
    /// Absolute notional allowed in one symbol, in quote currency.
    pub max_notional: f64,
    /// Gross exposure / equity ceiling for the whole book.
    pub max_leverage: f64,
    /// Peak-to-trough equity fall at which new risk is refused.
    pub max_drawdown_frac: f64,
}

impl Default for RiskLimits {
    /// Wide but finite. `f64::INFINITY` is indistinguishable from "no limit",
    /// and the point of this type is that it always says no *somewhere*.
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            max_position_qty: 1_000.0,
            max_notional: 1_000_000.0,
            max_leverage: 10.0,
            max_drawdown_frac: 0.5,
        }
    }
}

impl RiskLimits {
    /// Refuses non-positive or non-finite ceilings at construction, so a
    /// misconfigured limit fails at startup rather than on the first order.
    pub fn new(
        max_position_qty: f64,
        max_notional: f64,
        max_leverage: f64,
        max_drawdown_frac: f64,
    ) -> Result<Self, String> {
        for (name, value) in [
            ("max_position_qty", max_position_qty),
            ("max_notional", max_notional),
            ("max_leverage", max_leverage),
        ] {
            if !value.is_finite() || value <= 0.0 {
                return Err(format!("{name} must be finite and positive, got {value}"));
            }
        }
        if !max_drawdown_frac.is_finite() || max_drawdown_frac <= 0.0 || max_drawdown_frac > 1.0 {
            return Err(format!(
                "max_drawdown_frac must be in (0, 1], got {max_drawdown_frac}"
            ));
        }
        Ok(Self {
            max_position_qty,
            max_notional,
            max_leverage,
            max_drawdown_frac,
        })
    }
}

/// Why an order was refused.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub enum RiskBreach {
    /// The request itself was malformed; carries the underlying reason.
    Malformed(OrderError),
    /// No mark for this symbol, so the notional cannot be judged.
    UnknownMark {
        symbol: String,
    },
    PositionLimit {
        resulting: f64,
        limit: f64,
    },
    NotionalLimit {
        resulting: f64,
        limit: f64,
    },
    LeverageLimit {
        resulting: f64,
        limit: f64,
    },
    DrawdownLimit {
        current: f64,
        limit: f64,
    },
}

impl fmt::Display for RiskBreach {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        match self {
            Self::Malformed(e) => write!(f, "malformed order: {e}"),
            Self::UnknownMark { symbol } => {
                write!(f, "no mark price for {symbol}, refusing to size the order")
            }
            Self::PositionLimit { resulting, limit } => {
                write!(f, "order would leave {resulting} units, limit {limit}")
            }
            Self::NotionalLimit { resulting, limit } => {
                write!(f, "notional {resulting:.2} over limit {limit:.2}")
            }
            Self::LeverageLimit { resulting, limit } => {
                write!(f, "leverage {resulting:.2}x over limit {limit:.2}x")
            }
            Self::DrawdownLimit { current, limit } => {
                write!(f, "drawdown {current:.4} past limit {limit:.4}")
            }
        }
    }
}

impl std::error::Error for RiskBreach {}

/// Check `request` against `limits` and the book it would create.
///
/// Check order matters: a malformed request and a blown account are both
/// refused before anything is sized, because neither has a meaningful resulting
/// size to compare against a ceiling.
pub fn check(
    limits: &RiskLimits,
    portfolio: &Portfolio,
    marks: &BTreeMap<String, f64>,
    request: &OrderRequest,
) -> Result<(), RiskBreach> {
    request.validate().map_err(RiskBreach::Malformed)?;

    let drawdown = portfolio.ledger().max_drawdown();
    if drawdown > limits.max_drawdown_frac {
        return Err(RiskBreach::DrawdownLimit {
            current: drawdown,
            limit: limits.max_drawdown_frac,
        });
    }

    let mark = *marks
        .get(&request.symbol)
        .ok_or_else(|| RiskBreach::UnknownMark {
            symbol: request.symbol.clone(),
        })?;

    let holding = portfolio.holdings().holding(&request.symbol);
    let resulting_size = holding.size + request.side.signed(request.qty);
    if resulting_size.abs() > limits.max_position_qty {
        return Err(RiskBreach::PositionLimit {
            resulting: resulting_size.abs(),
            limit: limits.max_position_qty,
        });
    }
    let resulting_notional = resulting_size.abs() * mark;
    if resulting_notional > limits.max_notional {
        return Err(RiskBreach::NotionalLimit {
            resulting: resulting_notional,
            limit: limits.max_notional,
        });
    }

    // Leverage is a property of the whole book *after* the fill, not of this
    // order alone, so the check runs on a throwaway copy filled at the mark.
    let mut probe = portfolio.clone();
    probe.apply_fill(&Fill::new(
        OrderId::new("risk-probe"),
        request.symbol.clone(),
        request.side,
        request.qty,
        mark,
        0.0,
    ));
    let leverage = probe.effective_leverage(marks);
    if leverage > limits.max_leverage {
        return Err(RiskBreach::LeverageLimit {
            resulting: leverage,
            limit: limits.max_leverage,
        });
    }
    Ok(())
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/stats.rs =====
//! Performance statistics: what the dashboard renders at the front.
//!
//! Pure functions over the equity curve and the trade list, so every number on
//! screen is recomputable from the log — and therefore assertable in a test
//! instead of taken on faith.

use super::pnl::{PnlLedger, Trade};

/// The headline numbers.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Default)]
pub struct PerformanceStats {
    /// Annualised Sortino ratio (downside deviation only).
    pub sortino: f64,
    /// Winning trades / all trades, in `[0, 1]`. Zero when there are none.
    pub win_rate: f64,
    /// `(ending - starting) / starting`.
    pub cumulative_return: f64,
    /// Largest peak-to-trough fall, as a fraction.
    pub max_drawdown: f64,
    pub trades: usize,
    pub wins: usize,
    pub losses: usize,
}

impl PerformanceStats {
    /// Everything the GUI shows, computed from one ledger.
    ///
    /// `periods_per_year` annualises the ratio; pass `1.0` for an
    /// un-annualised figure, which is what a short intraday session wants.
    pub fn from_ledger(ledger: &PnlLedger, periods_per_year: f64) -> Self {
        let equity = ledger.equity_curve();
        let returns = returns_from_equity(equity);
        let ending = equity
            .last()
            .copied()
            .unwrap_or_else(|| ledger.starting_equity());
        Self {
            sortino: sortino(&returns, 0.0, periods_per_year),
            win_rate: win_rate(ledger.trades()),
            cumulative_return: cumulative_return(ledger.starting_equity(), ending),
            max_drawdown: ledger.max_drawdown(),
            trades: ledger.trades().len(),
            wins: ledger.wins(),
            losses: ledger.losses(),
        }
    }
}

/// Period-over-period simple returns from an equity curve.
///
/// A non-positive previous equity yields no return: division by a wiped account
/// is undefined, not infinite.
pub fn returns_from_equity(equity: &[f64]) -> Vec<f64> {
    equity
        .windows(2)
        .filter_map(|w| {
            if w[0] > 0.0 {
                Some((w[1] - w[0]) / w[0])
            } else {
                None
            }
        })
        .collect()
}

/// Annualised Sortino ratio.
///
/// The denominator is the *downside* deviation over **all** periods, not only
/// the losing ones. That is the standard definition, and the reason a strategy
/// with one very large loss scores worse here than on a Sharpe ratio.
pub fn sortino(returns: &[f64], target: f64, periods_per_year: f64) -> f64 {
    if returns.is_empty() {
        return 0.0;
    }
    let n = returns.len() as f64;
    let excess_sum: f64 = returns.iter().map(|r| r - target).sum();
    let mean = excess_sum / n;
    let downside_sq: f64 = returns
        .iter()
        .map(|r| {
            let e = r - target;
            if e < 0.0 { e * e } else { 0.0 }
        })
        .sum();
    let downside = (downside_sq / n).sqrt();
    if downside > 0.0 {
        let annualise = if periods_per_year > 0.0 {
            periods_per_year.sqrt()
        } else {
            1.0
        };
        (mean / downside) * annualise
    } else if mean > 0.0 {
        // No downside at all: infinite ratio, which the JSON writer renders as
        // `null` so the dashboard shows "--" rather than a fake number.
        f64::INFINITY
    } else {
        0.0
    }
}

/// Winning trades / all trades. A breakeven counts as neither.
pub fn win_rate(trades: &[Trade]) -> f64 {
    if trades.is_empty() {
        return 0.0;
    }
    trades.iter().filter(|t| t.is_win()).count() as f64 / trades.len() as f64
}

pub fn cumulative_return(starting_equity: f64, ending_equity: f64) -> f64 {
    if starting_equity <= 0.0 {
        return 0.0;
    }
    (ending_equity - starting_equity) / starting_equity
}

/// Largest peak-to-trough fall as a fraction, from an equity curve alone.
///
/// Exposed separately from the ledger so a curve pulled out of a file can be
/// scored without replaying it through the ledger.
pub fn max_drawdown(equity: &[f64]) -> f64 {
    let mut peak = f64::NEG_INFINITY;
    let mut worst: f64 = 0.0;
    for &value in equity {
        if value > peak {
            peak = value;
        }
        if peak > 0.0 {
            let drawdown = (peak - value) / peak;
            if drawdown > worst {
                worst = drawdown;
            }
        }
    }
    worst
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::core::order::Side;

    fn trade(entry: f64, exit: f64) -> Trade {
        Trade::new("BTCUSDT", Side::Buy, entry, exit, 1.0, 0.0, 1, 2)
    }

    #[test]
    fn equity_returns_skip_a_wiped_step() {
        assert_eq!(returns_from_equity(&[100.0, 110.0, 99.0]), vec![0.1, -0.1]);
        assert_eq!(returns_from_equity(&[0.0, 5.0]), Vec::<f64>::new());
        assert!(returns_from_equity(&[]).is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn sortino_penalises_only_downside_volatility() {
        // 10% up then 10% down: mean excess is zero, so the ratio is zero.
        assert_eq!(sortino(&[0.1, -0.1], 0.0, 1.0), 0.0);
        // Up only: there is no downside deviation at all.
        assert_eq!(sortino(&[0.1, 0.1], 0.0, 1.0), f64::INFINITY);
        assert!(sortino(&[-0.1, -0.1], 0.0, 1.0) < 0.0);
        assert_eq!(sortino(&[], 0.0, 1.0), 0.0, "no data is zero, not infinite");
    }

    #[test]
    fn sortino_annualises_by_the_square_root_of_the_period_count() {
        let unannualised = sortino(&[0.02, -0.01], 0.0, 1.0);
        let annualised = sortino(&[0.02, -0.01], 0.0, 252.0);
        assert!((annualised - unannualised * 252.0_f64.sqrt()).abs() < 1e-12);
    }

    #[test]
    fn win_rate_ignores_breakevens_in_the_numerator_only() {
        assert_eq!(win_rate(&[]), 0.0);
        let trades = vec![trade(100.0, 110.0), trade(100.0, 90.0), trade(100.0, 100.0)];
        assert!((win_rate(&trades) - 1.0 / 3.0).abs() < 1e-12);
    }

    #[test]
    fn cumulative_return_is_a_fraction_of_the_starting_equity() {
        assert_eq!(cumulative_return(1_000.0, 1_250.0), 0.25);
        assert_eq!(cumulative_return(1_000.0, 750.0), -0.25);
        assert_eq!(
            cumulative_return(0.0, 100.0),
            0.0,
            "no starting equity, no percentage"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn max_drawdown_finds_the_worst_peak_to_trough_fall() {
        assert_eq!(max_drawdown(&[100.0, 120.0, 90.0, 110.0]), 0.25);
        assert_eq!(max_drawdown(&[100.0, 110.0, 120.0]), 0.0, "monotonic rise");
        assert_eq!(max_drawdown(&[]), 0.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn stats_are_recomputable_from_the_ledger() {
        let mut ledger = PnlLedger::new(1_000.0);
        ledger.mark(1_100.0);
        ledger.mark(990.0);
        ledger.record_trade(trade(100.0, 110.0));
        let stats = PerformanceStats::from_ledger(&ledger, 1.0);
        assert_eq!(stats.trades, 1);
        assert_eq!(stats.wins, 1);
        assert_eq!(stats.losses, 0);
        assert_eq!(stats.win_rate, 1.0);
        assert!((stats.cumulative_return - (-0.01)).abs() < 1e-12);
        assert!((stats.max_drawdown - 0.1).abs() < 1e-12, "110 of 1100");
        assert!(stats.sortino.is_finite());
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/strategy/decision.rs =====
//! Feature-driven decision rule.
//!
//! The rule is intentionally a *pure function of the current feature row*: it
//! reads no clock, holds no position, and has no I/O. Position and sequencing
//! live in [`super::MarketState`], so a rule can be unit-tested by handing it a
//! row and asserting one [`Decision`] — which is what makes strategy research
//! possible at all.

use super::MarketState;
use super::state::Position;

/// What the engine wants done for this row.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Decision {
    /// No change: stay flat, or keep the current position.
    Hold,
    EnterLong,
    EnterShort,
    /// Close whatever position is open.
    Exit,
}

/// Something that turns a feature row into a [`Decision`].
pub trait DecisionEngine: Send + Sync {
    fn decide(&self, state: &MarketState) -> Decision;
}

/// Enter long above `long_above`, short below `short_below`, otherwise hold.
///
/// Refuses to act on a missing feature: a rule that cannot see its input must
/// hold, not guess. Entering a position on absent data is how a backtest ends
/// up looking excellent and a live account ends up flat.
/// `Clone` but not `Copy`: the feature name is a `String`, and a rule that
/// silently copied a heap string per row would allocate on the hot path.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct ThresholdDecision {
    pub feature: String,
    pub long_above: f64,
    pub short_below: f64,
    /// Leave an open position when the signal reverts to neutral.
    pub exit_on_hold: bool,
}

impl ThresholdDecision {
    pub fn new(feature: impl Into<String>, long_above: f64, short_below: f64) -> Self {
        Self {
            feature: feature.into(),
            long_above,
            short_below,
            exit_on_hold: false,
        }
    }

    pub fn exiting(mut self) -> Self {
        self.exit_on_hold = true;
        self
    }
}

impl DecisionEngine for ThresholdDecision {
    fn decide(&self, state: &MarketState) -> Decision {
        let Some(value) = state.features.get(&self.feature) else {
            // Absent input, not a zero: hold rather than trade on a gap.
            return Decision::Hold;
        };
        let signal = if value > self.long_above {
            Decision::EnterLong
        } else if value < self.short_below {
            Decision::EnterShort
        } else if self.exit_on_hold {
            Decision::Exit
        } else {
            Decision::Hold
        };
        // Only an *entry* is suppressed while already positioned. An exit must
        // still get through, or `exit_on_hold` could never flatten a position.
        match (signal, state.position) {
            (Decision::EnterLong | Decision::EnterShort, Position::Flat) => signal,
            (Decision::EnterLong | Decision::EnterShort, _) => Decision::Hold,
            (Decision::Exit, Position::Flat) => Decision::Hold,
            (Decision::Exit, _) => Decision::Exit,
            (Decision::Hold, _) => Decision::Hold,
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::core::feature::FeatureRow;
    use crate::core::strategy::Position;

    fn state(value: Option<f64>, position: Position) -> MarketState {
        let mut features = FeatureRow::new();
        match value {
            Some(v) => features.insert("z", v),
            None => features.insert_missing("z"),
        };
        MarketState {
            features,
            position,
            sequence: 1,
        }
    }

    fn rule() -> ThresholdDecision {
        ThresholdDecision::new("z", 1.0, -1.0)
    }

    #[test]
    fn goes_long_above_the_upper_threshold() {
        assert_eq!(
            rule().decide(&state(Some(2.0), Position::Flat)),
            Decision::EnterLong
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn goes_short_below_the_lower_threshold() {
        assert_eq!(
            rule().decide(&state(Some(-2.0), Position::Flat)),
            Decision::EnterShort
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn holds_inside_the_band() {
        assert_eq!(
            rule().decide(&state(Some(0.5), Position::Flat)),
            Decision::Hold
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn holds_when_the_feature_is_missing() {
        // The important one: absent must never trade.
        assert_eq!(rule().decide(&state(None, Position::Flat)), Decision::Hold);
    }

    #[test]
    fn does_not_stack_entries_while_already_positioned() {
        assert_eq!(
            rule().decide(&state(Some(2.0), Position::Long)),
            Decision::Hold
        );
        assert_eq!(
            rule().decide(&state(Some(-2.0), Position::Short)),
            Decision::Hold
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn exit_is_only_issued_from_an_open_position() {
        let exiting = rule().exiting();
        assert_eq!(
            exiting.decide(&state(Some(0.0), Position::Long)),
            Decision::Exit
        );
        assert_eq!(
            exiting.decide(&state(Some(0.0), Position::Flat)),
            Decision::Hold
        );
    }
}

//! Strategy orchestration.  State construction, model choice, and decisions
//! are separate so a strategy can be replaced without touching feature code.

mod decision;
mod model;
mod reconstruct;
mod registry;
mod state;

pub use decision::{Decision, DecisionEngine, ThresholdDecision};
pub use model::{FixedModel, ModelId, ModelSelector, RotatingModel};
pub use reconstruct::{Bar, ReconstructError, Reconstructor};
pub use registry::{StrategyRegistry, select_model};
pub use state::{MarketState, Position};


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/strategy/model.rs =====
//! Model identity and selection.
//!
//! Selection is a separate trait from the decision rule so a strategy can be
//! pointed at a different model without the rule knowing: a model id is data,
//! and swapping it must not require editing the trading logic.

/// Identifies a trained model by name.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub struct ModelId(pub String);

impl ModelId {
    pub fn new(name: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self(name.into())
    }

    pub fn as_str(&self) -> &str {
        &self.0
    }
}

impl std::fmt::Display for ModelId {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(&self.0)
    }
}

/// Chooses which model serves a given (symbol, sequence) pair.
pub trait ModelSelector: Send + Sync {
    fn select(&self, symbol: &str, sequence: u64) -> ModelId;
}

/// Always returns the same model. The simple, predictable default.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct FixedModel(pub ModelId);

impl FixedModel {
    pub fn new(id: impl Into<ModelId>) -> Self {
        Self(id.into())
    }
}

impl ModelSelector for FixedModel {
    fn select(&self, _symbol: &str, _sequence: u64) -> ModelId {
        self.0.clone()
    }
}

/// Rotates models on a fixed cadence, e.g. to average several models' signals.
///
/// Rounds the sequence down to the cadence so the choice depends only on the
/// step, never on a clock: a backtest replaying the same rows then selects the
/// same models as the live run that produced them.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct RotatingModel {
    models: Vec<ModelId>,
    cadence: u64,
}

impl RotatingModel {
    /// Refuses an empty model list or a zero cadence, both of which would make
    /// `select` panic or silently divide by zero at the worst possible moment.
    pub fn new(models: Vec<ModelId>, cadence: u64) -> Result<Self, String> {
        if models.is_empty() {
            return Err("RotatingModel needs at least one model".to_string());
        }
        if cadence == 0 {
            return Err("RotatingModel cadence must be positive".to_string());
        }
        Ok(Self { models, cadence })
    }

    pub fn models(&self) -> &[ModelId] {
        &self.models
    }
}

impl ModelSelector for RotatingModel {
    fn select(&self, _symbol: &str, sequence: u64) -> ModelId {
        let step = (sequence / self.cadence) as usize % self.models.len();
        self.models[step].clone()
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn fixed_model_always_returns_itself() {
        let selector = FixedModel::new(ModelId::new("m1"));
        assert_eq!(selector.select("BTCUSDT", 0), ModelId::new("m1"));
        assert_eq!(selector.select("BTCUSDT", 999), ModelId::new("m1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn rotation_depends_only_on_the_step_not_a_clock() {
        let selector =
            RotatingModel::new(vec![ModelId::new("a"), ModelId::new("b")], 10).expect("valid");
        assert_eq!(selector.select("BTCUSDT", 0), ModelId::new("a"));
        assert_eq!(selector.select("BTCUSDT", 9), ModelId::new("a"));
        assert_eq!(selector.select("BTCUSDT", 10), ModelId::new("b"));
        assert_eq!(selector.select("BTCUSDT", 20), ModelId::new("a"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn invalid_rotation_is_rejected_at_construction() {
        assert!(RotatingModel::new(Vec::new(), 10).is_err());
        assert!(RotatingModel::new(vec![ModelId::new("a")], 0).is_err());
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/strategy/reconstruct.rs =====
//! Causal bar reconstruction.
//!
//! Aggregates a stream of rows into fixed-size bars, emitting a bar only once
//! it is **complete**. This is the Rust counterpart of
//! `live.feature.reconstruct_rows` and of the compiled `reconstruct` op.
//!
//! Causality is the whole point: a bar is stamped with the timestamp of its
//! *last* constituent row, so it is only emitted after that row has been seen.
//!
//! # Why this lives in `strategy` and not `core::feature`
//!
//! `core::feature` computes a value for every row. Reconstruction changes the
//! *shape* of the data â€” many rows in, one bar out â€” which is a decision about
//! how a strategy consumes data, not about a feature's value. Keeping it
//! separate means a strategy can be fed either tick-level or bar-level data
//! without either side knowing about the other.

use crate::core::feature::{Aggregation, aggregate};

/// One reconstructed bar.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct Bar {
    /// Timestamp of the **last** row in the bar, i.e. the bar close time.
    pub timestamp_ms: i64,
    /// Number of rows that made up the bar.
    pub row_count: usize,
    /// The aggregated value.
    pub value: f64,
}

/// Why reconstruction could not be set up or driven.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub enum ReconstructError {
    /// A bar size of zero would never complete.
    ZeroBarSize,
    /// Timestamps went backwards, so bar boundaries would be meaningless.
    NonMonotonicTimestamp { previous: i64, current: i64 },
}

impl std::fmt::Display for ReconstructError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        match self {
            Self::ZeroBarSize => write!(f, "bar size must be positive"),
            Self::NonMonotonicTimestamp { previous, current } => write!(
                f,
                "timestamps must be non-decreasing: {current} follows {previous}"
            ),
        }
    }
}

impl std::error::Error for ReconstructError {}

/// Streaming bar builder.
///
/// Feed rows with [`Reconstructor::push`]; it returns a `Bar` whenever one
/// completes and `None` while the current bar is still filling. The trailing
/// partial bar is never emitted, matching Python â€” it could still change, so
/// treating it as closed would be a look-ahead bug.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct Reconstructor {
    size: usize,
    aggregation: Aggregation,
    buffer: Vec<f64>,
    last_timestamp: Option<i64>,
}

impl Reconstructor {
    /// Build a reconstructor for bars of `size` rows.
    pub fn new(size: usize, aggregation: Aggregation) -> Result<Self, ReconstructError> {
        if size == 0 {
            return Err(ReconstructError::ZeroBarSize);
        }
        Ok(Self {
            size,
            aggregation,
            buffer: Vec::with_capacity(size),
            last_timestamp: None,
        })
    }

    pub fn size(&self) -> usize {
        self.size
    }

    /// Rows accumulated into the bar currently being built.
    pub fn pending(&self) -> usize {
        self.buffer.len()
