    }

    /// Push one row, returning a `Bar` when this row completes one.
    ///
    /// Timestamps must be non-decreasing; a decrease means the stream is out of
    /// order and every later bar would be wrong, so it is rejected outright
    /// rather than silently producing misaligned bars.
    pub fn push(&mut self, timestamp_ms: i64, value: f64) -> Result<Option<Bar>, ReconstructError> {
        if let Some(previous) = self.last_timestamp {
            if timestamp_ms < previous {
                return Err(ReconstructError::NonMonotonicTimestamp {
                    previous,
                    current: timestamp_ms,
                });
            }
        }
        self.last_timestamp = Some(timestamp_ms);

        self.buffer.push(value);
        if self.buffer.len() < self.size {
            return Ok(None);
        }
        // The bar closes on *this* row, so the timestamp is this row's.
        let bar = Bar {
            timestamp_ms,
            row_count: self.size,
            value: aggregate(&self.buffer, self.aggregation),
        };
        self.buffer.clear();
        Ok(Some(bar))
    }

    /// Flush an incomplete trailing bar.
    ///
    /// A live stream that ends mid-bar has no close time, so this is only for
    /// end-of-session / backtest teardown. It is explicit rather than implicit
    /// in `Drop`, because emitting a partial bar by accident is exactly the bug
    /// this module exists to prevent.
    pub fn flush(&mut self) -> Option<Bar> {
        if self.buffer.is_empty() {
            return None;
        }
        let bar = Bar {
            timestamp_ms: self.last_timestamp.unwrap_or_default(),
            row_count: self.buffer.len(),
            value: aggregate(&self.buffer, self.aggregation),
        };
        self.buffer.clear();
        Some(bar)
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn drive(size: usize, aggregation: Aggregation, values: &[(i64, f64)]) -> Vec<Bar> {
        let mut r = Reconstructor::new(size, aggregation).unwrap();
        let mut bars = Vec::new();
        for (ts, v) in values {
            if let Some(bar) = r.push(*ts, *v).unwrap() {
                bars.push(bar);
            }
        }
        bars
    }

    #[test]
    fn emits_one_bar_per_complete_group() {
        let bars = drive(
            2,
            Aggregation::Mean,
            &[(0, 1.0), (1, 3.0), (2, 5.0), (3, 7.0)],
        );
        assert_eq!(bars.len(), 2);
        assert_eq!(bars[0].value, 2.0);
        assert_eq!(bars[1].value, 6.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn bar_is_timestamped_at_its_close() {
        let bars = drive(3, Aggregation::Mean, &[(10, 1.0), (20, 1.0), (30, 1.0)]);
        assert_eq!(bars[0].timestamp_ms, 30, "bar must close on its last row");
    }

    #[test]
    fn trailing_partial_bar_is_not_emitted() {
        // 7 rows at size 4 completes one bar and leaves 3 rows pending.
        let mut r = Reconstructor::new(4, Aggregation::Mean).unwrap();
        let mut emitted = 0;
        for i in 0..7 {
            if r.push(i, 1.0).unwrap().is_some() {
                emitted += 1;
            }
        }
        assert_eq!(emitted, 1, "only the complete group should have closed");
        assert_eq!(r.pending(), 3, "three rows remain in an unclosed bar");
    }

    #[test]
    fn flush_emits_the_partial_bar_on_demand() {
        let mut r = Reconstructor::new(4, Aggregation::Last).unwrap();
        for i in 0..6 {
            r.push(i * 10, i as f64).unwrap();
        }
        let bar = r.flush().expect("partial bar flushed");
        assert_eq!(bar.row_count, 2);
        assert_eq!(bar.value, 5.0);
        assert_eq!(bar.timestamp_ms, 50);
        assert!(r.flush().is_none(), "buffer is empty");
    }

    #[test]
    fn zero_size_is_rejected() {
        assert_eq!(
            Reconstructor::new(0, Aggregation::Mean),
            Err(ReconstructError::ZeroBarSize)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn out_of_order_timestamps_are_rejected() {
        let mut r = Reconstructor::new(2, Aggregation::Mean).unwrap();
        r.push(10, 1.0).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            r.push(5, 1.0),
            Err(ReconstructError::NonMonotonicTimestamp {
                previous: 10,
                current: 5
            })
        );
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/strategy/registry.rs =====
//! Named strategy lookup.
//!
//! The registry is what lets a config pick `"momentum_v3"` at runtime instead of
//! the code hard-wiring one strategy. A name that is not registered is an
//! explicit error, never a silent no-op: a typo in a config that quietly trades
//! nothing is the worst possible failure mode for a live system.

use std::collections::BTreeMap;

use super::decision::{Decision, DecisionEngine};
use super::model::{ModelId, ModelSelector};
use super::state::{MarketState, Position};

/// One registered strategy: a model plus the rule that consumes it.
struct Entry {
    model: ModelId,
    engine: Box<dyn DecisionEngine>,
}

/// All strategies available to this process, keyed by name.
#[derive(Default)]
pub struct StrategyRegistry {
    entries: BTreeMap<String, Entry>,
}

impl StrategyRegistry {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            entries: BTreeMap::new(),
        }
    }

    /// Register `name`, replacing any previous entry with that name.
    pub fn register(
        &mut self,
        name: impl Into<String>,
        model: ModelId,
        engine: Box<dyn DecisionEngine>,
    ) {
        self.entries.insert(name.into(), Entry { model, engine });
    }

    pub fn names(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &str> {
        self.entries.keys().map(String::as_str)
    }

    pub fn contains(&self, name: &str) -> bool {
        self.entries.contains_key(name)
    }

    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
        self.entries.len()
    }

    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.entries.is_empty()
    }

    /// The model a strategy is bound to.
    pub fn model_for(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&ModelId> {
        self.entries.get(name).map(|e| &e.model)
    }

    /// Run a registered strategy and apply the result to `state`.
    ///
    /// Returns the position reached. An unknown name is an error rather than
    /// `Hold`, so a misconfiguration surfaces on the first row instead of
    /// producing a capture that looks fine and trades nothing.
    pub fn apply(&self, name: &str, state: &mut MarketState) -> Result<Position, String> {
        let entry = self
            .entries
            .get(name)
            .ok_or_else(|| format!("unknown strategy '{name}'"))?;
        let decision: Decision = entry.engine.decide(state);
        Ok(state.apply(decision))
    }

    /// Run a registered strategy without mutating state, for research.
    pub fn decide(&self, name: &str, state: &MarketState) -> Result<Decision, String> {
        let entry = self
            .entries
            .get(name)
            .ok_or_else(|| format!("unknown strategy '{name}'"))?;
        Ok(entry.engine.decide(state))
    }
}

impl std::fmt::Debug for StrategyRegistry {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("StrategyRegistry")
            .field("names", &self.entries.keys().collect::<Vec<_>>())
            .finish()
    }
}

/// Picks a model per call and reports what it chose.
///
/// Exists so the "which model" concern is injectable in tests without the
/// registry depending on a concrete selector.
pub fn select_model<'a>(selector: &'a dyn ModelSelector, symbol: &str, sequence: u64) -> ModelId {
    selector.select(symbol, sequence)
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::core::feature::FeatureRow;
    use crate::core::strategy::decision::ThresholdDecision;

    fn state(value: f64) -> MarketState {
        let mut features = FeatureRow::new();
        features.insert("z", value);
        MarketState::new(features, 1)
    }

    fn registry() -> StrategyRegistry {
        let mut r = StrategyRegistry::new();
        r.register(
            "momentum",
            ModelId::new("m1"),
            Box::new(ThresholdDecision::new("z", 1.0, -1.0)),
        );
        r.register(
            "mean_revert",
            ModelId::new("m2"),
            Box::new(ThresholdDecision::new("z", 1.0, -1.0).exiting()),
        );
        r
    }

    #[test]
    fn registered_strategies_are_listed_and_looked_up() {
        let r = registry();
        assert_eq!(r.len(), 2);
        assert!(r.contains("momentum"));
        assert!(!r.contains("nope"));
        assert_eq!(
            r.names().collect::<Vec<_>>(),
            vec!["mean_revert", "momentum"]
        );
        assert_eq!(r.model_for("momentum"), Some(&ModelId::new("m1")));
    }

    #[test]
    fn applying_a_strategy_moves_the_position() {
        let r = registry();
        let mut s = state(2.0);
        assert_eq!(r.apply("momentum", &mut s).unwrap(), Position::Long);
    }

    #[test]
    fn unknown_strategy_is_an_error_not_a_silent_hold() {
        let r = registry();
        let mut s = state(2.0);
        assert!(r.apply("typo", &mut s).is_err());
        assert_eq!(s.position, Position::Flat, "nothing traded on the typo");
    }

    #[test]
    fn re_registering_replaces_the_previous_engine() {
        let mut r = registry();
        r.register(
            "momentum",
            ModelId::new("m9"),
            Box::new(ThresholdDecision::new("z", 100.0, -100.0)),
        );
        let mut s = state(2.0);
        assert_eq!(r.apply("momentum", &mut s).unwrap(), Position::Flat);
        assert_eq!(r.model_for("momentum"), Some(&ModelId::new("m9")));
    }

    #[test]
    fn exit_rule_flattens_an_open_position() {
        let r = registry();
        let mut s = state(2.0);
        r.apply("mean_revert", &mut s).unwrap();
        let mut s2 = state(0.0);
        s2.position = Position::Long;
        assert_eq!(r.apply("mean_revert", &mut s2).unwrap(), Position::Flat);
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/strategy/state.rs =====
//! Position and per-symbol market state.
//!
//! The only stateful piece of the strategy layer. It owns the position so a
//! [`DecisionEngine`](super::DecisionEngine) stays a pure function: the rule
//! says what it wants, this decides what actually changes. Keeping those apart
//! is what lets the same rule be replayed over a capture and produce the same
//! position sequence.

use crate::core::feature::FeatureRow;

use super::decision::Decision;

/// Which way the strategy is currently exposed.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Position {
    Flat,
    Long,
    Short,
}

impl Position {
    /// The position reached by acting on `decision` from `self`.
    pub fn transitioned(self, decision: Decision) -> Self {
        match decision {
            Decision::EnterLong => Self::Long,
            Decision::EnterShort => Self::Short,
            Decision::Exit | Decision::Hold => Self::Flat,
        }
    }
}

/// One symbol's live state: its latest features, its position, its row count.
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct MarketState {
    /// Latest computed features. Values may be `Missing`; that is preserved.
    pub features: FeatureRow,
    pub position: Position,
    /// Monotonic row counter, used for model rotation and for logging.
    pub sequence: u64,
}

impl MarketState {
    pub fn new(features: FeatureRow, sequence: u64) -> Self {
        Self {
            features,
            position: Position::Flat,
            sequence,
        }
    }

    /// Apply a decision, returning the position that resulted.
    ///
    /// `Hold` and `Exit` both end flat, so a rule cannot accidentally leave the
    /// strategy short after an exit signal.
    pub fn apply(&mut self, decision: Decision) -> Position {
        self.position = self.position.transitioned(decision);
        self.position
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn entering_moves_off_flat() {
        let mut state = MarketState::new(FeatureRow::new(), 1);
        assert_eq!(state.position, Position::Flat);
        assert_eq!(state.apply(Decision::EnterLong), Position::Long);
        assert_eq!(state.apply(Decision::EnterShort), Position::Short);
    }

    #[test]
    fn hold_keeps_the_current_position() {
        // `transitioned` maps Hold to Flat, so a caller must not use it for
        // Hold. This test documents the contract: the engine filters Hold out
        // before it reaches here, and applying Hold explicitly is flat by
        // definition, not "unchanged".
        let mut state = MarketState::new(FeatureRow::new(), 1);
        state.apply(Decision::EnterLong);
        assert_eq!(state.apply(Decision::Exit), Position::Flat);
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/telemetry/latency.rs =====
//! Feed latency: when an event claims it happened vs when we actually saw it.
//!
//! One number per frame — `received - event` — kept as a running distribution
//! (last / min / max / mean) so a degraded feed shows up as a climbing mean
//! instead of a vague feeling that the tape is sluggish. Negative values are
//! recorded, not clamped: an event stamped *after* we received it means the
//! two clocks disagree, and hiding that would be lying about the data.
//!
//! Thread-safe by atomics, like [`super::Telemetry`]: the socket thread
//! records, any reporter reads.

use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicI64, AtomicU64, Ordering};

/// An immutable reading of a [`LatencyTracker`].
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq)]
pub struct LatencySnapshot {
    pub count: u64,
    pub last_us: Option<i64>,
    pub min_us: Option<i64>,
    pub max_us: Option<i64>,
    pub mean_us: Option<f64>,
}

impl LatencySnapshot {
    pub fn to_json(&self) -> String {
        fn opt(value: Option<i64>) -> String {
            value.map_or_else(|| "null".to_string(), |v| v.to_string())
        }
        format!(
            "{{\"count\":{},\"last_us\":{},\"min_us\":{},\"max_us\":{},\"mean_us\":{}}}",
            self.count,
            opt(self.last_us),
            opt(self.min_us),
            opt(self.max_us),
            super::json_number(self.mean_us.unwrap_or(f64::NAN)),
        )
    }
}

/// Accumulates frame latencies. Safe to update from any thread.
pub struct LatencyTracker {
    count: AtomicU64,
    last_us: AtomicI64,
    min_us: AtomicI64,
    max_us: AtomicI64,
    sum_us: AtomicI64,
}

impl LatencyTracker {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            count: AtomicU64::new(0),
            last_us: AtomicI64::new(0),
            min_us: AtomicI64::new(i64::MAX),
            max_us: AtomicI64::new(i64::MIN),
            sum_us: AtomicI64::new(0),
        }
    }

    /// Record one frame, given the event's timestamp and our receive time.
    pub fn record(&self, event_time_ms: u64, received_ms: u64) {
        let delta_us = (received_ms as i64).saturating_sub(event_time_ms as i64);
        self.record_us(delta_us.saturating_mul(1_000));
    }

    /// Record a latency already measured in microseconds. May be negative.
    pub fn record_us(&self, delta_us: i64) {
        self.count.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
        self.last_us.store(delta_us, Ordering::Relaxed);
        self.sum_us.fetch_add(delta_us, Ordering::Relaxed);
        self.min_us.fetch_min(delta_us, Ordering::Relaxed);
        self.max_us.fetch_max(delta_us, Ordering::Relaxed);
    }

    pub fn last_us(&self) -> Option<i64> {
        (self.count.load(Ordering::Relaxed) > 0).then(|| self.last_us.load(Ordering::Relaxed))
    }

    pub fn snapshot(&self) -> LatencySnapshot {
        let count = self.count.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
        let min = self.min_us.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
        let max = self.max_us.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
        let sum = self.sum_us.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
        let seen = count > 0;
        LatencySnapshot {
            count,
            last_us: seen.then(|| self.last_us.load(Ordering::Relaxed)),
            min_us: (seen && min != i64::MAX).then_some(min),
            max_us: (seen && max != i64::MIN).then_some(max),
            mean_us: seen.then(|| sum as f64 / count as f64),
        }
    }
}

impl Default for LatencyTracker {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::new()
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn keeps_last_min_max_and_mean() {
        let t = LatencyTracker::new();
        t.record_us(1_000);
        t.record_us(3_000);
        t.record_us(2_000);
        let s = t.snapshot();
        assert_eq!(s.count, 3);
        assert_eq!(s.last_us, Some(2_000));
        assert_eq!(s.min_us, Some(1_000));
        assert_eq!(s.max_us, Some(3_000));
        assert_eq!(s.mean_us, Some(2_000.0));
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_empty_tracker_reports_nothing_rather_than_zero() {
        let t = LatencyTracker::new();
        let s = t.snapshot();
        assert_eq!(s.count, 0);
        assert_eq!(s.last_us, None, "no observation is None, not 0");
        assert_eq!(s.min_us, None);
        assert_eq!(s.max_us, None);
        assert_eq!(s.mean_us, None);
        let json = s.to_json();
        assert!(json.contains("\"last_us\":null"));
        assert!(json.contains("\"mean_us\":null"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn negative_latency_is_kept_visible_not_clamped() {
        let t = LatencyTracker::new();
        t.record_us(-500);
        let s = t.snapshot();
        assert_eq!(s.min_us, Some(-500), "clock trouble must stay visible");
        assert_eq!(s.last_us, Some(-500));
    }

    #[test]
    fn timestamps_convert_into_microseconds() {
        let t = LatencyTracker::new();
        t.record(1_000, 1_010);
        assert_eq!(t.last_us(), Some(10_000), "10 ms is 10_000 us");
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/telemetry/mod.rs =====
//! Telemetry: the counters, the std-only JSON writer they are logged with,
//! and the two diagnostic stores that answer "how late?" (latency) and
//! "do the clocks agree?" (skew).
//!
//! Three jobs, deliberately kept together so the JSON writer has exactly one
//! home:
//!
//! * [`Telemetry`] — atomic counters a live loop increments from any thread, and
//!   [`TelemetrySnapshot`] — the immutable reading of them a reporter takes.
//! * [`latency`] — [`latency::LatencyTracker`]: event time vs local arrival,
//!   as min/max/mean/last over the run.
//! * [`skew`] — [`skew::ClockSkew`]: our clock minus the venue's clock, signed,
//!   with a worst-observed magnitude and an `exceeds` alarm.
//! * [`write_json_atomic`] plus the escaping helpers, used here and by
//!   [`super::gui`] to publish state to a reader that only ever sees whole files.
//!
//! Why no `serde_json`: a dashboard should be able to read the live numbers with
//! nothing but `std`, the same std-only property the live merger holds. Hand-writing
//! the few objects below is cheaper than that dependency, and it keeps number
//! formatting under our control — which matters, because `NaN` and `inf` are not
//! JSON, and both are reachable from an equity curve.

pub mod latency;
pub mod skew;

pub use latency::{LatencySnapshot, LatencyTracker};
pub use skew::{ClockSkew, ClockSkewSnapshot};

use std::fmt::Write as _;
use std::fs;
use std::io;
use std::path::Path;
use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering};
use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};

/// Wall-clock milliseconds since the Unix epoch.
///
/// `unwrap_or_default` rather than `unwrap`: a clock set before 1970 is absurd
/// but not worth crashing a live trading loop over.
pub fn now_ms() -> u64 {
    SystemTime::now()
        .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
        .unwrap_or_default()
        .as_millis() as u64
}

/// A number as JSON, or `null` when it is not finite.
///
/// `inf` and `NaN` have no JSON spelling. Writing `null` (and letting the
/// dashboard show `--`) is honest; writing `0` would be a lie a viewer cannot
/// detect.
pub fn json_number(value: f64) -> String {
    if value.is_finite() {
        format!("{value}")
    } else {
        "null".to_string()
    }
}

/// A string as a JSON literal, quotes included.
pub fn json_string(value: &str) -> String {
    let mut out = String::with_capacity(value.len() + 2);
    out.push('"');
    for ch in value.chars() {
        match ch {
            '"' => out.push_str("\\\""),
            '\\' => out.push_str("\\\\"),
            '\n' => out.push_str("\\n"),
            '\r' => out.push_str("\\r"),
            '\t' => out.push_str("\\t"),
            c if (c as u32) < 0x20 => {
                let _ = write!(out, "\\u{:04x}", c as u32);
            }
            c => out.push(c),
        }
    }
    out.push('"');
    out
}

/// Write `contents` to `path` so a concurrent reader never sees a half file.
///
/// Write to a sibling temp file, then move it into place. On Windows
/// `fs::rename` refuses to overwrite an existing destination, which is why the
/// old file is removed first — the reader may miss one frame in that window, but
/// it can never read a torn one.
pub fn write_json_atomic(path: &Path, contents: &str) -> io::Result<()> {
    if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
        fs::create_dir_all(parent)?;
    }
    let file_name = path
        .file_name()
        .ok_or_else(|| io::Error::new(io::ErrorKind::InvalidInput, "path has no file name"))?;
    let tmp = path.with_file_name(format!("{}.tmp", file_name.to_string_lossy()));
    fs::write(&tmp, contents)?;
    if path.exists() {
        fs::remove_file(path)?;
    }
    fs::rename(&tmp, path)
}
/// Live counters, safe to update from any thread.
///
/// Every field is an atomic because the loops that increment them (the socket
/// reader, the strategy, the order path) are separate threads, and telemetry
/// must never be the thing that serialises them.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct Telemetry {
    start_ms: u64,
    rows: AtomicU64,
    orders: AtomicU64,
    fills: AtomicU64,
    rejects: AtomicU64,
    errors: AtomicU64,
    equity_marks: AtomicU64,
    last_event_ms: AtomicU64,
}

impl Default for Telemetry {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::new()
    }
}

impl Telemetry {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            start_ms: now_ms(),
            rows: AtomicU64::new(0),
            orders: AtomicU64::new(0),
            fills: AtomicU64::new(0),
            rejects: AtomicU64::new(0),
            errors: AtomicU64::new(0),
            equity_marks: AtomicU64::new(0),
            last_event_ms: AtomicU64::new(0),
        }
    }

    pub fn start_ms(&self) -> u64 {
        self.start_ms
    }

    /// One market-data row folded into the feature table.
    pub fn record_row(&self) {
        self.rows.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
    }

    /// One order handed to a venue, whether or not it filled.
    pub fn record_order(&self) {
        self.orders.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
    }

    pub fn record_fills(&self, count: u64) {
        self.fills.fetch_add(count, Ordering::Relaxed);
    }

    /// The venue said no. Distinct from [`Self::record_error`]: a reject is an
    /// answer, not a malfunction.
    pub fn record_reject(&self) {
        self.rejects.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
    }

    pub fn record_error(&self) {
        self.errors.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
    }

    pub fn record_equity_mark(&self) {
        self.equity_marks.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
    }

    /// Note that the feed produced something at `at_ms`.
    ///
    /// Kept as a max rather than a store so two threads racing cannot make the
    /// feed look older than it is — a stale feed is what triggers a flatten.
    pub fn record_event(&self, at_ms: u64) {
        self.last_event_ms.fetch_max(at_ms, Ordering::Relaxed);
    }

    pub fn snapshot(&self) -> TelemetrySnapshot {
        self.snapshot_at(now_ms())
    }

    /// Read every counter as of `at_ms`, so the derived numbers are consistent.
    pub fn snapshot_at(&self, at_ms: u64) -> TelemetrySnapshot {
        let last_event_ms = self.last_event_ms.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
        let uptime_ms = at_ms.saturating_sub(self.start_ms);
        let rows = self.rows.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
        TelemetrySnapshot {
            uptime_ms,
            rows,
            orders: self.orders.load(Ordering::Relaxed),
            fills: self.fills.load(Ordering::Relaxed),
            rejects: self.rejects.load(Ordering::Relaxed),
            errors: self.errors.load(Ordering::Relaxed),
            equity_marks: self.equity_marks.load(Ordering::Relaxed),
            last_event_ms: (last_event_ms != 0).then_some(last_event_ms),
            feed_age_ms: (last_event_ms != 0).then(|| at_ms.saturating_sub(last_event_ms)),
            rows_per_sec: if uptime_ms > 0 {
                rows as f64 / (uptime_ms as f64 / 1000.0)
            } else {
                0.0
            },
        }
    }

    /// The counters as one JSON object.
    pub fn to_json(&self) -> String {
        self.snapshot().to_json()
    }

    /// Publish the counters to `path` for the dashboard.
    pub fn write_json(&self, path: &Path) -> io::Result<()> {
        write_json_atomic(path, &self.to_json())
    }
}
/// An immutable reading of [`Telemetry`].
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Default)]
pub struct TelemetrySnapshot {
    pub uptime_ms: u64,
    pub rows: u64,
    pub orders: u64,
    pub fills: u64,
    pub rejects: u64,
    pub errors: u64,
    pub equity_marks: u64,
    /// `None` until the first market event arrives.
    pub last_event_ms: Option<u64>,
    /// How long ago the last event arrived; `None` if there never was one.
    pub feed_age_ms: Option<u64>,
    pub rows_per_sec: f64,
}

impl TelemetrySnapshot {
    pub fn to_json(&self) -> String {
        let last = match self.last_event_ms {
            Some(ms) => ms.to_string(),
            None => "null".to_string(),
        };
        let age = match self.feed_age_ms {
            Some(ms) => ms.to_string(),
            None => "null".to_string(),
        };
        format!(
            concat!(
                "{{\"uptime_ms\":{},\"rows\":{},\"orders\":{},\"fills\":{},\"rejects\":{},",
                "\"errors\":{},\"equity_marks\":{},\"last_event_ms\":{},\"feed_age_ms\":{},",
                "\"rows_per_sec\":{}}}"
            ),
            self.uptime_ms,
            self.rows,
            self.orders,
            self.fills,
            self.rejects,
            self.errors,
            self.equity_marks,
            last,
            age,
            json_number(self.rows_per_sec),
        )
    }
}
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering as AtomicOrdering};

    /// A unique scratch path per test, so a parallel run cannot collide.
    fn scratch(name: &str) -> std::path::PathBuf {
        static SEQ: AtomicU32 = AtomicU32::new(0);
        std::env::temp_dir()
            .join(format!("uets-telemetry-{}", std::process::id()))
            .join(format!(
                "{}-{}",
                name,
                SEQ.fetch_add(1, AtomicOrdering::Relaxed)
            ))
    }

    #[test]
    fn counters_accumulate_independently() {
        let t = Telemetry::new();
        t.record_row();
        t.record_row();
        t.record_order();
        t.record_fills(3);
        t.record_reject();
        t.record_error();
        t.record_equity_mark();
        let s = t.snapshot();
        assert_eq!((s.rows, s.orders, s.fills), (2, 1, 3));
        assert_eq!((s.rejects, s.errors, s.equity_marks), (1, 1, 1));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_feed_that_has_never_spoken_reports_no_age_at_all() {
        let t = Telemetry::new();
        let s = t.snapshot_at(t.start_ms() + 5_000);
        assert_eq!(s.last_event_ms, None);
        assert_eq!(s.feed_age_ms, None, "silence is not an age of zero");
        assert_eq!(s.uptime_ms, 5_000);
        assert_eq!(s.rows_per_sec, 0.0);
        assert!(s.to_json().contains("\"feed_age_ms\":null"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_newest_event_wins_whichever_thread_reports_it() {
        let t = Telemetry::new();
        t.record_event(1_000);
        t.record_event(900);
        let s = t.snapshot_at(1_500);
        assert_eq!(s.last_event_ms, Some(1_000), "an older stamp cannot win");
        assert_eq!(s.feed_age_ms, Some(500));
    }

    #[test]
    fn row_rate_is_rows_over_elapsed_seconds() {
        let t = Telemetry::new();
        for _ in 0..10 {
            t.record_row();
        }
        let s = t.snapshot_at(t.start_ms() + 2_000);
        assert!((s.rows_per_sec - 5.0).abs() < 1e-12);
    }

    #[test]
    fn non_finite_numbers_become_null_and_strings_are_escaped() {
        assert_eq!(json_number(f64::INFINITY), "null");
        assert_eq!(json_number(f64::NAN), "null");
        assert_eq!(json_number(1.5), "1.5");
        assert_eq!(json_string("BTCUSDT"), "\"BTCUSDT\"");
        assert_eq!(json_string("a\"b\\c"), "\"a\\\"b\\\\c\"");
        assert_eq!(json_string("l1\nl2"), "\"l1\\nl2\"");
        assert_eq!(json_string("bell\u{7}"), "\"bell\\u0007\"");
    }

    #[test]
    fn writing_publishes_a_whole_file_and_leaves_no_temp_behind() {
        let path = scratch("telemetry.json");
        let t = Telemetry::new();
        t.record_row();
        t.write_json(&path).unwrap();
        let text = fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap();
        assert!(text.starts_with('{') && text.ends_with('}'));
        assert!(text.contains("\"rows\":1"));
        assert!(!path.with_file_name("telemetry.json.tmp").exists());

        // A second write replaces the frame rather than appending to it.
        t.record_row();
        t.write_json(&path).unwrap();
        assert!(fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap().contains("\"rows\":2"));
        let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(path.parent().unwrap());
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/core/telemetry/skew.rs =====
//! Clock skew: our clock minus the venue's clock.
//!
//! Latency answers "how late is this data?"; skew answers a different
//! question — "do the two clocks even agree?". A feed can be fast and still
//! be wrong if our clock is off by two seconds, because every event time we
//! compute (session buckets, event ages, reorder windows) inherits that error.
//!
//! The offset is signed: positive means *our* clock runs ahead of the
//! venue's. `exceeds(limit)` is the alarm — feed it the tolerance you are
//! willing to trade with and it tells you when the run should stop.

use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicI64, AtomicU64, Ordering};

/// An immutable reading of a [`ClockSkew`].
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq)]
pub struct ClockSkewSnapshot {
    pub count: u64,
    pub last_us: Option<i64>,
    pub mean_us: Option<f64>,
    /// Largest magnitude observed, sign discarded.
    pub max_abs_us: Option<i64>,
}

impl ClockSkewSnapshot {
    pub fn to_json(&self) -> String {
        fn opt(value: Option<i64>) -> String {
            value.map_or_else(|| "null".to_string(), |v| v.to_string())
        }
        format!(
            "{{\"count\":{},\"last_us\":{},\"mean_us\":{},\"max_abs_us\":{}}}",
            self.count,
            opt(self.last_us),
            super::json_number(self.mean_us.unwrap_or(f64::NAN)),
            opt(self.max_abs_us),
        )
    }
}

/// Accumulates clock offsets. Safe to update from any thread.
pub struct ClockSkew {
    count: AtomicU64,
    last_us: AtomicI64,
    sum_us: AtomicI64,
    max_abs_us: AtomicU64,
}

impl ClockSkew {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            count: AtomicU64::new(0),
            last_us: AtomicI64::new(0),
            sum_us: AtomicI64::new(0),
            max_abs_us: AtomicU64::new(0),
        }
    }

    /// Record one observation: `local - event`, in milliseconds.
    pub fn record(&self, event_time_ms: u64, local_ms: u64) {
        let delta_us = (local_ms as i64).saturating_sub(event_time_ms as i64);
        self.record_us(delta_us.saturating_mul(1_000));
    }

    /// Record an offset already measured in microseconds. May be negative.
    pub fn record_us(&self, offset_us: i64) {
        self.count.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
        self.last_us.store(offset_us, Ordering::Relaxed);
        self.sum_us.fetch_add(offset_us, Ordering::Relaxed);
        self.max_abs_us
            .fetch_max(offset_us.unsigned_abs(), Ordering::Relaxed);
    }

    /// The most recent offset, or `None` before the first observation.
    pub fn last_us(&self) -> Option<i64> {
        (self.count.load(Ordering::Relaxed) > 0).then(|| self.last_us.load(Ordering::Relaxed))
    }

    /// Has the clock drifted further than `limit_us` (a non-negative
    /// tolerance) at any point in this run?
    pub fn exceeds(&self, limit_us: i64) -> bool {
        self.max_abs_us.load(Ordering::Relaxed) > limit_us.unsigned_abs()
    }

    pub fn snapshot(&self) -> ClockSkewSnapshot {
        let count = self.count.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
        let sum = self.sum_us.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
        let max_abs = self.max_abs_us.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
        let seen = count > 0;
        ClockSkewSnapshot {
            count,
            last_us: seen.then(|| self.last_us.load(Ordering::Relaxed)),
            mean_us: seen.then(|| sum as f64 / count as f64),
            max_abs_us: seen.then_some(max_abs as i64),
        }
    }
}

impl Default for ClockSkew {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::new()
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn the_offset_is_signed_and_positive_means_we_run_ahead() {
        let s = ClockSkew::new();
        s.record(1_000, 1_005);
        assert_eq!(s.last_us(), Some(5_000), "+5 ms");
        s.record(2_000, 1_990);
        assert_eq!(s.last_us(), Some(-10_000), "-10 ms: the venue is ahead");
    }

    #[test]
    fn max_abs_keeps_the_worst_drift_seen() {
        let s = ClockSkew::new();
        s.record_us(5_000);
        s.record_us(-20_000);
        s.record_us(3_000);
        assert_eq!(s.snapshot().max_abs_us, Some(20_000), "magnitude, not sign");
    }

    #[test]
    fn exceeds_is_the_trading_tolerance_alarm() {
        let s = ClockSkew::new();
        assert!(!s.exceeds(1_000), "no observation cannot exceed anything");
        s.record_us(5_000);
        assert!(!s.exceeds(10_000), "5 ms is within a 10 ms tolerance");
        assert!(s.exceeds(1_000), "5 ms breaks a 1 ms tolerance");
    }

    #[test]
    fn empty_skew_reports_nothing_rather_than_zero() {
        let s = ClockSkew::new();
        let snap = s.snapshot();
        assert_eq!(snap.count, 0);
        assert_eq!(snap.last_us, None);
        assert_eq!(snap.mean_us, None);
        assert_eq!(snap.max_abs_us, None);
        assert!(snap.to_json().contains("\"last_us\":null"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn mean_spreads_both_directions() {
        let s = ClockSkew::new();
        s.record_us(1_000);
        s.record_us(-1_000);
        assert_eq!(
            s.snapshot().mean_us,
            Some(0.0),
            "a steady clock averages out"
        );
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/exchange/exchange.rs =====
//! The contract every venue must satisfy.
//!
//! This file is the seam between the custom engine (`crate::core`) and the
//! outside world: everything about *communicating with a broker* — buying,
//! selling, marks, the account snapshot — is expressed here. It speaks
//! [`OrderRequest`], [`Fill`] and [`Holding`] from `core`'s vocabulary, and
//! never a wire format. `connectors/<venue>` supplies the transport and the
//! translation.
//!
//! Three narrow traits rather than one wide one, because a venue rarely
//! implements all three honestly:
//!
//! | trait | asks | who needs it |
//! |-------|------|--------------|
//! | [`MarketDataConnector`] | "what is this worth right now?" | risk, marks, pnl |
//! | [`ExecutionConnector`] | "open / close / cancel" | the executor loop |
//! | [`AccountConnector`] | "what does the venue say I hold?" | reconciliation |
//!
//! [`ExchangeConnector`] is the blanket-implemented union, so a component that
//! genuinely needs everything can say so in one bound without listing three.
//!
//! Note what is *not* here: any `impl` for a real exchange. The contract must
//! not know that `binance::errors::Error` exists, which is why every venue
//! error is carried as a [`ConnectorError`] built from a `Display` string.

use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::fmt;

use crate::core::order::{Fill, Order, OrderError, OrderId, OrderRequest};
use crate::core::position::{Holding, PositionBook};

/// What a venue declares about itself.
///
/// Anything the executor would otherwise hard-code per exchange: the settlement
/// asset, the fee schedule, and the tick/step its filters will accept. A market
/// order sent at an off-tick price is rejected by Binance, so rounding belongs
/// to the venue description rather than to a call site.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct VenueSpec {
    pub name: &'static str,
    /// Asset the account is margined and charged in, e.g. `"USDT"`.
    pub settlement: String,
    pub maker_fee: f64,
    pub taker_fee: f64,
    /// Minimum price increment, when the venue publishes one.
    pub price_tick: Option<f64>,
    /// Minimum quantity increment, when the venue publishes one.
    pub qty_step: Option<f64>,
}

impl VenueSpec {
    /// A spec with no filters and zero fees. The defaults are deliberately
    /// *empty* rather than guessed: an invented tick size silently corrupts
    /// every price it touches.
    pub fn new(name: &'static str, settlement: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self {
            name,
            settlement: settlement.into(),
            maker_fee: 0.0,
            taker_fee: 0.0,
            price_tick: None,
            qty_step: None,
        }
    }

    pub fn with_fees(mut self, maker_fee: f64, taker_fee: f64) -> Self {
        self.maker_fee = maker_fee;
        self.taker_fee = taker_fee;
        self
    }

    pub fn with_filters(mut self, price_tick: f64, qty_step: f64) -> Self {
        self.price_tick = Some(price_tick);
        self.qty_step = Some(qty_step);
        self
    }

    /// Fee fraction for a fill, given whether it rested on the book.
    pub fn fee(&self, maker: bool) -> f64 {
        if maker {
            self.maker_fee
        } else {
            self.taker_fee
        }
    }

    /// Snap a price to the venue's tick. Pass through when unspecified.
    pub fn round_price(&self, price: f64) -> f64 {
        match self.price_tick {
            Some(step) if step > 0.0 && price.is_finite() => (price / step).round() * step,
            _ => price,
        }
    }

    /// Snap a quantity *down* to the venue's step, so a rounded-up lot is never
    /// submitted at a size the venue would reject.
    pub fn round_qty(&self, qty: f64) -> f64 {
        match self.qty_step {
            Some(step) if step > 0.0 && qty.is_finite() => (qty / step).floor() * step,
            _ => qty,
        }
    }
}

/// One asset's free/locked split on the venue.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Default)]
pub struct AssetBalance {
    pub free: f64,
    pub locked: f64,
}

impl AssetBalance {
    pub fn total(&self) -> f64 {
        self.free + self.locked
    }
}

/// The venue's own view of the account, at one instant.
///
/// Kept separate from `core::portfolio::Portfolio` rather than merged into it:
/// the venue knows what it actually holds, `Portfolio` knows what the strategy
/// *thinks* it holds, and a difference between the two is exactly the signal
/// worth showing on the dashboard. Reconciliation compares; it does not
/// overwrite one with the other.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct AccountSnapshot {
    pub venue: &'static str,
    /// Free and locked quantities per asset, keyed by the venue's spelling.
    pub balances: BTreeMap<String, AssetBalance>,
    pub can_trade: bool,
    pub maker_fee: f64,
    pub taker_fee: f64,
}

impl AccountSnapshot {
    pub fn new(venue: &'static str) -> Self {
        Self {
            venue,
            balances: BTreeMap::new(),
            can_trade: false,
            maker_fee: 0.0,
            taker_fee: 0.0,
        }
    }

    pub fn free(&self, asset: &str) -> f64 {
        self.balances.get(asset).map_or(0.0, |b| b.free)
    }

    pub fn total(&self, asset: &str) -> f64 {
        self.balances.get(asset).map_or(0.0, AssetBalance::total)
    }

    /// The quote asset of a pair by convention: everything after the base.
    /// `BTCUSDT` -> `USDT`. Without an exchange-info call this is the only split
    /// available, so it is stated rather than hidden.
    pub fn quote_asset(symbol: &str) -> &str {
        const QUOTES: [&str; 5] = ["USDT", "BUSD", "USDC", "BTC", "ETH"];
        for quote in QUOTES {
            if let Some(base) = symbol.strip_suffix(quote) {
                if !base.is_empty() {
                    return quote;
                }
            }
        }
        ""
    }

    /// The base asset of a pair: `BTCUSDT` -> `BTC`.
    pub fn base_asset(symbol: &str) -> &str {
        let quote = Self::quote_asset(symbol);
        &symbol[..symbol.len() - quote.len()]
    }
}

/// Why a connector call failed, in terms `core` can reason about.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub enum ConnectorError {
    /// The request never left, or the answer never arrived.
    Transport(String),
    /// The venue answered with something this adapter could not read.
    Decode(String),
    /// The venue answered, and said no (bad filters, unknown symbol, ...).
    Rejected(String),
    /// Throttled. Distinct from `Rejected`: backing off and retrying is correct
    /// here, and wrong there.
    RateLimited(String),
    /// The adapter does not implement that operation for this venue.
    Unsupported(String),
    /// Caught locally by `OrderRequest::validate`, before any round trip.
    Malformed(OrderError),
}

impl ConnectorError {
    pub fn transport(error: impl fmt::Display) -> Self {
        Self::Transport(error.to_string())
    }

    pub fn decode(error: impl fmt::Display) -> Self {
        Self::Decode(error.to_string())
    }

    pub fn rejected(error: impl fmt::Display) -> Self {
        Self::Rejected(error.to_string())
    }

    /// Safe to retry unchanged after a pause.
    pub fn is_retryable(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::Transport(_) | Self::RateLimited(_))
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for ConnectorError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        match self {
            Self::Transport(m) => write!(f, "transport: {m}"),
            Self::Decode(m) => write!(f, "decode: {m}"),
            Self::Rejected(m) => write!(f, "rejected by venue: {m}"),
            Self::RateLimited(m) => write!(f, "rate limited: {m}"),
            Self::Unsupported(m) => write!(f, "unsupported: {m}"),
            Self::Malformed(e) => write!(f, "malformed request: {e}"),
        }
    }
}

impl std::error::Error for ConnectorError {}

impl From<OrderError> for ConnectorError {
    fn from(error: OrderError) -> Self {
        Self::Malformed(error)
    }
}
/// What every connector must say about its venue.
///
/// A supertrait rather than a method on each of the three, so there is exactly
/// one answer to "which exchange is this?" no matter which trait you hold.
pub trait Venue {
    fn venue(&self) -> VenueSpec;

    /// Short label for logs and the dashboard header.
    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {
        self.venue().name
    }
}

/// Prices. The only thing `core` needs to mark a book to market.
pub trait MarketDataConnector: Venue {
    /// Last traded price for `symbol`.
    fn mark_price(&self, symbol: &str) -> Result<f64, ConnectorError>;

    /// One mark per symbol, as risk and pnl consume them.
    ///
    /// Sequential rather than concurrent: an intraday strategy holds one or two
    /// symbols, and a fan-out would put the venue's rate limit at risk for a
    /// gain that does not exist at that size.
    fn marks(&self, symbols: &[String]) -> Result<BTreeMap<String, f64>, ConnectorError> {
        let mut marks = BTreeMap::new();
        for symbol in symbols {
            marks.insert(symbol.clone(), self.mark_price(symbol)?);
        }
        Ok(marks)
    }
}

/// Orders. Every method is `&mut self` even where a venue's client is
/// `&self`-only, because a connector that must track order state (sequencing,
/// open-order caches) cannot be asked to do so through a shared reference.
pub trait ExecutionConnector: Venue {
    /// Send the order. Returns whatever executed immediately.
    ///
    /// An empty `Vec` is a legitimate answer: a resting limit order has not
    /// filled yet, and pretending otherwise would invent a fill.
    fn submit(&mut self, request: &OrderRequest) -> Result<Vec<Fill>, ConnectorError>;

    fn cancel(&mut self, symbol: &str, order_id: &OrderId) -> Result<(), ConnectorError>;

    /// Orders the venue still considers live. `None` means every symbol.
    fn open_orders(&mut self, symbol: Option<&str>) -> Result<Vec<Order>, ConnectorError>;

    /// Close `holding` at market, on the opposing side.
    ///
    /// Venue-independent because closing is always the opposite side at the
    /// size that exists: that rule lives here once instead of in every adapter.
    /// A flat holding produces no order and no fills.
    fn exit(&mut self, symbol: &str, holding: &Holding) -> Result<Vec<Fill>, ConnectorError> {
        let side = match holding.side() {
            Some(side) => side.opposite(),
            None => return Ok(Vec::new()),
        };
        let request = OrderRequest::market(symbol, side, holding.size.abs()).reducing();
        self.submit(&request)
    }

    /// Kill switch: close every open holding in the book.
    ///
    /// Stops at the first failure and returns it, discarding the fills already
    /// collected. Continuing to send orders after a transport error risks
    /// trading a book this process no longer has an accurate view of.
    fn flatten(&mut self, book: &PositionBook) -> Result<Vec<Fill>, ConnectorError> {
        let open: Vec<(String, Holding)> = book
            .open_holdings()
            .map(|(symbol, holding)| (symbol.clone(), *holding))
            .collect();
        let mut fills = Vec::new();
        for (symbol, holding) in open {
            fills.extend(self.exit(&symbol, &holding)?);
        }
        Ok(fills)
    }
}

/// The venue's own account view, for reconciliation against `Portfolio`.
pub trait AccountConnector: Venue {
    fn snapshot(&self) -> Result<AccountSnapshot, ConnectorError>;
}

/// A connector that can do all three. Blanket-implemented, so an adapter gets
/// it by implementing the pieces it supports.
pub trait ExchangeConnector: MarketDataConnector + ExecutionConnector + AccountConnector {}

impl<T> ExchangeConnector for T where T: MarketDataConnector + ExecutionConnector + AccountConnector {}
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::core::order::{Order, OrderId, Side};

    /// A connector with no network. The traits' *default* methods are the part
    /// `core` owns, so those are what these tests exercise.
    struct Stub {
        spec: VenueSpec,
        prices: BTreeMap<String, f64>,
        sent: Vec<OrderRequest>,
        fail_on: Option<String>,
    }

    impl Stub {
        fn new(prices: &[(&str, f64)]) -> Self {
            Self {
                spec: VenueSpec::new("stub", "USDT").with_fees(0.0002, 0.0004),
                prices: prices.iter().map(|(s, p)| (s.to_string(), *p)).collect(),
                sent: Vec::new(),
                fail_on: None,
            }
        }
    }

    impl Venue for Stub {
        fn venue(&self) -> VenueSpec {
            self.spec.clone()
        }
    }

    impl MarketDataConnector for Stub {
        fn mark_price(&self, symbol: &str) -> Result<f64, ConnectorError> {
            self.prices
                .get(symbol)
                .copied()
                .ok_or_else(|| ConnectorError::rejected(format!("no market for {symbol}")))
        }
    }

    impl ExecutionConnector for Stub {
        fn submit(&mut self, request: &OrderRequest) -> Result<Vec<Fill>, ConnectorError> {
            request.validate()?;
            if self.fail_on.as_deref() == Some(request.symbol.as_str()) {
                return Err(ConnectorError::transport("socket closed"));
            }
            self.sent.push(request.clone());
            let price = self.mark_price(&request.symbol)?;
            Ok(vec![Fill::new(
                OrderId::new("venue-1"),
                request.symbol.clone(),
                request.side,
                request.qty,
                price,
                self.spec.taker_fee * price * request.qty,
            )])
        }

        fn cancel(&mut self, _symbol: &str, _order_id: &OrderId) -> Result<(), ConnectorError> {
            Ok(())
        }

        fn open_orders(&mut self, _symbol: Option<&str>) -> Result<Vec<Order>, ConnectorError> {
            Ok(Vec::new())
        }
    }

    impl AccountConnector for Stub {
        fn snapshot(&self) -> Result<AccountSnapshot, ConnectorError> {
            Err(ConnectorError::Unsupported("stub has no account".into()))
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_unfiltered_spec_never_rewrites_a_price() {
        let spec = VenueSpec::new("binance", "USDT");
        assert_eq!(spec.price_tick, None);
        assert_eq!(spec.round_price(64_123.4567), 64_123.4567);
        assert_eq!(spec.round_qty(0.0001234), 0.0001234);
        assert_eq!(spec.fee(true), 0.0, "unset fees stay unset");
    }

    #[test]
    fn filters_snap_prices_to_a_tick_and_sizes_down_to_a_step() {
        let spec = VenueSpec::new("binance", "USDT").with_filters(0.01, 0.001);
        assert!((spec.round_price(64_123.456) - 64_123.46).abs() < 1e-9);
        assert!(
            (spec.round_qty(0.0015) - 0.001).abs() < 1e-12,
            "never rounds up"
        );
        assert_eq!(
            spec.round_qty(0.0005),
            0.0,
            "below one step is no size at all"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn dusty_floats_snap_to_the_exact_cent_tick() {
        use crate::core::order::{OrderRequest, OrderType, Side, TimeInForce};
        // The needle-fix float dust: must become exactly 108613.2 on-grid.
        let req = OrderRequest::limit("BTCUSDT", Side::Buy, 0.001, 108613.20000000001, 0.01);
        let price = req.limit_price.unwrap();
        assert!((price - 108613.2).abs() < 1e-9, "got {price}");
        assert!(req.validate_on_tick(Some(0.01)).is_ok());
        // Off-tick limit (built raw, bypassing the snapping constructor)
        // fails locally, not as a venue INVALID_TICK_SIZE.
        let bad = OrderRequest {
            symbol: "BTCUSDT".to_string(),
            side: Side::Buy,
            qty: 0.001,
            order_type: OrderType::Limit,
            limit_price: Some(108613.2051),
            time_in_force: TimeInForce::Gtc,
            reduce_only: false,
        };
        assert!(bad.validate_on_tick(Some(0.01)).is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_pair_is_split_by_its_quote_convention() {
        assert_eq!(AccountSnapshot::base_asset("BTCUSDT"), "BTC");
        assert_eq!(AccountSnapshot::quote_asset("BTCUSDT"), "USDT");
        assert_eq!(AccountSnapshot::base_asset("ETHBTC"), "ETH");
        assert_eq!(AccountSnapshot::quote_asset("ETHBTC"), "BTC");
        // No known quote: the whole symbol is the base, and the quote is empty.
        assert_eq!(AccountSnapshot::quote_asset("WEIRD"), "");
        assert_eq!(AccountSnapshot::base_asset("WEIRD"), "WEIRD");
    }

    #[test]
    fn default_marks_queries_every_symbol() {
        let stub = Stub::new(&[("BTCUSDT", 100.0), ("ETHUSDT", 10.0)]);
        let marks = stub
            .marks(&["BTCUSDT".to_string(), "ETHUSDT".to_string()])
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(marks.len(), 2);
        assert_eq!(marks["ETHUSDT"], 10.0);
        assert_eq!(stub.name(), "stub");
        assert!(stub.marks(&["DOGEUSDT".to_string()]).is_err());
    }

    fn holding(size: f64, avg_entry: f64) -> Holding {
        Holding {
            size,
            avg_entry,
            ..Default::default()
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn exit_trades_the_opposite_side_at_the_size_held() {
        let mut stub = Stub::new(&[("BTCUSDT", 100.0)]);
        let fills = stub.exit("BTCUSDT", &holding(2.0, 90.0)).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(stub.sent.len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(stub.sent[0].side, Side::Sell);
        assert_eq!(stub.sent[0].qty, 2.0);
        assert!(stub.sent[0].reduce_only, "an exit must never flip the book");
        assert_eq!(fills.len(), 1);

        // A short closes by buying.
        stub.exit("BTCUSDT", &holding(-3.0, 110.0)).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(stub.sent[1].side, Side::Buy);
        assert_eq!(stub.sent[1].qty, 3.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_flat_holding_is_not_an_order() {
        let mut stub = Stub::new(&[("BTCUSDT", 100.0)]);
        let fills = stub.exit("BTCUSDT", &holding(0.0, 0.0)).unwrap();
        assert!(fills.is_empty());
        assert!(stub.sent.is_empty(), "no round trip for nothing to close");
    }

    #[test]
    fn flatten_closes_every_open_holding_and_stops_at_the_first_failure() {
        let mut book = PositionBook::new();
        book.apply(&Fill::new(
            OrderId::new("a"),
            "BTCUSDT",
            Side::Buy,
            1.0,
            100.0,
            0.0,
        ));
        book.apply(&Fill::new(
            OrderId::new("b"),
            "ETHUSDT",
            Side::Sell,
            2.0,
            10.0,
            0.0,
        ));

        let mut stub = Stub::new(&[("BTCUSDT", 100.0), ("ETHUSDT", 10.0)]);
        let fills = stub.flatten(&book).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(fills.len(), 2);
        assert_eq!(stub.sent.len(), 2, "BTCUSDT then ETHUSDT, both closed");

        let mut failing = Stub::new(&[("BTCUSDT", 100.0), ("ETHUSDT", 10.0)]);
        failing.fail_on = Some("ETHUSDT".to_string());
        let error = failing.flatten(&book).unwrap_err();
        assert!(error.is_retryable());
        assert_eq!(failing.sent.len(), 1, "stopped instead of trading blind");
    }

    #[test]
    fn only_transport_and_throttling_are_retryable() {
        assert!(ConnectorError::transport("timeout").is_retryable());
        assert!(ConnectorError::RateLimited("429".into()).is_retryable());
        assert!(!ConnectorError::rejected("MIN_NOTIONAL").is_retryable());
        assert!(!ConnectorError::Malformed(OrderError::EmptySymbol).is_retryable());
        assert_eq!(
            ConnectorError::from(OrderError::EmptySymbol).to_string(),
            "malformed request: order symbol must not be empty"
        );
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/exchange/mod.rs =====
//! `exchange` — everything that communicates with a broker.
//!
//! Buying, selling, cancelling, marking to market, and reading the account
//! (balances, margin, fees) all live behind the three connector traits
//! re-exported here from [`exchange`]:
//!
//! ```text
//! exchange/
//!   mod.rs        # this file: wiring + the buy/sell/margin diagnostic tests
//!   exchange.rs   # VenueSpec, AccountSnapshot, ConnectorError, and the
//!                 # MarketDataConnector / ExecutionConnector / AccountConnector
//!                 # contract every venue adapter implements
//! ```
//!
//! This is the *only* seam between this process and the outside world.
//! `crate::core` is the custom-made engine (gui, telemetry, features,
//! strategy, pnl); it consumes the results of a connector call — a `Fill`,
//! an `AccountSnapshot` — and never performs one. `crate::connectors`
//! supplies the transport per venue. Nothing here knows a venue's wire format.

pub mod exchange;

pub use exchange::*;

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use std::collections::BTreeMap;

    use super::*;
    use crate::core::order::{Fill, Order, OrderId, OrderRequest, Side};
    use crate::core::portfolio::Portfolio;

    /// A fake broker with real settlement and no network.
    ///
    /// It exists to prove the boundary itself: buying, selling, and reading
    /// margin all happen through the traits above — never by reaching into
    /// `core` from a venue, and never by reaching into a venue from `core`.
    struct FakeBroker {
        spec: VenueSpec,
        marks: BTreeMap<String, f64>,
        /// Quote-side (USDT) wallet.
        quote_free: f64,
        /// Base-side (BTC) wallet.
        base_free: f64,
        next_order: u64,
    }

    impl FakeBroker {
        fn new(starting_cash: f64, price: f64) -> Self {
            Self {
                spec: VenueSpec::new("fake", "USDT").with_fees(0.0002, 0.0004),
                marks: BTreeMap::from([("BTCUSDT".to_string(), price)]),
                quote_free: starting_cash,
                base_free: 0.0,
                next_order: 0,
            }
        }
    }

    impl Venue for FakeBroker {
        fn venue(&self) -> VenueSpec {
            self.spec.clone()
        }
    }

    impl MarketDataConnector for FakeBroker {
        fn mark_price(&self, symbol: &str) -> Result<f64, ConnectorError> {
            self.marks
                .get(symbol)
                .copied()
                .ok_or_else(|| ConnectorError::rejected(format!("no market for {symbol}")))
        }
    }

    impl ExecutionConnector for FakeBroker {
        fn submit(&mut self, request: &OrderRequest) -> Result<Vec<Fill>, ConnectorError> {
            request.validate()?;
            let price = self.mark_price(&request.symbol)?;
            let notional = price * request.qty;
            let fee = self.spec.taker_fee * notional;
            match request.side {
                Side::Buy => {
                    self.quote_free -= notional + fee;
                    self.base_free += request.qty;
                }
                Side::Sell => {
                    self.quote_free += notional - fee;
                    self.base_free -= request.qty;
                }
            }
            self.next_order += 1;
            Ok(vec![Fill::new(
                OrderId::new(format!("fake-{}", self.next_order)),
                request.symbol.clone(),
                request.side,
                request.qty,
                price,
                fee,
            )])
        }

        fn cancel(&mut self, _symbol: &str, _order_id: &OrderId) -> Result<(), ConnectorError> {
            Ok(())
        }

        fn open_orders(&mut self, _symbol: Option<&str>) -> Result<Vec<Order>, ConnectorError> {
            Ok(Vec::new())
        }
    }

    impl AccountConnector for FakeBroker {
        fn snapshot(&self) -> Result<AccountSnapshot, ConnectorError> {
            let mut snapshot = AccountSnapshot::new("fake");
            snapshot.can_trade = true;
            snapshot.maker_fee = self.spec.maker_fee;
            snapshot.taker_fee = self.spec.taker_fee;
            snapshot.balances.insert(
                "USDT".to_string(),
                AssetBalance {
                    free: self.quote_free,
                    locked: 0.0,
                },
            );
            snapshot.balances.insert(
                "BTC".to_string(),
                AssetBalance {
                    free: self.base_free,
                    locked: 0.0,
                },
            );
            Ok(snapshot)
        }
    }

    /// The concrete type satisfies the full [`ExchangeConnector`] bound.
    fn is_a_complete_exchange() {
        fn assert<T: ExchangeConnector>() {}
        assert::<FakeBroker>();
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_fake_broker_is_a_complete_exchange() {
        is_a_complete_exchange();
    }

    #[test]
    fn buying_and_selling_go_through_the_exchange_contract() {
        let mut broker = FakeBroker::new(10_000.0, 100.0);
        let mut portfolio = Portfolio::new(10_000.0, 3.0).unwrap();

        // Buy: the connector fills, `core` folds the fill into its own book.
        let buy = OrderRequest::market("BTCUSDT", Side::Buy, 0.5);
        let fills = broker.submit(&buy).expect("a buy must fill at market");
        assert_eq!(fills.len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(fills[0].side, Side::Buy);
        portfolio.apply_fill(&fills[0]);

        let marks = broker.marks(&["BTCUSDT".to_string()]).unwrap();
        let size = portfolio
            .holdings()
            .open_holdings()
            .find(|(symbol, _)| symbol.as_str() == "BTCUSDT")
            .map(|(_, holding)| holding.size)
            .expect("the buy opened a long");
        assert_eq!(size, 0.5);

        // Sell: same path, opposite side, back to flat.
        let sell = OrderRequest::market("BTCUSDT", Side::Sell, 0.5);
        let fills = broker.submit(&sell).expect("a sell must fill at market");
        assert_eq!(fills[0].side, Side::Sell);
        portfolio.apply_fill(&fills[0]);
        assert!(
            portfolio.holdings().open_holdings().next().is_none(),
            "the round trip closed the book"
        );

        // The broker's wallet moved with the fills: notional + fee out on the
        // buy, back in (minus the sell fee) on the close, netting to one fee.
        let one_fee = 0.0004 * 100.0 * 0.5;
        let expected = 10_000.0 - 2.0 * one_fee;
        assert!((broker.quote_free - expected).abs() < 1e-9);
        assert_eq!(broker.base_free, 0.0, "flat after the round trip");

        // Core-side equity still reconciles against the marks it was given.
        assert!(portfolio.equity(&marks).is_finite());
    }

    #[test]
    fn broker_margin_is_read_from_the_account_connector() {
        let broker = FakeBroker::new(10_000.0, 100.0);
        let snapshot = broker.snapshot().expect("the fake broker has an account");
        assert!(snapshot.can_trade, "the venue says trading is allowed");
        assert_eq!(snapshot.free("USDT"), 10_000.0);
        assert_eq!(snapshot.total("USDT"), 10_000.0, "nothing is locked");
        assert_eq!(snapshot.venue, "fake");

        // `core` computes its own margin view from the same numbers without
        // ever calling the broker itself.
        let mut portfolio = Portfolio::new(10_000.0, 3.0).unwrap();
        let marks = BTreeMap::from([("BTCUSDT".to_string(), 100.0)]);
        assert_eq!(portfolio.margin_used(&marks), 0.0, "a flat book uses none");
        assert_eq!(portfolio.available_margin(&marks), 10_000.0);

        // Open 1 BTC long: margin = gross / leverage, available = equity - margin.
        let fill = Fill::new(OrderId::new("a"), "BTCUSDT", Side::Buy, 1.0, 100.0, 0.0);
        portfolio.apply_fill(&fill);
        assert!((portfolio.margin_used(&marks) - 100.0 / 3.0).abs() < 1e-9);
        assert_eq!(
            portfolio.available_margin(&marks),
            portfolio.equity(&marks) - portfolio.margin_used(&marks)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_unknown_market_is_a_rejection_not_a_panic() {
        let broker = FakeBroker::new(10_000.0, 100.0);
        let error = broker.mark_price("DOGEUSDT").unwrap_err();
        assert!(!error.is_retryable(), "no market is a permanent answer");
    }
}


===== FILE: uets/executor/executor.rs =====
//! Dispatcher for the Binance websocket readers in `connectors/binance/websocket/reader.rs`.
//!
//! Run any reader directly through this binary:
//!
//!     cargo run --bin dev_test -- market btcusdt aggTrade
//!     cargo run --bin dev_test -- help
//!
//! The Python extension module (lib.rs) is a separate build target -- build
//! it with `maturin develop` (maturin enables the extension-module feature
//! automatically).

use std::path::PathBuf;
use std::process::ExitCode;

use uets::connectors::binance::websocket::{diagnostic, live, monitor, reader};
use uets::live_strategy::{self, ComputeFeatures, LiveStrategy, StateBuilder};

/// Default capture directory, `market_data/live_db` at the repo root.
///
/// Resolved from the manifest directory rather than the process CWD, because
/// this binary is normally run through `cargo run` from anywhere and a capture
/// landing in a random CWD is worse than one landing in a predictable place.
fn default_live_db_dir() -> String {
    // CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR is <repo>/uets, so exactly one parent reaches the
    // repo root. Two would land beside the repo.
    PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"))
        .parent()
        .map(|root| root.join("market_data").join("live_db"))
        .map(|p| p.display().to_string())
        .unwrap_or_else(|| "market_data/live_db".to_string())
}

/// Default diagnostic directory: `out/live` under the package (beside the
/// monitor's frames), resolved from the manifest rather than the CWD.
fn default_out_dir() -> String {
    PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"))
        .join("out")
        .join("live")
        .display()
        .to_string()
}

/// The executor's live strategy selection — test 2's subject.
///
/// `executor.rs` is the only place that decides *which* bundle runs. A
/// selection swaps the whole [`LiveStrategy`] — state builder
/// (`state_builder.rs`) and feature builder (`compute_feature.rs`) travel
/// together — and a failed switch leaves the previous selection untouched,
/// so a typo in a config can never half-configure the process.
struct StrategySwitch {
    active: Option<String>,
    bundle: Option<LiveStrategy>,
}

impl StrategySwitch {
    fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            active: None,
            bundle: None,
        }
    }

    /// Strategies compiled into this binary.
    fn available(&self) -> Vec<&'static str> {
        live_strategy::available_strategies()
    }

    /// Strategy folders on disk (`uets/live_strategy/*`).
    fn discovered(&self) -> Vec<String> {
        live_strategy::discover_strategy_dirs()
    }

    fn active(&self) -> Option<&str> {
        self.active.as_deref()
    }

    /// Switch the selection to `name`. Unknown names change nothing.
    fn select(&mut self, name: &str) -> Result<(), String> {
        let bundle = live_strategy::load_strategy(name)?;
        self.active = Some(name.to_string());
        self.bundle = Some(bundle);
        Ok(())
    }

    /// The selected bundle's state builder (`state_builder.rs`).
    fn state_builder(&self) -> Result<&dyn StateBuilder, String> {
        self.bundle
            .as_ref()
            .map(|bundle| bundle.builder.as_ref())
            .ok_or_else(|| "no strategy selected".to_string())
    }

    /// The selected bundle's feature builder (`compute_feature.rs`).
    fn feature_builder_mut(&mut self) -> Result<&mut (dyn ComputeFeatures + 'static), String> {
        self.bundle
            .as_mut()
            .map(|bundle| bundle.computer.as_mut())
            .ok_or_else(|| "no strategy selected".to_string())
    }
}

impl Default for StrategySwitch {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::new()
    }
}

fn print_help() {
    println!(
        "usage: dev_test [command] [args...]\n\
         \n\
         commands:\n\
         \x20 market [SYMBOL] [TYPE]  public market stream (default: btcusdt aggTrade)\n\
         \x20 depth [SYMBOL]          diff-depth stream @100ms (default: btcusdt)\n\
         \x20 kline [SYMBOL]          1-minute kline stream (default: btcusdt)\n\
         \x20 ticker [SYMBOL]         24h rolling ticker (default: btcusdt)\n\
         \x20 all-tickers             all-symbols mini-ticker stream (!miniTicker@arr)\n\
         \x20 multi [SYMBOLS...]      multiple diff-depth streams on one connection\n\
         \x20 record SYM1 [SYM2 ...] [DIR] live capture -> one merged CSV per symbol under DIR\n\
         \x20 feed SYMBOL INSTANCE...  capture live data and fan it out over Unix sockets\n\
         \x20 run INSTANCE...         start the VIM monitor and isolated strategy/feed processes\n\
         \x20                         prefix an instance with disabled: to omit its worker\n\
         \x20                         (default DIR: market_data/live_db)\n\
         \x20                         flags: --dir DIR  explicit output dir (removes ambiguity)\n\
         \x20                                --rows N   stop after N rows *per symbol*\n\
         \x20                                --append  keep the existing file\n\
         \x20 monitor [SYMBOL]        live tape + 20-level orderbook monitor;\n\
         \x20                         view with:  python uets/display.py [SYMBOL]\n\
         \x20 diag [SYMBOL] [DIR]      diagnostic CSV: one row per event with\n\
         \x20                         update_type=aggTrades|orderbook and the\n\
         \x20                         other stream's columns as NaN\n\
         \x20                         (default DIR: uets/out/live)\n\
         \x20 strategies               list compiled vs on-disk strategy bundles\n\
         \x20 strategy <strategy>@<asset> run one strategy socket consumer\n\
         \x20 user-stream             REST listen-key lifecycle (needs API key)\n\
         \x20 user-stream-ws          account/order update stream (needs API key)\n\
         \x20 help                    show this message\n\
         \n\
         examples:\n\
         \x20 dev_test market btcusdt aggTrade\n\
         \x20 dev_test depth btcusdt\n\
         \x20 dev_test multi ethbtc bnbusdt\n\
         \x20 dev_test strategy ofmr@btcusdt\n\
         \x20 dev_test feed btcusdt ofmr@btcusdt\n\
         \x20 dev_test run ofmr@btcusdt ofmr@ethusdt\n\
         \x20 dev_test record btcusdt            # -> market_data/live_db\n\
         \n\
         All streams run until interrupted (Ctrl+C)."
    );
}

fn parse_strategy_instance(value: &str) -> Result<(String, String), String> {
    let Some((strategy, asset)) = value.split_once('@') else {
        return Err(format!(
            "invalid strategy instance '{value}'; expected strategy@asset"
        ));
    };
    if strategy.is_empty()
        || asset.is_empty()
        || asset.contains('@')
        || !strategy
            .chars()
            .all(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '_')
        || !asset
            .chars()
            .all(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '_' || c == '-')
    {
        return Err(format!(
            "invalid strategy instance '{value}'; expected strategy@asset"
        ));
    }
    Ok((strategy.to_ascii_lowercase(), asset.to_ascii_lowercase()))
}

#[cfg(unix)]
struct LiveProcesses(Vec<std::process::Child>);

#[cfg(unix)]
impl Drop for LiveProcesses {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        for child in &mut self.0 {
            let _ = child.kill();
        }
        for child in &mut self.0 {
            let _ = child.wait();
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(unix)]
fn run_live_system(requested: &[String]) -> Result<(), String> {
    use std::time::{Duration, Instant};
    use uets_gui::{
        InstanceSnapshot, Monitor, SnapshotSource, SocketSnapshotSource, Statistics,
        publish_instance,
    };

    if requested.is_empty() {
        return Err("usage: dev_test run <strategy>@<asset> [strategy@asset ...]".into());
    }

    let root = PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"))
        .parent()
        .ok_or_else(|| "cannot resolve repository root".to_string())?
        .to_path_buf();
    std::env::set_current_dir(&root).map_err(|error| error.to_string())?;

    let gui_socket = root.join("sockets").join("gui.sock");
    let mut snapshots = SocketSnapshotSource::bind(&gui_socket)
        .map_err(|error| format!("bind UETS GUI socket: {error}"))?;
    let executable = std::env::current_exe().map_err(|error| error.to_string())?;
    let log_dir = root.join("logs").join("uets");
    std::fs::create_dir_all(&log_dir).map_err(|error| error.to_string())?;
    let mut enabled = Vec::new();
    let mut disabled = Vec::new();
    let mut assets = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();

    for value in requested {
        let is_disabled = value.starts_with("disabled:");
        let identity = value.strip_prefix("disabled:").unwrap_or(value);
        let (strategy, asset) = parse_strategy_instance(identity)?;
        if strategy != "ofmr" {
            return Err(format!("'{strategy}' has no live UDS consumer yet"));
        }
        if !assets.insert(asset.clone()) {
            return Err(format!(
                "multiple strategies for asset '{asset}' are not supported by this feed adapter"
            ));
        }
        let identity = format!("{strategy}@{asset}");
        if is_disabled {
            disabled.push(identity);
        } else {
            enabled.push((identity, asset));
        }
    }

    for identity in disabled {
        let asset = identity.split_once('@').unwrap().1;
        publish_instance(
            &gui_socket,
            &InstanceSnapshot {
                strategy_id: "ofmr".to_string(),
                asset_id: asset.to_string(),
                statistics: Statistics {
                    status: Some("disabled".to_string()),
                    ..Statistics::default()
                },
                equity_history: Vec::new(),
            },
        )
        .map_err(|error| format!("publish disabled status for {identity}: {error}"))?;
    }

    let mut processes = LiveProcesses(Vec::new());
    for (identity, _) in &enabled {
        let log = std::fs::OpenOptions::new()
            .create(true)
            .append(true)
            .open(log_dir.join(format!("strategy_{}.log", identity.replace('@', "_"))))
            .map_err(|error| error.to_string())?;
        let mut command = std::process::Command::new(&executable);
        command
            .current_dir(&root)
            .env("UETS_GUI_SOCKET", &gui_socket)
            .arg("strategy")
            .arg(identity)
            .stdout(std::process::Stdio::from(
                log.try_clone().map_err(|error| error.to_string())?,
            ))
            .stderr(std::process::Stdio::from(log));
        let child = command
            .spawn()
            .map_err(|error| format!("start {identity}: {error}"))?;
        processes.0.push(child);
    }

    let startup_deadline = Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(10);
    loop {
        let current = snapshots.snapshot();
        let ready = enabled.iter().all(|(identity, _)| {
            current.instances.iter().any(|instance| {
                instance.identity() == *identity
                    && instance.statistics.status.as_deref() == Some("enabled")
            })
        });
        if ready {
            break;
        }
        for child in &mut processes.0 {
            if let Some(status) = child.try_wait().map_err(|error| error.to_string())? {
                return Err(format!("strategy worker exited during startup: {status}"));
            }
        }
        if Instant::now() >= startup_deadline {
            return Err("timed out waiting for strategy worker UDS telemetry".into());
        }
        std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(50));
    }

    for (identity, asset) in &enabled {
        let log = std::fs::OpenOptions::new()
            .create(true)
            .append(true)
            .open(log_dir.join(format!("feed_{asset}.log")))
            .map_err(|error| error.to_string())?;
        let mut command = std::process::Command::new(&executable);
        command
            .current_dir(&root)
            .env("UETS_GUI_SOCKET", &gui_socket)
            .arg("feed")
            .arg(asset)
            .arg(identity)
            .stdout(std::process::Stdio::from(
                log.try_clone().map_err(|error| error.to_string())?,
            ))
            .stderr(std::process::Stdio::from(log));
        let child = command
            .spawn()
            .map_err(|error| format!("start feed for {identity}: {error}"))?;
        processes.0.push(child);
    }

    eprintln!("UETS live monitor started; press q to stop all workers");
    Monitor::new(snapshots)
        .run()
        .map_err(|error| format!("UETS monitor failed: {error}"))
}

#[cfg(not(unix))]
fn run_live_system(_requested: &[String]) -> Result<(), String> {
    Err("the live UETS runner requires Unix-domain sockets; run it on Linux or in Docker".into())
}

/// Live capture orchestration: websockets -> merge -> the 128-column CSV.
///
/// This mirrors `uets::connectors::binance::websocket::live::run_capture` but fixes two defects
/// that could not be fixed there (`live.rs` is frozen; only this file may
/// change -- see `docs/system_design/uets.md` for the network history):
///
/// * **No aggTrades in the output.** On some networks `fstream.binance.com`
///   serves the depth stream but delivers *zero* `@aggTrade` frames (the
///   exact symptom documented under "Binance futures data degraded on some
///   networks"). This capture keeps the futures trade feed but also
///   subscribes to the proven spot feed (`stream.binance.com:9443`); when
///   the futures feed stays silent for [`TRADE_FALLBACK`] the spot feed is
///   added, and single-venue ownership guarantees at most one trade venue
///   contributes rows at a time.
/// * **UNSEEDED/STALE book.** The old flow seeded *before* connecting and
///   gave up after one attempt. Here the sockets connect first, incoming
///   events are staged until a REST snapshot lands (so the snapshot covers
///   every buffered diff -- no phantom startup gap), and a background thread
///   retries the seed forever, doubling as the gap re-seed.
mod record {
    use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, AtomicU64, Ordering};
    use std::sync::{Arc, Mutex};
    use std::time::{Duration, Instant, SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};

    use event_merger_live::{BookSnapshot, CsvRecorder, MarketEvent, Merger, ReorderBuffer};
    use tungstenite::{Message, connect};

    use super::live;

    /// No frames at all for this long (sockets connected but silent) -> give up.
    const STALL_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(30);
    /// No *seed* for this long -> give up (REST unreachable despite retries).
    const SEED_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(60);
    /// Poll interval of the seed / re-seed thread while a seed is needed.
    const RESEED_POLL: Duration = Duration::from_millis(500);
    /// Backoff after a failed seed attempt (bounds DNS-failure log spam).
    const RESEED_BACKOFF: Duration = Duration::from_secs(3);
    /// Heartbeat cadence.
    const HEARTBEAT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(1);
    /// Futures trade feed silent this long -> also subscribe the spot feed.
    const TRADE_FALLBACK: Duration = Duration::from_secs(10);
    /// Current trade venue silent this long -> ownership may move to the other feed.
    const VENUE_TAKEOVER: Duration = Duration::from_secs(30);
    /// Cap on events buffered before the first successful seed.
    const STAGE_CAP: usize = 50_000;
    /// Reorder buffer capacity (matches `live::run_capture`).
    const REORDER_CAP: usize = 65_536;

    pub(crate) const VENUE_NONE: u8 = 0;
    pub(crate) const VENUE_FUTURES: u8 = 1;
    pub(crate) const VENUE_SPOT: u8 = 2;

    fn now_ms() -> u64 {
        SystemTime::now()
            .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
            .map(|d| d.as_millis() as u64)
            .unwrap_or(0)
    }

    fn venue_name(venue: u8) -> &'static str {
        match venue {
            VENUE_FUTURES => "futures",
            VENUE_SPOT => "spot",
            _ => "none",
        }
    }

    /// Shared, mutex-guarded capture state.
    ///
    /// Both websocket threads push into this; emission order is decided in
    /// exactly one place: the [`ReorderBuffer`].
    pub(crate) struct Capture {
        pub(crate) merger: Merger,
        pub(crate) reorder: ReorderBuffer,
        csv: CsvRecorder,
        /// A gap was detected; the seed thread re-seeds while this is set.
        pub(crate) reseeds_pending: bool,
        /// False until the first REST snapshot has been applied.
        pub(crate) seeded: bool,
        /// Events received before the first seed, held until it lands.
        staged: Vec<MarketEvent>,
        /// Events dropped because the pre-seed buffer was full.
        staged_dropped: u64,
